      "raw_content": "[![Urban Guide Quebec](https://urbanguidequebec.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/11/cropped-urban-guide-quebec-logo-2024.png)](https://urbanguidequebec.com/)\n\n[![Urban Guide Quebec](https://urbanguidequebec.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/11/cropped-urban-guide-quebec-logo-2024.png)](https://urbanguidequebec.com/)\n\nSearch\n\n![](https://i0.wp.com/urbanguidequebec.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/10/deTerroir-cafe-in-Quebec-City.jpg?resize=1200%2C628&ssl=1)\n\n* Posted *in*\n* + [Cafés](https://urbanguidequebec.com/food/cafes/)\n\n# Cafes in Quebec City: Where to Sip, Relax and Enjoy the Local Coffee Culture\n\n* Posted by*by* [Pamela MacNaughtan](https://urbanguidequebec.com/author/pamela/)\n* 8 min read\n\nPost Views: 78,060\n\nLast Updated on January 27, 2025 by Pamela MacNaughtan\n\n**Note**: *This post was updated 26 Jan 2025. Closed cafes were deleted, and new ones were added – it’s a continuous pursuit to keep this post fresh.*\n\nThe diverse array of cafes in Quebec City begs one to take on a circuitous journey through the city; sipping coffee, and nibbling on pastries before sauntering to the next cafe – and the next neighbourhood.\n\nThere are cafes inside small museums and attractions; some are hidden in plain sight and others have bold signage. There are tiny ones with a couple of outdoor chairs and a round table, and ones with floor-to-ceiling windows that bathe the entire cafe in natural light.\n\nWhen I first moved to Old Quebec (Uppertown) in 2014, I would walk down to Paillard boulangerie or Chez Temporal for chocolat chaud (hot chocolate) and croissants. Paillard’s spacious dining area would be full by 10am, so I’d go when it opened at 7am and stay for a couple of hours.\n\nOn rue Couillard, Chez Temporal was quiet, a bit hidden from the heavy foot traffic on rue Saint-Jean. I’d sit at the chunky wood bar, surrounded by quirky neighbourhood artists, and sip on drinks topped with whipped cream while reading a book.\n\nThe selection of cafes in Quebec City has grown significantly in the last 11 years, and today, stopping at cafes while wandering through the city’s neighbourhoods is one of my favourite pastimes.\n\n[[**Click here to see the cafés listed by neighbourhood**](#neighbourhood)]\n\n## Café Apotek\n\n![Cafe Apotek ](https://i0.wp.com/urbanguidequebec.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/10/Cafe-Apotek-cafes-in-Quebec-City.jpg?resize=940%2C788&ssl=1)\n\nCafé Apotek in Vieux-Port (Old Port)\n\nOne of the newest cafes in Quebec City, Café Apotek opened its doors in November 2024 in the Vieux-Port neighbourhood. Its interior features elements of Danish contemporary design. There is a long curved brown and white marble table and old small rustic school stools, and the back wall has floating metal square tabletops paired with squat round metal stools. The lines are clean, some sharp, others flowing.   \n  \nThe building where Café Apotek is located has a completely different vibe. Erected in 1847, it is an ochre-coloured square clay brick building with a rounded truncated corner – with a corner turret on the top floor. On the side facing rue Saint-Paul is the word “Antiques” in brown. It’s worn, especially as new buildings have been erected around it, which is part of its charm. Honestly, I’m thrilled this building remains intact, and now, home to a sleek new cafe.  \n  \nOn my visit, I ordered a chocolat chaud (I am on a constant quest to find the best ones in the city), which is a milky dark chocolate concoction served in a beautifully crafted heavy ceramic cup. Unable to resist the small display of pastries and desserts, I ordered a choux with a creamy coffee-flavoured centre, Basque cheesecake (this style is crustless with a brunt top), and grapefruit pavlova (which was the perfect combination of tart, sweet, and creamy). Everything is made in-house (the chef trained with the talented team at La Cabra in Denmark), and next time I look forward to buying some bread to take home.  \n  \n**Café Apotek**: 209, rue Saint-Paul, Vieux-Port • Open Tues-Fri 8am to 4pm, Sat 8am to 5pm, Sun 9am to 5pm • [Instagram](https://www.instagram.com/apotek.bakery) • **Note**: this cafe is for enjoying a coffee and nibbles with freinds, not working on a laptop.\n\n## deTerroir Café\n\n![deTerroir - a cafe in Saint-Jean-Baptiste. Photo on the left is a latté, the photo on the right is people sitting at a communal table, working.](https://i0.wp.com/urbanguidequebec.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/10/Cafe-de-Terroir-cafes-in-Quebec-City.jpg?resize=940%2C788&ssl=1)\n\ndeTerroir Café in Saint-Jean-Baptiste\n\nLocated in the Saint-Jean-Baptiste neighbourhood, across the street from Bibliothèque Claire-Martin, is deTerroir. It’s a small café and micro-roaster, with communal bar-height tables, and some of the friendliest staff in Quebec City. The walls are adorned with colourful prints by local artists, and choosing between savoury scones, homemade whippets, and Belgian waffles is always a challenge. Among coffee aficionados, this is one of the best cafes in Quebec City.  \n  \ndeTerroir café is the passion project of Quebec culinary journalist, Allison Van Rassel, one of the city’s biggest foodies (seriously, she’s everywhere; TV, radio, newspapers, online publications). Van Rassel opened the café in 2022, partnering with Pier-Paul, a gifted coffee roaster. Together, they create incredible coffee blends that will hook you after one sip.   \n  \nI love coming here whenever I go to the library, ordering a latté or chocolate chaud (dark chocolate), and sometimes a treat. The onion scones are quite good, and the Belgian waffles too. The whippets are delicious, but almost too sweet for me (anyone with a sweet tooth will love them though). I always see locals here, working or reading while enjoying a coffee, and I love the current display of pop art by artist Chloé Lalancette (I may have ordered a couple of prints).  \n  \n**deTerroir Café**: 752, rue Saint-Jean; Saint-Jean-Baptiste • Open Mon-Fri 6:30am to 4pm, Sat-Sun 8am to 5pm • [Instagram](https://www.instagram.com/deterroir_cafe/)\n\n## Cantook Café Limoilou\n\nIt’s a trifecta of goodness in the heart of Limoilou: Cantook Café, Franky Johnny sandwicherie, and Borderon et fils boulangerie – all in one contemporary designed space (the trending style in Quebec City). They serve house-made coffee blends here, and the prices are quite reasonable compared to other cafés in the neighbourhood (and city). There are croissants (butter and almond)and chocolatines; bostock with orange and chocolate, cookies, muffins, and more.  \n  \nThe café is relatively quiet in the morning – ideal for getting a little work done or reading a book. Around 11h30 things pick up as locals arrive to order sandwiches from Franky Johnny (they are quite large, and filling!). As a lover of Borderon’s baguettes, I always grab a couple for future snacking at home. Miam!  \n  \n**Cantook Café** **Limoilou**: 1101, ave 3e; Limoilou • Open Tues-Fri 7am to 6pm, Sat-Sun 8am to 5pm • [cantookcafe.com](https://cantookcafe.com/)\n\n## La Maison Smith / Smith Café\n\n![La Maison Smith in Place-Royale ](https://i0.wp.com/urbanguidequebec.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/10/Cafes-in-Quebec-City-La-Maison-Smith.jpg?resize=940%2C788&ssl=1)\n\nLa Maison Smith – the original location, located in Place-Royale\n\nI visited La Maison Smith on my first trip to Quebec City in 2013, and I have returned many times over the years – it helps that it’s a one-minute walk from where I currently live. There have been a couple of decor updates, otherwise, it has remained the same – excellent coffee, chocolat chaud, and croissants and pastries from Paillard.  \n  \nLocated in Maison Smith in Place-Royale, across from the historic Notre-Dame-des-victoires, La Maison Smith has a large outdoor patio in summer and autumn – perfect for people-watching while sipping coffee and slathering strawberry jam on a flaky croissant. On a hot summer day, they have ice cream, which is a welcome treat. The sandwiches and salads, while good, are overpriced (IMO), so I suggest having lunch in one of the nearby restaurants instead.   \n  \nWhile the cafe in Place-Royale is my go-to, there are now several cafe locations throughout the city, all of which are under the name Smith Café. In Old Quebec, there is a Smith Café on rue des Jardins near [Hôtel Clarendon](https://www.booking.com/hotel/ca/clarendon.en.html?aid=876617&no_rooms=1&group_adults=2), and another on rue Saint-Jean near [Hôtel Manoir Victoria](https://www.booking.com/hotel/ca/manoir-victoria-quebec-city.en.html?aid=876617&no_rooms=1&group_adults=2). The Smith Café in Limoilou (*201, 3e Ave*) is perfect for getting some work done while sipping coffee-inspired cocktails; and the location in Charlesburg (7685, 1re Ave) has delicious pizza.  \n  \n**La Maison Smith**: 23, rue Notre-Dame, Place-Royale • Open daily 7am-6pm – until 7pm Fri & Sat • [Instagram](https://www.instagram.com/smithcafeco) • **Note**: Smith roasts coffee in several of their locations, which can be purchased to enjoy at home.\n\n## Café Saint-Henri\n\n![Café Saint-Henri in the Saint-Roch neighbourhood. ](https://i0.wp.com/urbanguidequebec.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/10/Saint-Henri-Cafe-in-Saint-Roch.jpg?resize=940%2C788&ssl=1)\n\nCafé Saint-Henri in Saint-Roch\n\nIt was an exciting day when Saint-Henri in Montreal opened a cafe in Quebec City’s Saint-Roch neighbourhood. Located on the end of rue Saint-Joseph Est, near the Metro grocery store, [Café Saint-Henri](https://urbanguidequebec.com/cafe-saint-henri-saint-roch/) was an instant hit. The white walls, blonde wood furniture, and wall of floor-to-ceiling windows give the cafe a light and refreshing feel. There are bar-height tables stretching along the windows, and the centre of the cafe has long chunky communal tables.   \n  \nCafé Saint-Henri is a popular spot for coworking, studying, and meeting friends for coffee. They serve insanely delicious donuts, croissants, and scones; the lunch menu is quite good as well. In fact, many locals who work in the area come here for lunch. I love the croissants here, and the donuts too. They also serve a really good chocolat chaud, as well as [thé du Labrador](https://www.monsieurt.ca/en/the-du-labrador-1g.html) – another favourite.   \n  \nAdhering to the Third Wave coffee movement, Café Saint-Henri takes great pride in the quality of the coffee – which is roasted in-house and sold in bags at the cafe. They also sell bags of coffee from BC-based Parallel 49, and other third wave coffee roasters.   \n  \n**Café Saint-Henri**: 849, rue Saint-Jeseph Est, Saint-Roch • Open Mon-Fri 7:30am to 6pm, Sat-Sun 8am to 6pm • [Instagram](https://www.monsieurt.ca/en/the-du-labrador-1g.html) • **Note**: the food also includes a couple of vegan options.\n\n## Maelstrom Café\n\n![Maelstrom Cafe - cafes in Quebec City](https://i0.wp.com/urbanguidequebec.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/10/Maelstrom-Cafe-cafes-in-Quebec-City.jpg?resize=940%2C788&ssl=1)\n\nMaelstrom Café in Saint-Roch\n\nThe moment I learned about [Maelstrom Café](https://urbanguidequebec.com/cafe-maelstrom-saint-roch/)‘s concept, cafe by day and cocktail bar by night, I was hooked. I love a business that does double duty, and in Quebec City, we have a few that are quite good. They have coffee, they have cocktails, and they have coffee cocktails. It’s a coffee lover’s dream cafe!  \n  \nMaelstrom Café has an eclectic vibe with mismatched tables and chairs, exposed brick walls, and well-worn wood floors. It’s a popular spot for reading, meeting friends, and doing remote work. In the evening the atmosphere is electric as locals crowd in for drinks and cocktails. The brunch menu has dishes like labneh, breakfast sandwich, gravlax, and eggs benny – as well as a couple of vegan dishes. I’m a huge fan of the breakfast sandwich and the gravlax.  \n  \nThis is a Third Wave coffee shop, with several expresso-based coffees – like most cafes. The real treat here, though, is the cold brew, coffee slushies, and coffee cocktails. Tea lovers will find plenty to enjoy here, too. Maelstrom Café now offers an evening menu of nibbles (think hummus, labneh, scallop crudo, etc). Easily one of my favourite cafes in Quebec City.  \n  \n**Maelstrom Café**: 181, Saint-Vallier Est, Saint-Roch • Open Mon 8am to 5pm, Tues-Thurs 8am to 12pm, Fri 8am to 1am, Sat 9am to 1am, Sun 9am to 5pm • [Instagram](https://www.instagram.com/maelstromsaintroch)\n\n## Le Petit-Dep\n\n![Le Petit-Dep - cafes in Quebec City](https://i0.wp.com/urbanguidequebec.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/10/Le-Petit-Dep-cafes-in-Quebec-City.jpg?resize=940%2C788&ssl=1)\n\nLe Petit-Dep in Quartier Petit-Champlain\n\nA short walk from the Funiculaire and the Breakneck Steps in Le Petit-Dep, a funky little épicerie and cafe which opened in early 2024. Originally from Montreal (there are a few in Old Montreal), Le Petit-Dep is best for grab-and-go coffees and snacks – although, there are four round stools in front of one window if you want to try enjoying a coffee and snack inside.  \n  \nThe shelves here are filled with Quebec terroir; like artisanal maple syrup, jams, and chutneys. There are local wines, fancy candles, handcrafted soaps, and little souvenirs, too. The cafe menu has espresso-based classics, as well as lemonades, teas, chocolat chaud (so good), and my newest obsession, GOLDEN MILK (coconut oil, turmeric, maple syrup, and milk).   \n  \nIn the fridge are wrapped gourmet sandwiches, and the display cases are filled with all kinds of sweet treats (vegan and GF options), croissants, and chocolatine. If you want to indulge a little, buy a New York Roll – a round croissant with a sweet creamy filling. The staff here are super friendly; a great addition to the collection of cafes in Quebec City.  \n  \n**Le Petit-Dep**: 52, rue Sous-le-fort, Quartier Petit-Champlain • Open daily 8am to 5pm (until 6pm Fri-Sat) • [Instagram](https://www.instagram.com/lepetitdep)\n\n## Les Cafés du Soleil\n\n![Les Cafes du Soleil](https://i0.wp.com/urbanguidequebec.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/10/Les-Cafes-du-Soleil.jpg?resize=940%2C788&ssl=1)\n\nLes Cafés du Soleil in Vieux-Port (Old Port)\n\nAn institution in Vieux-Port since 1993, Les Cafés du Soleil is a small café with a couple of tables inside, and a little patio in summer. This is one of the best cafes in Quebec City for fresh flavourful coffee blends – they roast in small batches every week.   \n  \nI really love the neighbourhood vibe here. It’s a truly special place where one can get a sense of the enjoyment of life that is essential to the culture of Quebec. The coffee blends are made with beans from places like The Galapagos, Nicaragua, Kenya, Ethiopia, and Guatemala.   \n   \nIt’s a popular spot among locals in the neighbourhood, and you’ll see coffee lovers of multiple generations lining up for their favourite brew. The small display case at the cash has locally baked pastries like chocolatine and croissants – my greatest weakness in Quebec. If you’re lucky enough to snag a table, sit back and enjoy the atmosphere, but don’t stay too long, give others a chance to enjoy it too.  \n  \n**Les Cafés du Soleil**: 143, rue Saint-Paul; Vieux-Port • Open Mon-Fri 7:30am to 2:30pm, Sat-Sun 8am to 5pm • [Instagram](https://www.instagram.com/lescafesdusoleil/) • **Note**: This is not a cafe for pulling out a laptop to work.\n\n## l’Épicurien\n\n![l'Épicurien on Avenue Maguire in Quebec City](https://i0.wp.com/urbanguidequebec.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/10/lEpicurien.jpg?resize=940%2C788&ssl=1)\n\nl’Épicurien in Sillery\n\nTechnically l’Épicurien on Avenue Maguire in Sillery is an épicerie fine and ptisserie, with good coffee and chocolat chaud, and a delicious array of foods. It’s a popular breakfast spot, where the Breton-style crêpes (both savoury and sweet) are in high demand – and served until 3pm. The menu also features gourmet eggs benny, hearty salads, and sandwiches. Personally, I’m a big fan of the ptisserie creations, which never disappoint.  \n  \nl’Épicurien opened its doors on trendy Avenue Maguire in 2019 and quickly became a neighbourhood favourite. They have a large selection of gourmet prêt-à-manger (ready-to-eat) meals, a superb selection of cheeses (including one of my favourite cheeses, [Sabot de Blanchette](https://fromagesduquebec.qc.ca/en/cheeses/goat-milk/fromagerie-la-suisse-normande/sabot-de-blanchette-le)) and charcuterie. Best of all, the prices here are very fair – the burrata is cheaper here than at big-brand grocery stores!  \n  \nThis is one of my favourite “cafes” in Quebec City, and an excuse to spend more time on Avenue Maguire – sometimes picking up Montreal-style bagels at Maguire Bagel Café before going home.  \n  \n**l’Épicurien**: 1292 Avenue Maguire; Sillery • Open Tues-Fri 8am to 6pm (until 7pm Thurs-Fri), Sat 9am to 6pm, Sun 9am to 5pm • [Instagram](https://www.instagram.com/lepicurien.maguire/)\n\n---\n\nAre you looking for cafés in a particular neighbourhood? Here are all of the cafés listed in this post, by neighbourhood.\n\n**Petit-Champlain & Place Royale**:\n\n[La Maison Smith](#smith)\n\n[Le Petit Dep](#petit)\n\n**Vieux Port (Old Port)**:\n\n[Café Apotek](#apotek)\n\n[Les Cafés du Soleil](#soleil)\n\n**Saint-Jean-Baptiste**:\n\n[deTerroir Café](#deterroir)\n\n**Saint-Roch**:\n\n[Maelstrom Café](#maelstrom)\n\n[Saint-Henri Café](#henri)\n\n**Sillery**:\n\n[l’Épicurien](#epicurien)\n\n**Limoilou:**\n\n[Cantook Café](#cantook)\n\n![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/be69bb7acd0b33a09154a65d58b6826af14322982f55465cfee5ddd8a87613c8?s=90&d=mm&r=g)\n\n## Pamela MacNaughtan\n\nHello, I'm Pamela! I'm a travel and food writer, chocolat chaud addict, an anglophone who prefers life in Quebec.       "raw_content": "2020 - 2021 OFFICIAL TOURIST GUIDE Québec City and Area Parking Restroom Chemin du Roy Tourist route Route de la Nouvelle-France Tourist route Tourist information (permanent) LEGEND That is proud of its European heritage and Indigenous roots.\nThat loves to move and get you moving, with its nearby natural areas and the festive spirit that reigns all year long. That awakens all the senses and celebrates the pleasure of speaking French, the language of love, every single day.\nActive Authentic Alluring That welcomes every visitor with its legendary “joie de vivre” to make them feel right at home. Welcoming Discover “l’accent d’Amérique!” This guide will help you discover why Québec City is unlike any other city in North America and will wow the traveller in you.\nPhotos: Louise Bilodeau (Wendake), Luc-Antoine Couturier (plains), Valérie Busque (Chic Shack), Barrons-Nous (couple) 1 Welcome Québec City and the surrounding area will charm and surprise you!\nWelcome!\nFollow us on your favourite social network: /quebeccite JOIN IN THE CONVERSATION AND SHARE YOUR FAVOURITE MOMENTS IN THE QUÉBEC REGION USING THE HASHTAG #QUEBECCITE Plan your stay 204 24 hours in Québec City 207 The best of Québec City in 72 hours 214 5 days in Québec City 217 Île d’Orléans 222 Chemin du Roy 226 Route de la Nouvelle-France 229 Useful information 232 Getting around 236 The boroughs 148 Old Québec 150 Petit-Champlain 156 Old Port 161 Saint-Jean-Baptiste 166 Montcalm 172 Saint-Roch 178 Limoilou 183 Saint-Sauveur 188 Sillery 193 Sainte-Foy Area 198 226 178 207 3 Welcome Our region 4 Four seasons in our city 6 Why visit Québec City 12 Québec in a nutshell 14 Top 10 Must-See Attractions 16 What to do? 22 Themes Living History 27 Vibrant Culture 39 Outdoors Nearby 56 Local Food 65 Family Fun 89 Shopping 98 Seasons Summer 107 Fall 119 Winter 127 Spring 139 Contact Québec City Tourism If you have a question or comment, or need documents, our trained staff will be happy to answer your questions or help plan your stay.\n1-877-783-1608 418 641-6290 quebec-cite.com info@quebec-cite.com 42 39 42 Content Did you know that... Old Québec has been a UNESCO World Heritage jewel since 1985, thanks to the conservation of its fortifications?\n2 Québec stands out because of its history, its human warmth and its beauty in all seasons, which is good to discover all year long. Turn the page and fall in love... Our region 4 FOUR SEASONS IN OUR CITY Québec City is more than just a city; it’s an entire region to explore, in winter and in summer.\nPhoto: Robert Greffard, Ville de Québec The Château Frontenac as seen from the Pierre-Dugua-De Mons terrace.\nWith a fantastic view of the river and stunning vistas, the must-see Dufferin Terrace and the Château Frontenac make it feel like you’ve just stepped into a fairy tale. Starting at the Place D’Youville intersection, the boutiques, restaurants, churches and historic buildings along Saint-Jean Street create an eclectic and entirely unique ambiance. And then there’s the Citadelle, the plains of Abraham, the Old Port, Place-Royale, Petit-Champlain and Parliament Hill. Go to pages 150 to 171 to learn more about these extraordinary places!\nHistory in every corner Once in the heart of Old Québec, take the time to wander through the European-style streets and discover the over 400 years of history of this bastion of French America.\nA RICH HERITAGE TO EXPLORE Inquisitive minds are welcome here! Given Québec City’s long and abundant history, there’s something to learn and experience on every street corner! Head to pages 28 to 31 to see!\nLa Citadelle de Québec Photos: Jeff Frenette Photography (left), André-Olivier Lyra (right) 8 Four seasons, one lifestyle Summer on the patio, winter in ice skates, and anything is possible in between! It goes without saying: the city is alive and buzzing in every season! Quebecers know how to have fun, regardless of the weather. With the countless festivals, museums, dinners out, urban artworks and window-shopping strolls, there’s plenty to choose from to enjoy the city 365 days a year.\nA musical language Set at a geographical and cultural crossroads, Québec City is like a hyphen between Europe and the Americas, serving as the honoured guardian of the French language in North America. Quebecers are proud to speak French and will jump at the chance to chat with you in their langue natale*!\n*mother tongue Vibrant and alive all year long Savour the fun and soak in the festive atmosphere any time of year at the Québec Winter Carnival, summertime’s Festival d’été de Québec and many more. Let your senses be your guide through this world-renowned and completely unique culture. Québec knows how to celebrate!\nFestival d'été de Québec 9 Our region Summer Looking to take a breather away from the city? Nothing’s better than a short jaunt to parc national de la Jacques-Cartier, the Parc de la Chute-Montmorency, Île d’Orléans or Mont-Sainte-Anne. There are choices galore! Want to bike, swim, canoe, climb or hike? You can do it all in the Québec sunshine!\nFall Nature lovers’ favourite time to visit Québec is in the fall. The trees clad in blazing leaves put on an unforgettable show! Admire the colours on a walk in the woods or while observing wildlife, like the snow geese at Cap-Tourmente national wildlife area.\nWinter Winter in Québec City is simply magical! Hit the invigorating ski slopes, be filled with wonder while cross-country skiing or snowshoeing, explore miles and miles of snowmobile and fatbike trails or go for an exciting dogsled excursion. Spring The maple sap is running, and that means it’s time to satisfy your sweet tooth at the sugar shack. Springtime is also when the snowmelt swells up the rivers, making it an excellent moment to see the Montmorency Falls and Canyon Sainte-Anne in action!\nSeasonal pleasures The best season for an outdoor vacation in Québec City is the one you choose!\nPhotos: Francis Gagnon (dogs), Francis Fontaine (runners), Jeff Frenette Photography (right) 10 An entire region to explore Québec City and its lively neighbourhoods have much to offer, but the surrounding area also has gems to discover!\nÎle d’Orléans: Treasure island Île d’Orléans, just 15 minutes from downtown, across from the Montmorency Falls, is well worth a visit! Head off counter-clockwise around the island, stopping when you like to sample its many treasures.\nPortneuf: A natural way to relax The area around Portneuf is known for the legendary chemin du Roy and its charming towns. Anyone who loves outdoor sports and nature will get their fill in this area. Côte-de-Beaupré: A magnificent history A drive along the Route de la Nouvelle-France scenic route will not disappoint. The picturesque landscapes bordered by the St. Lawrence River, the Montmorency Falls and Mont-Sainte-Anne are Côte-de-Beaupré’s irrefutable assets. It’s also a sacred pilgrimage site and an excellent destination for those seeking local culinary delights. La Jacques-Cartier: A breath of fresh air The mountains and forests of the La Jacques-Cartier area make it a real recreational paradise in every season. The national park, the Réserve faunique des Laurentides and the Station touristique Stoneham are just a few of its attractions for nature lovers!\nTake the road to the outdoors and visit Île d’Orléans!\n11 Our region Why visit Québec City?\nEACH NEIGHBOURHOOD IN THE CITY HAS ITS OWN PERSONALITY. GO TO PAGE 148 TO LEARN MORE! 12 World-class food scene Québec City’s chefs—from the newly arrived to the seasoned experts—are deeply passionate about food and contribute daily to the city’s culinary renown. The food scene is recognized for its range of culinary experiences, its top-notch service and its French, British, North American and Indigenous influences. Close to the great outdoors A short 20-minute drive from Old Québec brings you to breathtaking natural areas, like the Montmorency Falls and the dizzying heights of Canyon Sainte-Anne. You might want to check out the parc national de la Jacques-Cartier or one of the many rivers that flow through the area, including the majestic St. Lawrence!\nFour seasons of fun Come enjoy all kinds of events that delight Quebecers throughout the year, like fireworks, concerts, historical celebrations, plays and sporting events. With so many exciting choices, it’s not surprising that Québec City is one of the top travel destinations in the world!\nBecause Québec City is completely unique in North America! While there are hundreds of good reasons, here are the top 6.\nA UNESCO World Heritage treasure It’s nearly impossible to resist the charms of Old Québec, the heartland of French America and the only walled city north of Mexico! Soak up the old city’s exceptional heritage and European charm as you stroll its 400-year-old cobblestone streets. A distinct culture in Canada Quebecers have a reputation for their warm welcome and their pride in their Francophone culture. And given Québec City’s location in North America, don’t be surprised to hear “Bonjour! Hello!” You’ll feel welcome in both English and French! The many activities and attractions in and around Québec City reflect this vibrant, authentic and rich culture. A winter wonderland A covering of snow transforms Québec City into an enchanting wonderland. Throughout winter, and especially during the holidays, Old Québec turns up the magic. And there are still a whole host of activities to enjoy, in the city and in nature. Just be sure to bundle up and the snowy season is yours to enjoy! 1 4 2 5 3 6 Photo: Emmanuel Coveney 13 Our region Perched with an unimpeded view of the entire city, Fairmont Le Château Frontenac is the world’s most photo graphed hotel!\nFounded in 1608, Québec City is the oldest Francophone city in North America. Québec is an Algonquin word that means where the river narrows.\nT his city's two parts—Upper T own and Lower T own—are connected by some 30 stairways. In winter, the city is home to the only ice hotel in North America.\nThe chemin du Roy (King’s Road), built in 1737, is the oldest roadway in Canada. It passes through the most beautiful towns in the Portneuf region.\nInaugurated in 1919, the Québec Bridge is the world’s longest cantilever bridge.\nAlthough the city’s culinary reputation is firmly established, poutine (French fries, gravy and cheese curds) is still its most emblematic dish!\nFUN IN FRENCH POUDREUSE (POWDER) Loose, light snow; the best for skiing!\nMAGASINER (SHOP) Run errands, go shopping in stores.\nDÎNER (LUNCH) The mid-day meal. Dinner is the evening meal. DÉPANNEUR (CONVENIENCE STORE) Neighbourhood store selling pantry staples, candy and beer.\nBANC DE NEIGE (SNOWBANK) Where the snow gathers in ever-growing piles.\nQuébec in a nutshell T he only Holy Door outside of Europe is located here.\nDid you know...\nQuébec City is the only walled city north of Mexico. The fortifications have withstood the test of time so well that Old Québec was listed as a UNESCO World Heritage site in 1985.\n14 2020 Travel + Leisure readers rated Québec City as the best destination in Canada for the fourth year in a row.\n— Travel + Leisure — World’s Best Awards 2019 Québec City was named Top Cruise Destination in the U.S. and Canada.\n— Cruise Critic Cruisers’ Choice Destination Awards 2019 Québec City was ranked 3rd safest city in the world.\n— CEOWORLD Magazine Québec City ranks 8th among the 20 best cities in the world. — Condé Nast Traveler (UK) — Readers’ Choice Awards 2019 Québec City ranks 3rd among the 10 best small cities in the world (outside the United States).\n— Condé Nast Traveler (USA) — Readers’ Choice Awards 2019 2019 Québec City is named the best Canada/New England cruise destination.\n— Porthole Cruise Magazine (USA) — Readers’ Choice Award 2019 Québec City ranks 23rd out of the top 50 most beautiful cities in the world.\n— FlightNetwork (USA) The Port of Québec was named Best Canadian Port.\n— Porthole Cruise Magazine (USA) — Editor-in-Chief Awards 2019 Québec City was selected 2nd best city for families around the world, scoring 99.06.\n— Movinga Awards Year after year, the Québec City region ranks among the best destinations on the planet, if we rely on magazines specializing in travel!\n2019 15 Our region Québec City’s top 10 Must-See Attractions Whether you have two days, two weeks or two months to explore the city, don’t miss these 10 exceptional spots!\nPlace Royale 16 Old Québec Wander along the oldest streets in North America Walk along the fortifications that surround this UNESCO World Heritage site. Get a peek into history at Petit-Champlain, the place Royale and the plains of Abraham. Wrap up your tour at the Dufferin Terrace with a picture of the famous Château Frontenac, the most photographed hotel in the world!\nParc de la Chute-Montmorency Stand in awe of this waterfall, which is 30 metres taller than Niagara Falls Whether you take the cable car, the panoramic stairway or the suspended bridge, you’ll be captivated by the powerful Montmorency Falls, just 15 minutes from downtown. For the even more adventurous, three via ferrata routes and a double zipline will get your blood pumping!\nParc national de la Jacques-Cartier Explore one of the most beautiful glacial valleys in Québec This vast mountain plateau streaked with rivers is a paradise for outdoor enthusiasts. Experience how a kayak trip, a hike, a snowshoe outing, an overnight camping trip or a fishing excursion can transform into an unforgettable expedition in such lush, natural surroundings. 1 3 2 Photos: Emmanuel Coveney (left), Jeff Frenette Photography (canoe), Yves Marcoux (waterfall) 17 Our region 4 6 5 Musée national des beaux-arts du Québec Take in the inspiring works of the largest collection of art made in Québec The museum is unabashedly proud of its Québec-made art collection of more than 38,000 works. You’ll experience a vast panorama of the history of art in Québec, from the 17th century all the way up to the present. Roam the museum’s four pavilions, set in the heart of the Plains of Abraham, and appreciate the artistic talent found in the province and elsewhere. Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré Shrine Take some time for reflection at the oldest pilgrimage destination north of Mexico Walk the holy grounds of this place of worship dedicated to Sainte Anne, which has welcomed visitors for 350 years and is one of the five national shrines in Québec. Inside the church, contemplate the sumptuous stained glass, paintings, mosaics, and stone and wood sculptures. Île d’Orléans Discover local food artisans You’ll want to stop again and again to test the thousand-and-one wares at the many artisanal farms and produce stands. Fill your shopping bags during a gourmet trip to the country courtesy of Île d’Orléans, with its rich historical towns, farms, churches and century homes. Cassis Monna & Filles 18 7 8 Village Vacances Valcartier Have a blast at the biggest recreational and tourist attraction in Eastern Canada Whether you’re inside or outside at the Village Vacances Valcartier and its Bora Parc water amusement park, you’ll leave all your cares behind and experience pure, exhilerating fun! In winter, let loose at the continent’s biggest winter playground and explore the Hôtel de Glace, an ephemeral, architectural wonder that is absolutely unique in North America!\nAquarium du Québec Immerse yourself in the fascinating universe of nearly 10,000 specimens Located on a magnificent site with a view of the St. Lawrence River, the Aquarium du Québec will introduce you to the living things hidden in the depths of the Pacific Ocean as you walk through the underwater tunnel. You’ll meet some marine mammals, too, like polar bears, walruses and several seal species. Photos: Cassis Monna & Filles, Jeff Frenette Photography (museum, church, Village Vacances Valcartier, Aquarium), Danny Vachon (Hôtel de Glace) 10 Wendake Learn about the Huron-Wendat people’s ancestral traditions Immerse yourself in Indigenous culture at the Huron-Wendat Nation in Wendake. Dance performances and storytelling of myths and legends take you into the nation’s imagination. The Huron-Wendat Museum and the boutiques displaying handcrafted treasures give you a glimpse into the proud nation’s culture and traditional skills.\n9 Musée de la civilisation Increase your knowledge at Québec City’s popular interactive museum The whole family can participate in interactive workshops on historical, current and sometimes quirky topics. The huge museum’s 10 exhibits take a fresh look at a wide range of cultures and time periods, focusing on Québec identity and global issues as well. THE MUSÉE DE LA CIVILISATION IS A TRUE “HOUSE OF THE WORLD” WHERE EVERYONE MEETS TO LEARN ABOUT TODAY’S SOCIETY. Photos: Jeff Frenette Photography (museum), François Després (Wendake) 20 Centre Infotouriste® de Québec 12, rue Sainte-Anne, Québec (Near Fairmont Le Château Frontenac) quebec-cite.com • info@quebec-cite.com 418-641-6290 • 1-877-783-1608 TOURIST INFORMATION No matter your personality, if you are travelling alone, as a couple, as a family or as a group, Québec has something to offer you in every season!\nWhat to do?\n22 22 What to do?\nRue du Cul-de-Sac, in Petit-Champlain 24 Here, you will find 400 years of history and 1001 experiences to have. ONE CITY, SIX ANGLES Living History Follow in the footsteps of history at astounding and remarkable heritage sites.\nVibrant Culture Awaken your emotions by immersing yourself in an original, vibrant culture that merges art and urban pleasures.\nOutdoors Nearby Venture to the nearby nature areas and experience the outdoors like never before.\nLocal Food Sample local flavours and cooking that combine influences and honour the seasons.\nFamily Fun Create lasting memories you and your loved ones will talk about for a long time.\nShopping Shop for original treasures made by passionate local craftspeople.\nPierre-Dugua-De Mons Terrace Photos: Jeff Frenette Photography 25 What to do?\ntourismewendake.ca Accommodations • Museum • Spa • Activities • Longhouse • Restaurants Terraces • Boutiques • Native Arts • Myths & Legends • Traditional site Aboriginal experience Living History More than 400 years of history Québec City was founded by French explorer Samuel de Champlain in 1608, establishing the cradle of French civilization in North America. A little later, the siege of Québec City and the famous Battle of the Plains of Abraham irrevocably changed the future of New France. The era of French reign had a huge influence on the culture and particularly on the dominant language, still spoken today in the province. Discover this rich heritage and the English and Indigenous influences!\nSaint-Louis Gate tourismewendake.ca Accommodations • Museum • Spa • Activities • Longhouse • Restaurants Terraces • Boutiques • Native Arts • Myths & Legends • Traditional site Aboriginal experience Photo: Claudel Huot 10 ways to travel back in time Welcome to the only walled city north of Mexico! With its rich past built on its European roots, Québec City is overflowing with historical museums and attractions. Get ready to experience over 400 years of history in this bastion of French America!\nA UNESCO World Heritage site History can be felt everywhere in Old Québec, at every street corner, monument, historical site and heritage building. The best way to appreciate its long history is to walk the cobbled streets. You’ll be dazzled by the various neighbourhoods’ charm and the European architecture. The fortifications around the city are 4.6 km long!\nBuilt in 1688, Notre-Dame-des-Victoires is the oldest stone church in North America. GET IN ON THE FUN!\nIn early August, immerse yourself in colonial times in the midst of the authentic Old Québec setting at the New France Festival. Parades in period attire, street performances, shows, plays, historical re-enactments and public markets bring the era’s habits and customs to life during the entertaining historical festival!\n1 28 Photos: Jeff Frenette Photography (church, wall, couple), Francis Gagnon (powwow) Places of historical importance Walk along part of the 4.6-kilometre Fortifications of Québec that surround Old Québec. Wrap it up at Artillery Park where people in period costumes welcome you into the 17th and 18th century military buildings. Listen to the history of the Saint-Louis Forts and Châteaux— an archeological crypt discovered under the Dufferin Terrace— where decisions were made that changed the fate of North America. Then find out what Jacques Cartier’s first winter in 1535 was like and delve in to the Jesuits’ story here at the Cartier-Brébeuf National Historic Site. A rich military legacy Explore the “Gibraltar of North America” on Cap Diamant. An integral part of the city’s fortifications, the star-shaped Citadelle of Québec is an excellent representation of the city’s military past. Also visit the Musée Royal 22e Régiment located in a French gunpowder magazine from 1750 and in an old military prison from 1842, the summertime changing of the guard and cannon firing, the official residence of the Governor General of Canada, and the Musée du Fort devoted to Québec City’s military history. The legacy of the founding nations The Québec City area resonates with the history of people who are unique on the continent, proud of their Indigenous and European roots. Get acquainted with the Huron-Wendat people, an Indigenous nation that has witnessed the shaping of the country. And visit several buildings and remnants of early New France that are still here, like the Maison des Jésuites de Sillery, and from the days of English reign, like the Holy Trinity Cathedral.\nThe European atmosphere of the city is so romantic.\nTake a look at traditional Huron-Wendat clothing during a powwow. 2 3 4 29 What to do? — Living History Architectural marvels Religious tourists will find that Québec City’s churches hold veritable heritage treasures. The amazing crypt at the Notre-Dame de Québec Basilica-Cathedral preserves the tombs of four governors from the New France era. Also of note are the Notre-Dame-des-Victoires Church, erected in 1688 (the oldest stone church in North America), the Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré Shrine and the many century-old churches in the area.\n5 6 7 A place of power Meet the men and women who made their mark on Québec history with a visit to the 26 bronze statues representing them in front of the imposing façade of the Parliament Building. Inside, take in the impressive décor of the building, constructed from 1786 to 1886. And don’t miss getting a glimpse of the National Assembly Chamber, the Legislative Council Chamber, the President’s Gallery and the highly esteemed Le Parlementaire Restaurant. Sites that witnessed Canada’s making Head out to the Plains of Abraham, where the French and English armies clashed. The Battlefields Park is one of the most prestigious urban parks in the world. You’ll find several exhibits and interpretation activities here as well. The architecture of the Parliament Building, virtually unique in North America, is inspired from the Second Empire style. The Basilica-Cathedral is classified as a national historic monument of Canada.\nThe Martello Towers are testaments to the city’s military past.\nPhotos: Jeff Frenette Photography (basilica, Séminaire de Québec), Luc-Antoine Couturier (plains), Francis Gagnon (house winter) 30 8 9 10 An island that’s seen history Revel in the unhampered view of the river during a visit to the Parc Maritime de Saint-Laurent on Île d’Orléans. The costumed guides at the Manoir Mauvide-Genest will take you back to the time of the French seigneurial system. Get a taste of life as an early settler at the Maison Drouin, an authentic house from the days of New France. And at the Maison de nos Aïeux, trace the history of the 300 families who founded the island colony.\nTreasures before you Several artifacts in the vaulted cellars of the Îlot des Palais shine a light on the era of the Intendant’s Palace and Jean Talon’s brewery, dating back to the 18th century. At the Augustines’ museum, the nuns were the ones who carefully preserved nearly 40,000 objects, starting in 1639. Lastly, the continent’s colonial history and the religious heritage of the Séminaire de Québec are rekindled before your eyes at the Musée de l’Amérique francophone. Age-old roadways Admire magnificent ancestral homes, mills and churches along the Route de la Nouvelle-France, one of the oldest roadways in North America. Or take the Chemin du Roy (King’s Road) in Portneuf, built in 1737 as the first road between Québec City and Montréal.\nÎle d'Orléans is full of well-preserved ancestral homes.\nThe Séminaire de Québec, beautifully well-preserved, was built in 1663. Houses with roofs at a steeper angle than usual are said to be French inspired.\n31 What to do? — Living History Indigenous history and culture What to expect on your visit: Indigenous hospitality and the lure of a timeless world built on wisdom and traditions. Begin by walking through Old Wendake and admiring the Notre-Dame-de-Lorette church, built in 1730, and its treasures. Learn about the chiefs who lived in the Tsawenhohi House, dating back to the early 19th century. Go on a guided visit of the home, watch handicraft demonstrations and explore the exhibits of cultural objects and archeological items. The Fresque du peuple wendat mural depicts myths and legends of the Huron Nation and other First Nations. Nearby, the stunning Kabir Kouba Falls awaits your visit. Stop at the interpretation centre to learn more about the geological and human history of this unique natural site. Discover ancestral traditions in Wendake Learn about the Huron-Wendat Nation and its culture in Wendake, an Indigenous community right in Québec City.\nA visit to Wendake wouldn’t be complete without stopping at the Onhoüa Chetek8e Traditional Huron Site. This authentic replica of a Huron village provides a glimpse into the nation’s history, traditions and lifestyle. Wendake is also home to a vibrant community of artists and artisans. The local galleries and stores offer a wide range of artworks and traditional and contemporary handcrafted items.\nTraditional longhouse 32 Food and hospitality More curious visitors won’t miss the occasion to try Indigenous foods at local restaurants, like Nek8arre and Sagamité. The Hôtel-Musée Premières Nations welcomes you to its enchanting setting on the banks of the Akiawenrahk’ (Saint-Charles) River. This four-star hotel and its gourmet restaurant, La Traite, give you the chance to explore the many facets of the Huron-Wendat Nation’s rich universe. After a visit to the museum and a phenomenal meal, the storyteller will take you straight to the heart of Indigenous myths and legends. Continue the adventure by spending the night in a traditional longhouse!\nThe fascinating powwow Don’t miss the chance to attend the Wendake International Pow Wow in late June! Traditionally, powwows were shamanic events or occasions CELEBRATE THE CULTURE In late August, experience the KWE! festival. This free, knowledge-sharing event hosts a range of activities—shows, workshops, discussions and more—on the cultural treasures of 11 Indigenous nations in Québec. Kwe! (That’s “Hello” in Wendat!) From June to October, take the shuttle (for a fee) between Old Québec (12 rue Sainte‑Anne) and the Hôtel‑Musée Premières Nations.\nto celebrate warriors’ exploits. Today, they are festive events, and the occasion for the First Nations to express their cultural heritage through dance, chanting and regalia. It’s a once-in-a-lifetime experience! tourismewendake.ca The powwow is an impressive traditional gathering to discover!\nPhotos : Robert Baronet (left), François Després (powwow), Jeff Frenette Photography (building) 33 What to do? — Living History The Battlefields Park, Plains of Abraham Content Partnership Photo: Melissa Guerette Although it was the stage of the great battles of 1759 and 1760, the Battlefields Park isn’t stuck in the past. This enormous city park that extends from the Plains of Abraham to the Des Braves Park and farther is as alive as ever with a host of activities. The Battlefields Park, Plains of Abraham: History comes to life LET’S HAVE FUN You can even try an escape game in Martello Tower 4.\nHistorical!\nThe veritable lungs of the city’s core, the park nicknamed Québec City’s Central Park not only preserves the city’s natural environment, but also its heritage, through a rich program of interactive activities. Your visit begins at the Plains of Abraham Museum where you can interact with its dynamic, user-friendly exhibits. Did you see the immersive film projected onto a 180-degree screen? There’s no better way to literally plunge into the action!\nAll year long, Canada’s first national historic park offers interactive activities led by historical figures like storytelling evenings—some that will make your hair stand on end! It’s the most exciting way to learn about history! And don’t forget the many outdoor shows, sporting events and the four million visitors that make the Battlefields Park a living gathering place. 835, Wilfrid-Laurier Avenue 1-855-649-6157 theplainsofabraham.ca WALKS, EXHIBITS, PICNICS, CROSS-COUNTRY SKIING AND ICE SKATING ARE JUST SOME OF THE THINGS YOU CAN DO AT THE BATTLEFIELDS PARK.\nPhotos: Melissa Guerette (left, suit), CCNB (tower) 37 Content Partnership Vibrant Culture A unique culture in North America Regardless of what you decide to do, it’s impossible to avoid the city’s joie de vivre… It’s ingrained! Take your time to savour the fun moments, immerse yourself in the festive spirit and let your senses guide you toward this culture that is known around the world for its unique character, bold cultural initiatives and warm welcome. Photo: Catherine Côté With a pride-inspiring collection of more than 38,000 works, the Musée national des beaux-arts du Québec fine arts museum now boasts a new pavilion, itself considered an architectural masterpiece. Awe and wonder guaranteed!\nThe Musée de la civilisation will surprise and inform all visitors, young and old. Explore more than 10 intriguing interactive exhibits and attend family-focused workshops on current, historical and sometimes unusual topics. Travel back in time to experience Québec City’s military history with the light and sound show at the Musée du Fort. In just 30 minutes, you’ll find out why Québec City was impenetrable!\nJust a few steps from the Holy Door, you can admire the chapel, valuable relics and other prized articles from Québec City’s religious heritage from the Séminaire de Québec collection and on display at the Musée de l’Amérique francophone.\nLocated at the Citadelle of Québec, the Musée Royal 22e Régiment possesses one of the largest military collections in Canada. The permanent exhibit traces the history of the fortress and the only French-language infantry regiment in Canada. Finally, the Plains of Abraham Museum will teach you about the major battles of the 18th century that forever changed the course of Québec history. A real voyage in time!\nStroll through avant-garde museums The museums peppered throughout the region share their rich history and varied treasures. Here are six to get you started.\nDid you know... there are 11 museums and no fewer than 19 interpretive centres in the city?\nPierre Lassonde Pavilion of Musée national des beaux-arts du Québec Photos: Jeff Frenette Photography Musée de la civilisation 40 Two exhibitions that explore Québec society Explore the 21st century realities of First Nations and Inuit communities.\nThis is our Story People of Québec Discover Québec then and now through the revolutions that changed the province.\nmcq.org BROWSE THROUGH THE HISTORY OF QUÉBEC What to do? — Vibrant Culture Morrin Centre library Briséis Trahan, 11 years old “Le Diamant made a big impression on me and I can’t wait to go back again. I liked its architecture, location and plays of all kinds, from all over the world.” Amine Haouari, 11 years old “I chose the Méduse complex because, every time I go, there are tons of activities, like movies, paintings and even the Mois Multi. It’s great!” Marine Caron, 11 years old “I chose the Morrin Centre because I really like its history and a lot of things impressed me, like the library and prison.” The favourite spots of... Machin Club, which initiates artistic and gathering projects for children in art and culture 41 8 cultural experiences you just can’t miss Feel like taking a deep dive into the Québec culture and lifestyle? Here are eight ways to do just that!\n1 Catch the rhythm of the city’s many events and festivals The city hosts an ever-changing roster of cultural festivities. As soon as summer begins, street performers inject vibrancy into the lanes of Old Québec. The Festival d’été de Québec music festival brings well-known international artists to the stage, while the walking theatre (Où tu vas quand tu dors en marchant?) is one of the best kept secrets of Québec City’s cultural scene. Turn back time during the New France Festival and celebrate the cold at the Québec Winter Carnival!\n3 Discover art that reflects who we are All around the city, historical monuments and figures rub shoulders with contemporary public art and bold, often surprising murals. In summer, an outdoor self-guided tour (Les Passages Insolites) encourages visitors to see the city from a different perspective by taking them on a tour of several temporary art installations in Old Québec. On Avenue Cartier, take a moment to admire Lumière sur l’art, where painting invites itself onto lampposts, whose giant lampshades are adorned with reproductions of works of art.\n2 Immerse yourself in French culture As the bridge between Europe and the Americas, Québec City’s great advantage is its location at a cultural and geographic crossroads. As a proud guardian of the French language in North America, the city is a great place to learn this second language. But don’t worry if the only French you know is “Bonjour.” The city welcomes all visitors and you can get by easily in English.\nPhotos: André-Olivier Lyra (Château Frontenac), Francis Gagnon (right) 42 4 Keep an ear out for literature in real life Québec’s authors and poets like to take literature out of the box, and do so at festivals celebrating the written word (Québec en toutes lettres, Salon international du livre de Québec). Drop by the Maison de la littérature public library to meet authors from here and farther afield. And take the opportunity to check out the incredible Victorian library of the Morrin Centre that’s right next door. 5 See artists at work The street called Rue du Trésor is like an outdoor gallery, with a flurry of artists exhibiting their works on the walls of the surrounding buildings. During the Nuit des Galeries in the Old Port, dozens of galleries and artisan shops open their doors in this festival combining art, music and food. Finally, in August you can't miss Plein Art and in fall, you should head to the Salon des artisans de Québec craft fair!\n6 Relax like the locals To truly capture the city’s vibe, take a break in one of the temporary summer sitting areas or winter warming stations located in all of the downtown neighbourhoods. Distinctive and welcoming, these hang-outs are the perfect place to get a sense of the city’s rhythm, chat with locals and take great photos. 8 Take in the whole city in a glance Journey up to the highest point in Québec City—the Observatoire de la Capitale—to admire the singular and spectacular views of the city, its maze of streets, the majestic St. Lawrence River and the two nearby mountain ranges. The panorama will take your breath away!\n7 Look up to appreciate original architecture Québec City owes its eclectic charm to many influences: French, British, romantic, Art Deco and more. The proof is in the magnificent repurposing of the Monastère des Augustines and the Maison de la littérature, the construction of the new super-modern Pierre Lassonde pavilion at the MNBAQ, the preservation of the Petit-Champlain neighbourhood, the charming and colourful houses and storefronts in the Saint-Jean-Baptiste neighbourhood and more!\nWhat to do? — Vibrant Culture 43 The Plains of Abraham We couldn’t even start this list without mentioning one of the most beautiful parks in the world: the Plains of Abraham, Québec City’s Central Park! With jogging, cycling and inline skating in summer and cross-country skiing, ice skating and snowshoeing in winter, the park’s trails deliver everything sports-lovers could want in every season. While you’re there, check out the Martello Towers, remnants of the city’s military past. Enjoy the green life The region’s many parks and gardens are the ideal places to unwind and to revel in the glorious views. Parc linéaire de la Rivière-Saint-Charles This 32-kilometre path meandering alongside the river from Saint-Charles Lake to the Old Port is a runner’s delight!\nJardin Jean-Paul-L’Allier With its waterfall and myriad types of plants, this cool oasis in the heart of downtown is the lungs of the Saint-Roch neighbourhood.\nPromenade Samuel-De Champlain Along the river between the Pierre-Laporte bridge and the Sillery shore, the promenade of 4.3 km is a large park built on the banks of the St. Lawrence River. The shoreline redevelopment offers walkers, cyclists and motorists new perspectives on the Québec shore.\nParc du Bois-de-Coulonge One of the city’s best public parks, this is a great spot to people-watch, play with your kids or see various bird species. Domaine de Maizerets This historical estate offers over 10 kilometres of trails that are open year-round. This is also the starting point of two bike paths: the Corridor des cheminots and the Corridor du Littoral.\nParc du Bois-de-Coulonge Photos: André-Olivier Lyra (left), Catherine Côté (right) 44 Ophelia & L’Atelier Content Partnership Ophelia and L’Atelier: There’s nothing quite like them!\nThese two unique culinary establishments add a bit of spice to the enduring and popular Grande Allée. Housed under the same roof, L’Atelier and Ophelia draw epicureans into the city’s core. Feeling like a gourmet meal in good company? Ophelia is the answer. Its chic, luxury yacht-like décor invites you into the elegant restaurant to discover its land-and-sea menu, where you’ll find grilled octopus rubbing shoulders with Angus beef, in a dazzling setting. Chef Hugues Rhéaume will tantalize your taste buds with his unparalleled attention to detail and his refined, distinctive cooking. Cheers!\nABSOLUTE MUSTS! The Jack Sparrow and Cowgirl cocktails, two house classics!\nA combination bar and bistro, this is the place to celebrate whatever occasion is at hand! Québec City’s first cocktail bar is a standout thanks to its avant-garde creativity. It was also one of the first to democratize and customize tartares, which still take the star role on L’Atelier’s mouth-watering menu, along with burgers and shared appetizers.\nOphelia 634, Grande Allée Est 418-524-8228 restaurantophelia.com L’Atelier 624, Grande Allée Est 418-522-2225 bistrolatelier.com Or are you planning a fun night out with friends? Then L’Atelier is the hip, buzzing spot for you. Its goal is to pump up the energy until late into the night. Photos: Stéphane Groleau (left), Catherine Côté (meat), André-Olivier Lyra (drink) 47 Content Partnership SkySpa’s urban location, on the 17th floor of the Complexe Jules-Dallaire, invites you to replenish your energy in the Californian baths on its terrace, with a great view of the city!\nOn the shore of the St. Lawrence River, the Strøm Nordic Spa in Old Québec lets you relax in nature, just a few steps from downtown.\nJust outside the city, the Sibéria Spa is an oasis in the middle of the forest that offers the benefits of heat therapy. Move from the hot pools, to the cold river, to the relaxation spot in the yurt!\nAfter a full day of activities at the Village Vacances Valcartier, there’s nothing better than a visit to Aroma Spa for the hot water baths, a massage or a skin care treatment.\nFor an unbeatable restful experience in the middle of the wilderness, you should try one of the two Nordique Spas, either in Mont-Sainte-Anne or in Stoneham.\nThe Spa des Neiges makes the most of the splendid views at its riverside location, right near Mont-Sainte-Anne. It’s the perfect place to let it all go after a long day skiing or hiking. Hot and Cold Gift yourself a day of deep relaxation in any season. Experience the sensations of going from hot to cold at one of the Québec region’s Nordic spas. It’s pure heaven!\nStrøm Nordic Spa in Old Québec Photos: Strøm Nordic Spa (left), Guillaume D. Cyr (right) 48 Le Capitole Content Partnership Le Capitole enters a new era This iconic figure stands tall in Place d’Youville, at the entrance to Old Québec. To celebrate its 115 years of existence, the legendary Capitole got a major facelift and a new hotel, which offers a distinctive and unique experience. New design! GOING BIG Le Capitole Theatre hosts major variety shows and large-scale musicals for everyone.\nThe new and improved version added several elements that will quickly win over clients. Apart from the completely renovated restaurant— the Ristorante Il Teatro is now two storeys and offers four private luxury loungers—there’s also a new multi-use reception hall called Le Confessionnal and an eye-catching and prestigious hotel. Built onto the northern side of the building, the hotel, with 109 rooms, suites and penthouses, also boasts luxurious meeting rooms, a pool and a terrace on the ninth floor. You’ll want to drink in the panoramic view of Old Québec and the Laurentian mountains as you check in at the reception desk on the seventh floor. History with a modern touch Its new architecture was designed to retain the character of the building, given its immense heritage value. Fully in keeping with its purpose, the new Le Capitole is even more unique and entertaining than before, with its sumptuous mix of prestige and comfort that offers an unparalleled experience in Québec City.\n972, rue Saint-Jean 418-694-4444 lecapitole.com Photos: André-Olivier Lyra (Lounge, Le Confessionnal, room) 51 Content Partnership hotelaristocrate.com 3100, chemin Saint-Louis Québec (Québec) G1W 1R8 418 653-2841 1 866 875-4850 10 minutes from Old Québec (free shuttle in season) Free parking Renowned restaurant – 4 diamonds hotelsepia.ca 1 888 294-2458 3135, chemin Saint-Louis Québec (Québec) G1W 1R9 10 minutes from Old Québec (free shuttle in season) Free parking Charging point for electric car • Free American breakfast buffet • Fitness room on site • Outdoor spa in season • 10 minutes from Old Québec (free shuttle in season) hiexquebec.com 3145, avenue des Hôtels Québec (Québec) G1W 3Z7 418 653-9321 1 800 463-1867 • Free wireless high-speed Internet • Free parking QUEBEC CITY – STE-FOY Monastère des Augustines Le Monastère des Augustines Content Partnership Soak up the ambiance As soon as you step inside, As soon as you step inside, you’ll be blown away by you’ll be blown away by the combination of modern the combination of modern architecture and carefully architecture and carefully preserved historical character. preserved historical character. Experience a unique stay Located in the historic Hôtel-Dieu de Québec monastery, the oldest monastery and hospital north of Mexico has been revamped into a wellness centre that offers authentic lodging unlike anywhere else in the world. Are you ready for an invigorating experience that will boost your overall health?\n1 Do you feel like you need a break or need to recharge your batteries? Offer yourself a relaxing stay at Le Monastère des Augustines, a haven of peace and wellness in the heart of Old Québec.\n2 Le Monastère des Augustines: 5 reasons to slow down Photos: André-Olivier Lyra (chair, bed, hall) Enjoy the area As a rare wellness establishment right in the centre of a historical city, Le Monastère is the ideal starting point for visiting various attractions, at your own pace. 77, rue des Remparts 1-844-694-1639 monastere.ca Try mindful eating Open to the public, Le Restaurant showcases healthy, local, organic cuisine. Order the Experience plan for a genuine culinary adventure in six courses. And the menu changes every night! Another great reason to keep coming back.\nCreate your perfect stay Yoga, meditation, massage… What would you like to do? Le Monastère offers a range of activities and specialized treatments to help you feel healthy and balanced.\n3 4 5 Wellness A CONTEMPORARY MISSION Every wellness stay at Le Monastère helps us carry out our cultural and social mission to provide respite to caregivers. When you take care of yourself at Le Monastère, you’re also taking care of others. You can also purchase Signature Le Monastère products in the store or online. 55 Content Partnership Outdoors Nearby Nature in all its grandeur Getting from city to nature in 15 minutes is yet another amazing quality of Québec City! And coated in a thick blanket of snow, it’s simply magical. Parc national de la Jacques-Cartier Photos: Jasmin Brochu (mountain), Luc Charbonneau (falls) At the Parc national de la Jacques-Cartier, take in the area’s natural wonders on the 100-plus kilometres of hiking trails. Whether they lead you alongside a river or into the heart of a spectacular glacial valley, the park’s trails promise exceptional views in every season. The Cap Tourmente National Wildlife Area is a protected nature area filled with stunning features, like vast coastal wetlands, plains, the mountain and, of course, the river itself. Witness the spring and fall migration of thousands of snow geese that stop here to feed. All the fun and fresh air you could want The city is surrounded on every side by incredible nature areas just begging to be visited. Here are seven outdoor experiences to enjoy them to the fullest. The Station touristique Duchesnay, on the shores of Saint-Joseph lake, is a dream location for practising an array of outdoor activities, like hiking, cross-country skiing, canoeing, stand-up paddleboarding, climbing, archery, guided outings and more. Duchesnay boasts 47.5 km of cross-country ski trails, 20 km of snowshoeing trails and 25 km of hiking trails. Mont-Sainte-Anne offers three skiable slopes that are lit at night, snow parks and 200 km of cross-country skiing trails. In summer, the mountain biking trails and hiking trails are perfect for sport-lovers of all levels. In fall, the panorama of colours will leave you in awe!\nThe Vallée Bras-du-Nord promises fun in every season, with hiking, canyoning, snowshoeing, guided backcountry skiing and fatbiking. In spring, summer and fall, you can travel the river by canoe or kayak, try out canyoning and the via ferrata, spend a few nights camping or hit the mountain biking trails. At Parc de la Chute-Montmorency, the Montmorency Falls are breathtaking standing at a dizzying 83 meters! Get close enough to feel the falls’ mist by taking the 487-step panoramic stairway or the suspended bridge. Or take the cable car up for stunning views, then give yourself goosebumps on the via ferrata. In winter, the frozen falls resemble a sparkling loaf of sugar. If you really want a thrill, try out the zipline!\nThe Canyon Sainte-Anne is entirely unique in Canada. Take one of three suspended bridges over the chasm, then try out the Air Canyon suspended ride or the via ferrata course that’s open in winter too.\nParc de la Chute-Montmorency 57 What to do? — Outdoors Nearby In a yurt Yurts come with the basic amenities, like beds with mattresses. Some of them have glass domes in the roof so you can spend the evening stargazing!\nParc national de la Jacques-Cartier Vallée Bras-du-Nord Centre de l’Hêtre In a log cabin The mournful cry of the loon and the crackling of the fire in the hearth… It’s just what lovers of open spaces would expect from a stay in an authentic log house. It’s the quintessential Canadian cabin experience!\nParc national de la Jacques-Cartier Au Chalet en bois rond Les Maisonnettes sur le Cap In a ready-to-camp Effortless camping is possible at ready-to-camp sites. Since all the equipment you’ll need is provided, it’s a great solution for camping aficionados who want to travel light and worry-free.\nForêt Montmorency Parc national de la Jacques-Cartier Parc naturel régional Portneuf Réserve faunique de Portneuf In an igloo Since snow is an excellent insulator, inside an igloo is surprisingly comfortable and soundproof. For the ultimate experience, build your own igloo then spend the night in it!\nAuberge Le P’tit Bonheur Expéditions de la Rivière Blanche Extraordinary sleeping arrangements!\nSleeping in the middle of nature is great. But trying out a very different sleeping experience, like in a longhouse or ice hotel, takes it to a whole new level!\nParc national de la Jacques-Cartier Photo: Francis Gagnon 58 • Hotel, lodge, or cabin accommodations • Bistro cuisine • Arbraska aerial adventure circuit • Water activities and other organized adventures • Bike path NEW BEACH AREA AND WATER ACTIVITIES ONLY 30 MINUTES FROM QUEBEC CITY 1 877 511-5885 sepaq.com/duchesnay DUCHESNAY Treat yourself to a river crossing aboard the Québec–Lévis ferry. The panoramic view of Québec City and Cap Diamant is breathtaking in every season!\nStop in the Baie de Beauport, just a few minutes outside the city, to try your hand at a panoply of nautical activities. The unique maritime location offers wind-surfing, catamaraning, kayaking, sailboating, stand-up paddle-boarding and kitesurfing!\nFamilies, walkers, runners, cyclists and roller-bladers will adore spending a warm day at the delightful Promenade Samuel-De Champlain linear park. Are you up for it?\nHead out from the Old Port in a dinghy to get a completely different view of the Château Frontenac, the Montmorency Falls and Île d’Orléans. Or take a cruise on the river, with brunch, dinner or a show!\nAre you the athletic and adventurous type? Try ice canoeing! Expert guides lead organized trips out on the freezing river. You’ll quickly learn that the river never sleeps, even in winter!\nThe majestic St. Lawrence River The St. Lawrence River is part of Québec City’s DNA. To experience it to the fullest, go ahead and get out there, in a sailboat, on a kayak or in a dinghy!\nPhotos: Anthony Desgagnés (windsurfing), Jeff Frenette Photography (couple), Xavier Girard (right) 60 Monastère des Augustines Tourisme Jacques-Cartier Content Partnership Tourisme Jacques-Cartier: The biggest playground in Québec! Are you craving the fresh air? The Jacques-Cartier region is an outdoor enthusiast’s paradise, in all seasons! Just 20 minutes from downtown Québec City, it’s the perfect destination for carefully planned or impromptu excursions.\nWhether you’ve got a stay with family or friends in mind, or it’s a last-minute outing, there’s something here for everyone! Are you adventurous? Contemplative? Take it at your own pace! From Parc national de la Jacques-Cartier to Station Duchesnay, there’s a vast network of hiking trails at your fingertips. Depending on the season, thrill-seekers can hop on a fatbike or mountain bike, or speed down the tubing hills or waterslides at Village Vacances Valcartier.\nLooking for an out-of-the-ordinary experience? How about sleeping in a geodesic dome at the Centre de l’Hêtre, riding a dogsled or relaxing at a spa perched on the shores of the Jacques-Cartier River, all in the same day? You’ll even have time to get back to town for an evening show!\nNature is well within reach in the Jacques-Cartier region, and there are several packages that enable you to enjoy a range of activities. 1-877-844-2358 jacques-cartier.com Great escape DID YOU KNOW?\nThe national park offers more than 100 km of hiking trails in summer and over 90 km of snowshoeing trails in winter.\nPhotos: Xavier Girard (canoe, little boy), Christyna Merette Photographe (dome), Village Vacances Valcartier (tubes) 63 Content Partnership TWO UNIQUE DESTINATIONS IN QUEBEC CITY LUXURY FOR EVERYONE #BEexperience #ESLinspiration 1 800 463-4390 | LeBonneEntente.com 1 833 463-8998 | EntourageResort.com SLEEP WELL. MOVE WELL. EAT & DRINK WELL.\nLocal Food An unforgettable culinary experience Québecois cuisine combines French and Indigenous knowledge with British influences to entice food-lovers with the best of all worlds. Here, passion-fuelled artisans and cutting-edge chefs work together to showcase local products. Here, the concept of farm-to-table isn’t just a trend: it’s a way of life. Here, every meal quickly becomes a pretext for a celebration, and Québecers are proud to share their unique gastronomy. Pull up a chair and enjoy!\nGrand Marché de Québec Photo: Valérie Busque The island’s left side exudes the scents of apples and wine. Our first favourite stop is in Saint-Pierre at Cassis Monna & Filles, where they have transformed the liqueur-maker trade into an art. At the gourmet restaurant La Monnaguette, the Monna family serves one of the best handcrafted soft ice creams in the Québec City area.\nWhen the clock strikes noon, it’s time for a break at the Isle de Bacchus winery and vineyard. The feast reaches its summit with a delicious glass of ice cider from the Cidrerie Verger Joe Giguère and a selection of delectable cheeses from Fromages de l’isle d’Orléans. Intersperse all this good food with stops along the Royal Road, which goes all around the island, to taste the garden-based products made by the island farmers and witness their infectious zest for life.\nContinuing your trip, you’ll arrive at the island’s eastern point, where there’s a required stop at the Confiserie de la vieille école, a confectionery that concocts fudge and nostalgic candies that can be enjoyed at the end of the pier or on the sandy beach.\nArtisanal soft ice cream at Cassis Monna & Filles Île d’Orléans: a food lovers’ island Some 15 minutes from Québec City, a legendary island holds hidden gourmet treasures. Get out the picnic baskets and jump in the car. Destination? The island of gourmet delights!\nNext, there’s La Boulange where their buttery brioche is best appreciated on the sunny patio. The ultimate delicacy is to pair a warm slice of bread with Confiturerie Tigidou jam, made with berries, fresh herbs, boreal spices and lemon.\nIsle de Bacchus vineyard Photos: Jeff Frenette Photography (Isle de Bacchus, cheese, La Boulange), Caroline Grégoire (ice cream), Francis Fontaine (wine) Local treats from L’Île d’Orléans 66 Don’t feel like driving around the entire island? Take a shortcut on the north-south crossroad, Route du Mitan. Feeling thirsty? Pop into the Pub Le Mitan for handcrafted beer made by the local Microbrasserie de l’Île d’Orléans.\nAnd don’t miss the Panache Mobile in Sainte-Pétronille. This gourmet food truck tucked between the grapevines of the Vignoble Ste-Pétronille vineyard serves up exceptional food accompanied by divine elixirs. And the view is majestic, like everywhere else on this enchanted island!\n271 SPACIOUS ROOMS FOR YOUR VACATION OR BUSINESS TRIP AND SUITES WITH KITCHENETTE.
