0 1 4 2 0 1 5 A RICH HISTORIC HERITAGE, A GOURMET TRADITION AND GORGEOUS LANDSCAPES AWAIT.\nTOURISME.ILEDORLEANS.COM 1 866 941.9411 A place OF CULTURE AND HISTORY Photo: André-Olivier Lyra Le Saint-Amour: An undying passion After more than 40 years, what motivates Chef Jean-Luc Boulay to stay at Le Saint-Amour, which he co-founded, nourished and brought to its peak? “I’m still passionate about what I do. I have an amazing team that’s as dedicated as I am.” And he’s nowhere near ready to hang up his apron!\nA well-established gourmet institution in Québec City, Le Saint-Amour brings to life the full range of French haute cuisine in local, up-to-date flavours. While some flock there for the popular duck foie gras, others are drawn to the new dishes, like the seafood plate featuring Québec products including abalone, Stimpson’s surfclam and sea urchin. Saint-Amour’s menu proposes a selection of dishes for everyone, ranging from meat and fish options to vegetarian. The final touch—dessert—is a work of art, again highlighting the finesse and inquiry that goes into each creation. The most exacting wine aficionados will be pleased to know the restaurant’s wine cellar has won the Wine Spectator Award of Excellence several times, in recognition of the quality and diversity of its wines. Have a seat in the garden! Even when the mercury dips, the solarium still offers sunlight and greenery. The restaurant’s warm décor, white tablecloths and attentive service are what co-founder Chef Boulay, a virtual city ambassador, believes keep customers coming back again and again 48, rue Sainte-Ursule 418-694-0667 saint-amour.com IMPRESSIVE!\nThe Saint-Amour wine menu includes more than 13,000 carefully selected bottles. Sheer delight Photos: André-Olivier Lyra (portrait, dessert) 71 Content Partnership Stop by one of the many restaurants in the region to sample traditional Québecois cuisine, like cretons (a pork spread), beans with lard, pea soup, meat pâté, sugar pie or pouding chômeur (a syrup-soaked cake). For the best of the best, try Buffet de l’Antiquaire in the Old Port that’s been run by the same family for generations, or Aux Anciens Canadiens in Upper Town, lodged in a magnificent ancestral home built in 1675. If you’re heading to Île d’Orléans, visit the appropriately named Les Ancêtres restaurant and enjoy its majestic view of the Montmorency Falls!\nDID YOU SAY POUTINE?\nA visit to Québec City wouldn’t be complete without an illustrious poutine! It’s particularly satisfying on a cold winter day or with a great beer. Where do you go for the best? With so many opinions, it’s nearly impossible to narrow it down! Chez Ashton, Casse-Croûte Pierrot, Chez Gaston, Frite Alors!, Chic Shack, Poutineville, Le Projet… Or try them all!\nTreat your sweet tooth to maple A trip to the sugar shack means a steady flow of maple syrup and maple taffy on snow. But it’s also the joyful music of the fiddle, accordion and wood spoons, musicians playing one rigodon after another to fuel the festive feel, and tables weighed down with traditional dishes. For an urban version at any time of year, head to La Bûche in Old Québec.\nComfort food Our French-inspired cuisine was forced to adapt to difficult conditions during the colony’s early days. In the struggle to build a new country in the midst of harsh winters, with many mouths to feed but little food, substantial meals were essential!\nEating local In collaboration with Allison Van Rassel, food journalist Photos: Caroline Décoste (poutine), Alice Chiche (portrait), André-Olivier Lyra (waitress), Bernard Dagenais (L'Orygine) 72 So French Many French chefs are drawn to Québec City. That’s why the city can boast bread and pastries that are just as good as those in Paris! And French cuisine is very much alive here, particularly at the classic Le Continental, the authentic Lyonnais Le Pied Bleu, the sophisticated Saint-Amour, the friendly Café du monde and the original Clocher Penché.\nFoods from here and afar Québecois cuisine has been influenced for over 400 years by French, British and Indigenous traditions. Today’s local gastronomy is proud of its varied origins!\nIndigenous influences In the purest tradition, this cuisine relies on the basics: hunting, fishing and gathering. Consequently, Huron restaurants feature corn, game meats and fish. Wild mint, balsam fir jelly, wild berries and black spruce add a unique touch to these savoury local meals. You’ve got to try bannock and sagamité, two typical Indigenous dishes. Experience Indigenous cuisine at La Traite, Restaurant NEK8ARRE at the Traditional Huron Site and Sagamité in Old Québec.\nNorthern inspiration The heir of northern cookery, boreal cuisine changes with the seasons and relies on our forests as its pantry. Have you ever tasted green alder pepper, balsam fir, haskap berries or sea buckthorn? Discover these uniquely North American plants and fruits in renowned restaurants like Chez Boulay, Tanière3, L’Orygine, Légende, Kerrmess and the Monastère des Augustines.\nCafé du Monde L'Orygine Build up an appetite Since walking makes you hungry, taking a food tour is one of the most fun ways to explore the city’s neighbourhoods or Île d’Orléans! Discover Québecois cuisine, the trendiest new restaurants or local island food with Cicérone Tours, HQ Tourism Services, Local Québec City Food Tours or Québec Bus Tour. Good walking and above all... good tasting!\nTime to eat!\nQuébec City treats its gourmet visitors well all year long with high-calibre food festivals. In March, there’s Foodcamp Québec. In May, Québec Exquis! presents about two dozen restaurants and producers, then there’s the Fêtes gourmandes de Neuville in late August. November welcomes the Québec table gourmande. And there are several special-focus weeks: Poutine Week (February), Burger Week (September) and Gin Week (November). C O M P L E X E C A P I T A L E H É L I C O P T È R E COMPLEXCAPITALEHELICOPTERE.COM 1 855 871-0800 Live the experience!\nQuebec City by helicopter Photo: Michel Julien VISITE 360˚ DUPLESSIS HENRI IV DUPLESSIS HENRI IV 360˚ VIEWER Originally from Lyon, France, the young chef has an impressive backstory, taking him from the Haute-Savoie of the Alps to various places in Canada, where he worked in the greatest restaurants of Québec City such as Laurie Raphaël and Bistro B. With his valuable experience, he opened his own restaurant: Chez Arvi. To create a unique gourmet experience, each course is prepared and served by different employees. “I wanted to combine two ideas: warm, friendly bistro-type cooking and gourmet cuisine, from my restaurant experience,” Masia explains. “When you come into the restaurant, the kitchen and the dining Served by the chef himself In Limoilou, innovative chef Julien Masia decided to put a different spin on things. ARVI WAS NAMED CANADA’S BEST NEW RESTAURANT IN 2019 BY ENROUTE MAGAZINE.\nJULIEN’S FAVOURITES Tora-Ya Ramen “My daughters and I regularly eat at this Japanese restaurant. The agedashi tofu (fried tofu) is one of the best things you can eat in the city!” room are in the same space. Arvi breaks down borders, and people love it! The comment we get most often is that guests have the chance to see the whole team at work.” The five-course menu gets a complete overhaul every six weeks. “We don’t really have a fixed culinary identity. We might just as easily serve a steamed bun as a classic dish. Essentially, we choose three products to highlight that work in harmony. They’re usually products from Québec, of course!” Photo: Ville de Québec (market) 76 A wonderland for foodies The perfect destinations for those who love to eat… and those who love to eat well!\nwww.quebecregiongourmande.com More than enough to taste our region!\n@quebecregiongourmande @quebecregiongourmande Jonathan Robert What to do? — Local Food The Grand Marché de Québec, which takes its cue from well-known markets around the world, and the Galeries Gourmandes, which bring together Québec's artisans and merchants, put local products in the spotlight, in bright and friendly atmospheres. Visitors will get the chance to discover gourmet treasures and meet the local farmers, processors and merchants who care deeply about sharing the culinary culture of the Québec City area. Make sure you’ve got a big shopping bag, and let your taste buds be your guide!\nGrand Marché de Québec 77 Arnaud Marchand, the chef and co-owner of Chez Boulay, a boreal bistro in Old Québec, explains the influence of local products on his cooking this way: “I don’t think there’s really a common definition of boreal cuisine. Each chef has an interpretation. Given my background in gourmet restaurants, I offer French cuisine that’s adapted for local products and the The boreal forest: our pantry Even though he’s from France, Chef Arnaud Marchand loves boreal cuisine, so called for the use of ingredients from our northern location.\nARNAUD’S FAVOURITES Old Québec and the Petit-Champlain “In my mind, Old Québec and the Petit-Champlain neighbourhood are where it’s at. They’re incredibly charming! Even for me, and I live in Lac-Beauport, walking in the area with my kids is something special.” particularities of northern ingredients.” As an example, he explains how they flavour blanquette de veau (with Québec-grown veal, of course) with sweet gale rather than cloves. “You can only find sweet gale here. It gives the dish an authentic, local touch.” Both on the Chez Boulay menu and at his nearby Comptoir Boréal pastry shops, the chef expresses his creativity through ingenious yet delectable substitutes: organic canola, camelina or hemp oil replaces olive oil, wild berry vinegar or verjus subs for lemon. He also highlights the province’s berries, like black currant and haskap, by following the seasonal harvests. “We’ve had the sea buckthorn pie, much like a lemon meringue pie, on our menu for eight years, and I still love it!” 3 LOCAL SPICES TO TRY LOVAGE ROOT GREEN ALDER PEPPER BALSAM POPLAR Your hosts Close to the Château Frontenac 38, Saint-Louis Street (Old Quebec) RESERVATIONS 418 692-0341 www.restaurantparmesan.com info@restaurantparmesan.com ITALIAN AND FRENCH SPECIALTIES Homemade pasta, veal and seafood FREE VALET PARKING MAJOR CREDIT CARDS ACCEPTED The place where everything is special Every night… la Dolce Vita Luigi et Cesare 78 What to do? — Local Food Honō Izakaya “A well-executed Japanese restaurant with reasonable prices and a welcoming feel in a simple, pleasing décor.” The top choices of… Rémy Charest, wine writer and commentator Between the tasty yakitoris and the carefully prepared cocktails, we always find happiness at Honō Izakaya.\n3 LOCAL SPICES TO TRY LOVAGE ROOT GREEN ALDER PEPPER BALSAM POPLAR Your hosts Close to the Château Frontenac 38, Saint-Louis Street (Old Quebec) RESERVATIONS 418 692-0341 www.restaurantparmesan.com info@restaurantparmesan.com ITALIAN AND FRENCH SPECIALTIES Homemade pasta, veal and seafood FREE VALET PARKING MAJOR CREDIT CARDS ACCEPTED The place where everything is special Every night… la Dolce Vita Luigi et Cesare Honō Izakaya Kraken Cru “My favourite place in Québec City for oysters, with its open-minded and slightly crazy kitchen and some good wine.” Le Pied Bleu “The most generous restaurant in Québec City for portions, but also for its spirit of excess that overflows with joyfulness.” 79 Mixology madness As with many of the world’s capital cities, Québec has plunged into the cocktail culture. Mixologists experiment with creating tasty cocktails from local alcohols and other ingredients. They’re the perfect accompaniment during cocktail hour or a night out with friends!\nCoffee shop by day, the Maelstrøm Saint-Roch dons its evening attire to ply cocktails at what many call the best cocktail bar in the city. In the same neighbourhood is the festive Deux22 and its interesting “bar and boutique” concept, where you can enjoy tacos and cocktails on a heated terrace, even in winter. Also nearby, Québec meets Japan at Honō Izakaya, a Japanese pub where what’s in your glass is just as interesting as what’s on your plate. Over in Saint-Sauveur, the place to be is Chez Tao!, with its combination of Vietnamese-inspired street food and an impressive cocktail menu in a fun atmosphere. In Upper Town, L’Atelier is your best bet; the city’s first cocktail bar is still its most innovative!\nDon’t overlook hotel bars! The fabulous decor at the Le SAM Bistro at the Fairmont Le Château Frontenac is as impressive as the cocktails mixed by the reputed mixologists. The warm atmosphere at Bar Artefact in the Auberge Saint-Antoine will inspire you to raise a glass to the city. Cheers!\nThe return of gin Micro-distilleries are also making strides in the city. Craft distilleries like Stadaconé (in Limoilou) and Vice & Vertu (Saint-Augustin-de-Desmaures) make gins based on local ingredients. Jam Le SAM cocktail from Le SAM Bistro located in the Fairmont Le Château Frontenac Photo: André-Olivier Lyra (glass) 80 Fairmont Le Château Frontenac Content Partnership Renowned for its hotel, the Fairmont Le Château Frontenac is also acclaimed for its food. The “most photographed hotel in the world” invites visitors to a completely singular experience, whether it’s for Sunday brunch, afternoon tea or a romantic supper. Fairmont Le Château Frontenac: A variety of choices Photo: Michel Roy (chandelier) The charm of the setting, the staff’s attentiveness and the attention to detail are apparent everywhere, including on the plate. Drawing on regional knowledge and local, seasonal products, the emblematic hotel offers a diverse selection of restaurants. “Would you like a spot of tea?” Rediscover afternoon tea, now more contemporary and popular than ever, at Place Dufferin.\nHip and creative, the ever-changing Le Sam always has a few surprises in store for guests: the chefs’ choices change every day, and sometimes depending on the time of day!\nA visit to the Fairmont Le Château Frontenac is also perfect for all kinds of celebrations. For an unforgettable evening, the Champlain restaurant makes any event special with Chef Stéphane Modat’s inspired new takes on tradition.\nFeel like staying longer? Book yourself a dreamy night in one of the Château’s 610 luxurious rooms. 1, rue des Carrières 418-692-3861 fairmont.com/frontenac OUR VERY BEST Stéphane Modat, chef of our restaurants Champlain and Le SAM, was named chef of the year at the Lauriers of Québec gastronomy gala in 2019.\nBon appetit!\n83 Content Partnership A steady flow of beer production is nothing new in this city! Early on in New France, the clergymen had the honour of filling the first vats. These days, microbreweries are giving new life to this time-honoured industry through the fresh talent of new beer-makers, who are excited to incorporate local ingredients, like blueberries, maple, pumpkin, Labrador tea and more. 1 Build your own beer route in downtown, or take a road trip to Portneuf, Côte-de-Beaupré, the Jacques-Cartier region or Île d’Orléans. Check out our sections on the local regions and our neighbourhood maps on pages 222 to 231 to plan a winning itinerary!\n2 Dive into the world of beer on a guided tour of local microbreweries with Broue-Tours, for example, and meet passionate beer-makers! Or create your own circuit using the “Je bois local” passport that you’ll get stamped at each microbrewery you visit.\n3 Attend one of the many annual beer festivals, such as Festibière in mid-August, the Grand Cru Brassicole in April, 100% IPA in May, the Festival des brasseurs et artisans in late August, the Foire brassicole du quartier Saint-Sauveur in October, the Winter Warmer in February or the Foire brassicole de la Jacques-Cartier in August. 3 ways to find the best brews The bubbling microbrewery scene The city has over 20 microbreweries, from downtown to nearby areas, with more on their way!\nPhoto: Valérie Busque (beer) 84 Portofino Content Partnership Portofino: The very best of Italy It’s party time at Portofino! It’s always lively at this popular restaurant, but this year is particularly special as it’s the Italian mainstay’s 25th anniversary. A quarter of a century of putting Italy on a plate and making customers’ taste buds sing! Now that’s worth celebrating!\nDifferent!\nA few years ago, a second Portofino opened its doors. Whether you visit the hip city-chic Sainte-Foy address or the historical Old Québec spot, both locations offer the same refined menu, in very distinct atmospheres. Lodged in a historic home built in 1760, the Old Québec bistro is warm and inviting, with its brick walls, Ferrari lounge and Italian-designed wood oven. This is where it all began 25 years ago, recalls the restaurant’s founder, François Petit.\nA quarter of a century later, the place hasn’t lost a bit of its charm. More relevant than ever, it still draws crowds. Its perfect location makes it an ideal spot for exploring the city and its attractions, either before or after your meal. What should you eat? At Portofino, the food is sunny and delicious, and has been adapted to local tastes. Veal, fish, seafood, pasta and pizza make up the backbone of a full menu. Make a reservation online to guarantee a table!\nFrançois Petit, Owner LOTS OF PIZZA! Over the last 25 years, Portofino has served more than 450,000 pizzas!\n965, route de l’Église Sainte-Foy 418-657-8888 portofino.ca 54, rue Couillard Vieux-Québec 418-692-8888 portofino.ca ans 87 Content Partnership IN THE HEART OF THE ACTION, ALL YEAR ROUND Located directly on the mountain, the Château is the perfect place to enjoy the attractions, landscapes and leisure activities that our beautiful region has to offer in every season.\nC O N T A C T U S 4 1 8 . 8 2 7 . 1 8 6 2 | C H A T E A U M S A . C O M Family Fun Perfect moments Holidays are all about having fun with our families and creating lasting memories. It just so happens that Québec City offers loads of activities to keep the little ones entertained! Tobbogan Slide Au 1884 Dufferin Terrace Photo: Catherine Côté Plan a family getaway Québec City is the ideal destination to fill those family photo albums with unforgettable memories.\nLearning is fun!\nBring school learning to life by introducing your children to archeology, history and art through the Jeunes explorateurs program. This intern-for-a-day program gives children under 11 free entry to the Musée national des beaux-arts du Québec, the Musée de la civilisation, the Fortifications of Québec National Historic Site and the Saint-Louis Forts and Châteaux National Historic Site. Round off your historical tour with the Citadelle of Québec, Artillery Park, the Plains of Abraham Museum, the Îlot des Palais museum, the Huron-Wendat Museum and a guided tour of the Château-Frontenac. You’ll meet illustrious figures who shaped history. Take a little detour to the Hôtel-de-Ville public square to join in the laughing, singing and all around amusement with the street performers. End your day with a picnic where everyone can share their favourites from the day! Set out on an adventure Hop on your bike to discover the Québec City area on the safe, paved cycling paths that criss-cross the region. If it’s hot, instantly please the kids by taking them to the splash parks near the Promenade Samuel-De Champlain linear park or at the City Hall gardens. QUÉBEC CITY IS RANKED 3RD SAFEST CITY IN THE WORLD.\nPhotos: Stéphane Audet Photo (two boys, family), François Gamache (fountain), Jeff Frenette Photography (tubes) Promenade Samuel-De Champlain Place Royale Fontaine de Tourny 90 PREMIUM ENTERTAINMENT IN QUEBEC CITY! F UN T O THE M AX FASHION. FOOD. FUN.\nMPG_0919_GuideTouristique_ANGLAIS.pdf 2 19-10-25 15:54 What to do? — Family Fun Thrill-seeking teens will love the double zipline across the Montmorency Falls or the mind-blowing Air Canyon ride at Canyon Sainte-Anne. Village Vacances Valcartier is a guaranteed hit with families all year long! In winter, the site becomes a gigantic, snow-covered playground, with snow tubing and rafting. In summer, it’s time for waterslides and the wave pool!\nA regional gem, the Parc national de la Jacques-Cartier offers free entry to all children aged 17 and under. Take the whole family on a rafting or tubing trip down the river for an exciting floating expedition!\nVillage Vacances Valcartier 91 They’ll love the Au 1884 winter slide on the Dufferin Terrace, with its incredible view of the river. Hold onto your hat, though! With speeds reaching 70 km/hr, this slide promises an exhilarating ride!\nLace up your ice skates and try out the cheerful free skating rink at Place D’Youville, in the middle of the Old Québec bustle. Since the rink is refrigerated, you can skate starting in mid-November. For a completely unique excursion in nature, visit the Vallée Secrète gnome forest, designed to dazzle young and old. Do one of the 12 treasure hunts they’ve set up in the heart of the beautiful Portneuf area. Wonder and magic are guaranteed!\nA happening place!\nHere are three major events for fun at any age!\n1 Every summer, the Grands Feux Loto-Québec puts on six huge fireworks shows to enjoy from the riverbanks. 2 In February, families flock to the Québec Winter Carnaval for night parades, snow sculptures, the canoe races on ice and Bonhomme’s Ice Palace. 3 The Flip Fabrique circus troupe always has a few surprises in store in its free outdoor shows.\nMake some rosy cheeks Québec City is made for winter. Bundle the kids up and get out into the snowflakes!\nPhoto: Pierre Soulard (children) Speaking of wonder, the Hôtel de glace (ice hotel) will knock your socks off! This ephemeral work of art at Saint-Gabriel de Valcartier is made entirely of ice and snow. And no, it isn’t heated! The temperature is kept at -5°C (23°F). But yes, you can sleep there! You better go soon, because it will be gone by springtime!\n92 The Méga Parc at the Galeries de la Capitale mall boasts 18 attractions, including roller coasters and a Ferris wheel, that will please the whole family. Or maybe you’d prefer plunging into the underwater world of the Aquarium du Québec to see some 10,000 marine specimens from 300 different species and walk through the majestic Grand Océan tunnel.\nOr get suited up for fun at Bora Parc, an indoor water park with slides, a wave pool, a lazy river and a surfing wave!\nThe little ones can burn off their energy at the Récréofun or Carie Factory indoor amusement centres. Bounce to your heart’s content at iSaute, a vast trampoline park for everyone. Rekindle childhood joy with a visit to Benjo, a sensational toy store in Québec City!\nFickle Mother Nature?\nNobody can predict the weather ahead of time, but Québec City offers a number of indoor activities that families will love. Photos: André-Olivier Lyra (left), Emmanuel Coveney/Québec City Business Destination (right) THE TOP CHOICES OF… CAROLINE DÉCOSTE Community manager, foodie and mom to two little ones Nina pizza napolitaine “Everything at Nina’s is perfect: the pizza—which is as good as in Naples— the warm welcome, the toys and crayons to keep the kids busy. It’s awesome!” La Salsa “The whole family loves the sun-filled cuisine from El Salvador and Mexico, right in the heart of our neighborhood.\nThe kids love the horchata, especially on the terrace in summer.” The Billig “Eating crêpes for supper is dreamworthy for my family, including me!” Favourite family restaurants At these four friendly restaurants, kids and parents will find exactly what they want, with varied tastes accounted for.\nSpag & Tini Families are given the royal treatment at this welcoming Italian restaurant in Petit-Champlain. Best thing: the allergen-free options.\nChez Boub This fantastic number is located in Lac-Beauport, and it has its own playroom! Best thing: drinking your hot coffee while watching the kids play.\nPaillard Pop into this lovely bakery and sandwich shop in Old Québec for a quick lunch. Best thing: the children’s menu with milk and a sweet treat. Cochon Dingue All four locations of this Parisian bistro welcome families in style, especially for brunch. Best thing: the attentive service and the menu you can draw on. CERTIFIED FAMILY-FRIENDLY Downtown businesses with the “Certifié famille” sticker posted are easily accessible for strollers, have high chairs or booster seats, and allow parents to bottle-feed or breastfeed their children on-site without making a purchase. It’s great!\nCochon Dingue 94 Delta Hotels by Marriott Québec Content Partnership Delta Hotels by Marriott Québec: Complete comfort Planning a busy, activity-filled family trip to Québec City? Do like happy customers from the world over and choose the comfort and accessibility of Delta Hotels by Marriott Québec, your home base for excursions into the city or farther afield. This luxurious hotel near downtown Québec City offers fully furnished, spacious, elegant rooms and suites, and the same high-quality services you’d find in larger hotels. And it’s right in the centre of the city’s main tourist attractions and interesting sights. If you feel like getting active, try out our fitness centre during your stay, then hop into the heated outdoor pool to swim a few laps (it’s open all year!). After a day spent strolling through beautiful Québec City, nothing hits the spot better than a delicious meal at our Le Bistro restaurant. You’ll feel taken care of, with a flavourful meal and an unmatched holiday atmosphere. Newly renovated with Québec-grown maple to create a luxurious feel, Le Bistro has something for everyone. From breakfast to late night, its menu changes throughout the day. And you’ll always enjoy an excellent view of the Laurentian Mountains. High quality SECRET PASSAGEWAY An underground passageway connects the hotel to the Québec City Convention Centre. 690, boul. René-Lévesque Est 418-647-1717 marriott.com/yqbdr Photos: Stéphanie de la Ronde (pool), ABLSON Photography (restaurant) 97 Content Partnership Shopping Local Shops A little window shopping is a must when visiting Québec City. With the number of stores it offers, here is an exercise as commendable as it is pleasant!\nPetit-Champlain Photos: Jeff Frenette Photography (left), Francis Gagnon (couple winter), Francis Fontaine (couple), André-Olivier Lyra (Rue St-Jean) The Petit-Champlain neighbourhood The oldest commercial district on the continent will draw you into its stores filled with colourful art and its refined designer clothing and handicraft boutiques. Stroll along the pedestrian walkways while admiring the lovely windows of ancestral homes and drinking in the area’s romantic atmosphere. Come winter, the snow brings a sense of magic, with the holiday decorations and lights topping off the wonderland feel. The Old Port and Place-Royale The Old Port’s straight streets and venerable architecture are infused with history. In this charming neighbourhood, along Saint-Paul and Saint-Pierre streets, you’ll enjoy visiting the many antique stores, craft shops and art galleries. It’s easy to fall under its spell and just want to linger. The art of shopping If you love to shop, there are plenty of excellent opportunities in the area. Be prepared to fill your bags to bursting!\nRue Saint-Jean You’ll fall head over heels for the European charm of Rue Saint-Jean! Just outside the fortifications, you’ll encounter a dense area of lovely original shops, handicraft boutiques and fine-food purveyors. Within the walls, the more touristic side offers a host of clothing and souvenir stores. It’s the perfect place to poke around and find a few unique items!\n99 What to do? — Shopping Québec Shopping!\n1 destination, 2 shopping centers, 400 trendsetting shops & discounts of up to 20 %.\nplacestefoy.com | laurierquebec.com * Certain conditions apply. See details : visitorvip.com The Arts District Wander down Avenue Cartier in the Arts District to check out around one hundred businesses in this less touristy area. You’ll adore the lively atmosphere, gourmet food marts and hip specialty stores. Take a look upward to marvel at the giant streetlamps decked out in artwork by important Québec artists!\nSaint-Roch neighbourhood The more contemporary Saint-Roch district is worth a visit for its designer boutiques and trendy shops along Rue Saint-Joseph. You’ll find pretty much anything your heart desires: CDs, unusual gifts, vintage treasures, books, cookware, beauty products, video games, jewellery, toys, musical instruments and more!\nShopping centres Three malls are located near Québec City’s bridges: Laurier Québec, Place de la Cité and Place Ste-Foy. Together, they offer a range of options: popular stores, luxury brands, gourmet grocers, bookstores, specialty boutiques and more.\nThe Galeries de la Capitale is an exceptional family destination. The mall’s Méga Parc offers an indoor ice-skating rink, amusement park rides, an arcade and the country’s largest IMAX screen. Foodies will love the Galeries gourmandes food court. The Fleur de Lys mall houses 200 stores and services under one roof and is only five minutes from downtown Québec City, not far from the Videotron Center and the Grand Marché.\n100 Take a deep breath and ENJOY THE EXPERIENCE | AirCANYON | 3 suspension bridges | Spectacular waterfall | Via Ferrata | Complete forest immersion | Accessible to all FREE PARKING RESTAURANT GIFT SHOP PICNIC AREAS PET FRIENDLY ACCESSIBLE TRAILS N A T U R A L LY SENSATIONAL!\ncanyonsa.qc.ca 418 827-4057 30 minutes from Old Québec To admire A piece from an art gallery or on the Rue du Trésor, or some blown glass, a wooden sculpture, a work in copper repoussé or a religious object from Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré. To wear Fur garments, local designers’ pieces, handcrafted jewellery, leather or wool accessories, Indigenous items like the famous traditional moccasins, all made locally. What to bring home with you?\nHere are just some of the prizes you’ll want as truly meaningful souvenirs. To give Antiques from Rue Saint-Paul, a novel by a local author, a ceinture fléchée (traditional woven belt), a Je me souviens licence plate, a coffee table book on the city.\nTo taste Maple products, apple- or berry-based alcohol, beer, boreal spices and more.\nPhoto: Amimoc (moccassin) 102 We are waiting for you! C H O I C E H O T E L S C A N A D A T M Quality Suites Québec 1600 rue Bouvier Québec, Qc, G2K 1N8 418-622-4244 Quebecqualitysuites.com Comfort Inn Aéroport Est 1255 Autoroute Duplessis L’Ancienne-Lorette, Qc, G2G 2B4 418-872-5900 Anciennelorettecomfortinn.com After a good night sleep in one of our comfortable and functional room, enjoy our delicious free hot breakfast as well as the benefits to be a Choice Privileges member. A gentle way to start the day.\nThe queen of epicurean delights Founded in 1871, J.A. Moisan is the oldest grocery store in North America! Inside you’ll find a vast selection of Québec-made products, including preserves, cheeses and charcuterie. Stepping into this store on Rue Saint-Jean is like taking a trip back in time. Wonder is for everyone Located in the Saint-Roch neighbourhood, the Benjo toy store seems sprinkled with fairy dust. Just a peek behind the child-sized front door is enough to make your eyes widen as if looking into Santa’s workshop itself! Little ones can wind through the store on an electric train, their brains whirring with possibilities! 100% made in Québec From toys to fashion, fine foods and arts and crafts, here are four stores whose local expertise has earned them a well-deserved reputation. More than 100 years of passion La Maison Simons knows fashion. After all, it’s been in business since 1840! This family-owned Canadian retailer with five generations of history has always been on the cutting edge of trends for women, men and the home. Its store in Old Québec was the very first to open, and is a must for anyone with an eye for fashion!\nLocal talent on display The Boutique des métiers d’art holds court in the heart of Place-Royale, offering impressive works by more than 100 professional Québec craftspeople and artists. The store abounds with talent, from ceramics, jewellery, stained glass and woodwork, to leather work, paper art, weaving, screen prints, sculptures, knitted goods, paintings, textile items and more!\nPhotos: André-Olivier Lyra Benjo La Maison Simons 104 F_CuivreDartAlbertGilles_DEMI-Guide touristique2020-ANG.pdf 1 19-11-12 16:24 Craft shows You’ll find the most unique gifts because they’re made by hand, by a craftsperson you can meet! Put some Québec City under the tree The best places for holiday shopping!\nThe Petit-Champlain neighbourhood This is the best place to find those special gems in a Christmas village setting where the snow, lights and architecture conspire to create romance and magic.\nChristmas markets Discover more than 1,000 local products at the Grand Marché du Québec. Or experience authentic European ambiance by visiting the German Christmas market and its wooden booths. Just two of many!\nThe Boutique de Noël Feast your eyes on the bounty of decorations, nativity scenes, Christmas villages, collectables and souvenirs at this Old Québec landmark. It’s open year-round!\nNothing is more charming than doing your holiday shopping in the fairytale setting of Québec City.\n105 What to do? — Shopping Photo : Pierre Soulard ONLY 15 MINUTES FROM OLD QUÉBEC CITY Cable car | Suspension bridge | Panoramic stairway Manoir’s restaurant and terrace | Via ferrata | Zipline A NATURAL SHOW PARC DE LA CHUTE-MONTMORENCY 1 844-522-4883 sepaq.com/chutemontmorency summer Loads of fun and festivities In summer, Québec City comes alive with an array of exciting activities. On sunny days, wander the cobbled streets of Old Québec and stop to enjoy a microbrew on a patio. If you prefer to bask in nature, there’s nothing better than a trip to Parc national de la Jacques-Cartier, Parc de la Chute-Montmorency, Île d’Orléans or the Portneuf region. There are so many options to choose from!\nPhoto: Melissa Guerette Les Capitales de Québec Mid-May to September Bring the whole family out for a ball game and hot dogs! Enjoy a fun-filled baseball game featuring Les Capitales de Québec at the Canac Stadium in Québec City. Wendake International Pow Wow Late June This is the perfect opportunity to discover traditional First Nations dances, as dancers from near and far gather to compete! You can also try handicraft workshops, watch demonstrations of traditional skills and sample authentic Indigenous foods.\nQUÉBEC CITY: VIBRANT ALL SUMMER LONG!\nDéfi Escaliers Québec End of June Participants will cover all the staircases that connect Québec City’s upper and lower towns. Alternately climbing one staircase and descending the next, participants get to run about 30 staircases and a total of more than 3,000 steps.\nImagine Van Gogh End of June to mid-September Québec City Convention Centre will be host to the spectacular immersive exhibition dedicated to the works of Vincent Van Gogh. An invitation to wander through more than 200 paintings, projected in giant proportions. Passages Insolites Late June to mid-October For this free event, Les Passages Insolites invites professional artists and architectural groups to create unusual and surprising artwork on sidewalks, walls and other urban features in the Old Port, Place Royale, and the Petit-Champlain and Saint-Roch neighbourhoods.\nLes Passages Insolites Photos: Jasmin Brochu (fireworks), Stéphane Bourgeois (clown), André-Olivier Lyra (FEQ) 108 Fête nationale (Québec Day) on the Plains of Abraham End of June A large number of free festivities and activities are organized, from morning to evening, for locals and visitors on many different locations in Québec City, as well as a great show on the Plains of Abraham. Festival d’été de Québec Festival d’été de Québec (FEQ) Early July The FEQ hosts a star-studded line-up of international and emerging artists on indoor and outdoor stages. It’s Canada’s largest outdoor musical event, involving 1,000 artists and 300 shows in 10 venues over 11 days!\nThe Transat Québec Saint-Malo Mid-July The Transat Québec Saint-Malo is the oldest west-to-east, nonstop, trans-Atlantic, crewed race. Every four years since 1984, ocean-going racers speed along the St. Lawrence River between Québec City and Lévis, to face the challenge of sailing its hazards, currents and tides.\nNovena Mid-July Over the nine days before Saint Anne’s Feast Day on July 26, pilgrims and the faithful from around the world converge on the Shrine of Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré for the Novena. This time of grace is marked by a torchlight procession, preaching, singing, music and celebrations.\nQuébec City Day Early July Québec City celebrates 412 years! Everyone can celebrate together; a number of free activities will take place!\nCanada Day in Québec City Early July A great line-up of free activities and festivities will be on offer at two great Québec City locations: Dufferin Terrace and the Plains of Abraham. There will be activities, ceremonies, entertainment and performances all day long. And be sure to stick around for the nighttime fireworks display!\nFlip Fabrique Mid-July Flip Fabrique’s outdoor circus show is back with an all-new concept! For the fourth time, this grandiose event starring world-calibre circus artists is being offered to the public free of charge. Plein Art Late July to early August Over 120 professional artists and artisans from Québec come together to showcase their handcrafted items. Plein Art is the summer’s most inspiring event for fans of the creative arts and for the general public!\nIn Québec City, we love to celebrate sunny summer days!\n109 What to do? — Summer Grands Feux Loto-Québec August From the bank of the St. Lawrence River, enjoy the impressive fireworks of the Grands Feux Loto-Québec, set against the beautiful backdrop of Québec City.\nVélirium – Mountain Bike Festival and UCI Mountain Bike World Cup Mid-August Over 800 athletes representing 50 countries compete for a UCI Mountain Bike World Cup. Several other amateur competitions, long-distance raids, kids’ races and shows make Vélirium the ultimate mountain bike festival! ComediHa! Fest-Québec Mid-August This is THE comedy event in Québec City! Festival-goers can see a whole array of indoor and outdoor shows by comedians from here and elsewhere. The New France Festival Early August Travel through time to 17th and 18th centuryNorth Americaunder French rule. The authentic setting of Old Québec comes alive with many performances, as well as gourmet activities, talks, historical recreations and period costumes! Vélirium Festival d’opéra de Québec Late July to early August Music lovers get an opportunity to spend two weeks with exceptional musical creators and artists, to enjoy an array of events showcasing local and up-and-coming artists. Festibière de Québec Mid-August The Festibière beer festival is for both beer connoisseurs and newbies, with over 500 beers from about 50 microbreweries available for sampling! Festibière hosts talks by brewing industry experts and performances that will appeal to a whole range of tastes.\nPhotos: Sven Martin (Vélirium), Les Festifs (Festibière) It's in Québec City's DNA to celebrate music, play sports year‑round and get together for drinks!\nFestibière de Québec 110 C M Y CM MY CY CMY K zipline adventures for kids and adults - CHAUVEAU -arbraska.com 1 877 886-5500 - for ages 5 and up -find us at Quebec City’s Parc Chauveau REALLY REALLY FL Y Fêtes Gourmandes Desjardins de Neuville Late August This gourmet festival showcases Neuville’s renowned sweet corn along with other local produce from the Portneuf and Québec City regions. It also features artists and artisans from across the province.\nRaid Bras-du-Nord Late August This super-popular mountain-biking marathon will be back in August with new circuits! You won’t want to miss it!\nPhoto: Jeff Frenette Photography Did you know that... Neuville's corn is so unique in the world that it has its own protected designation of origin?\n112 zipline adventures for kids and adults - CHAUVEAU -arbraska.com 1 877 886-5500 - for ages 5 and up -find us at Quebec City’s Parc Chauveau REALLY REALLY FL Y The True North Singletrack Paradise quebec-cite.com/mountainbiking #RideQuebecCity Étienne Dionne In town Québec City has a whole network of bike paths branching out in every direction from downtown. Saddle up and discover the city, the outskirts and its greenbelt!\nTwo-wheeling! 2 Corridor du Littoral 50 kilometres (31 miles) From Bassin Louise (Old Port), pedal along a mostly flat route bordering the St. Lawrence River to the Montmorency Falls.\n3 Vélopiste Jacques-Cartier/ Portneuf 68 kilometres (36 miles) Starting at the Corridor des Cheminots bike path, explore the area along this former railway track, from Saint-Gabriel-de-Valcartier to Rivière-à-Pierre. In every part of the region, you’re spoiled for choice in terms of bicycle touring, mountain biking and bike paths.\n3 paths to discover 1 Corridor des Cheminots 22 kilometres (14 miles) Starting from Domaine de Maizerets, explore the residential neighbourhoods of the northern suburbs, all the way to Wendake.\n114 Mont-Sainte-Anne (MSA) MSA is a fantastic mountain biking destination, thanks to its variety of cross-country and downhill trails, some with spectacular views of the St. Lawrence River, and its facilities for the whole family (pump track, playground, beginner enduro track and forest parkour circuit). Sentiers du Moulin If you want to combine a ride with another activity, opt for this biking area close to downtown. You’ll find a network of fun, technically challenging trails, several of them on the mountainside. Empire 47 Most of this mountain bike trail network, in Lac Delage and in Stoneham-et-Tewkesbury, is open to beginners, but there are also a few expert-level trails, all in a majestic setting.\nQUÉBEC SINGLETRACK EXPÉRIENCE In early August, Mont-Sainte-Anne hosts Eastern North America’s only mountain bike stage race: 6 stages, 6 days, 6 exceptional destinations, plus 6 timed descents (Enduro) and 6 timed ascents (King of the Mountain). Now, that’s a mountain of a challenge! In nature Less than an hour from downtown, you’ll find several mountain biking options that will put your skills—and legs— to the test!\nVallée Bras-du-Nord In Saint-Raymond, experience majestic vistas as you ride through fields and over mountains on short, rolling trails and on other, sometimes more technical, singletracks. After a great ride, kick back at the on-site microbrewery.\nBeginners may prefer the beautiful rolling scenery of the Shannahan area, which has few steep uphills, and a series of long, narrow, fast trails with gorgeous riverside stretches. Photos: Ville de Québec (left), Philippe Jobin (bike, river), Olivier Croteau (Mont-Sainte-Anne) Mont-Sainte-Anne 115 What to do? — Summer Beaches There’s a beach just five minutes from downtown! You’ll find tons to do at Baie de Beauport: swim or sunbathe, or get active with a canoe, kayak, rabaska, catamaran, windsurf board or paddle board. And the kids will have a great time at their very own playground with water games.\nThe Base de plein air Sainte-Foy is a large, easily accessible recreation centre to enjoy nature just steps away from the city. Swim at the beach or rent kayaks, pedal boats and canoes.\nLac Saint-Joseph in Duchesnay has a nice little sandy beach just perfect for playing and lounging. Access it with a daily pass.\nPools and spray parks On a hot summer day, the whole family will enjoy time at one of the city’s spray parks: at the Aquarium du Québec, on Promenade Samuel-De Champlain, at Place des Canotiers in the Old Port, or at the city hall gardens. Baie de Beauport Cool off! Beaches, swimming and spray parks—find an oasis to make the most of the hot weather! Most public outdoor pools open on June 23. Located in the city centre along the Saint-Charles River, Marina Saint-Roch offers a swimming pool and a lively area the whole family will enjoy.\nYou can also cool off at Village Vacances Valcartier, which has 35 water slides, 2 river rides and a massive wave pool. Little ones can slide, splash around and generally get soaked at the Cockadoodle Farm, Pirates Hideout and Mirage water games.\nRivers Chill out at Canots Légaré, a calm 20-kilometre (12.5-mile) stretch of the Saint-Charles River where you can canoe, kayak or stand-up paddleboard.\nParc nautique de Cap-Rouge is dedicated to recreational boating. Explore the St. Lawrence and Cap-Rouge rivers aboard a canoe, kayak, sailboat or deck boat.\nPhotos: Jeff Frenette Photography (beach), Sam St Onge (Lapin Sauté), Alice Chiche (portrait), Les Festifs (Cours arrière du Festibière) 116 La Barberie La Barberie brews and bottles its own varieties of top-quality beer that’s best enjoyed on the patio after lunch. Decked out with the work of the Urbainsculteurs horticultural artists, this Saint-Roch terrace is the best place to get a taste of the good life!\nLa Cour arrière du Festibière An oasis in the heart of the Old Port! On weekends, enjoy the live music acts at a pool/terrace that’s beloved by craft beer fans. La Cour arrière is the most refreshing terrace in town. Le Lapin Sauté Tucked in the shade of the adjacent park, this terrace in the Petit-Champlain neighbourhood looks tiny under an enormous cliff. The whole place takes on the personality of the rock face, which gives off a subtle fresh breeze. You have to experience it to really get the full effect.\nPanache Mobile at Vignoble Ste-Pétronille Just the scenery around this food truck is worth the visit to Île d’Orléans! Overlooking the vines and the Montmorency Falls, Panache Mobile’s terrace is the most highly coveted view according to local gourmets. L’Orygine With its partial view of the St. Lawrence River, its giant parasols and its many heaters available in cooler weather, the patio at L’Orygine bistro, in the Petit-Champlain neighbourhood, rises to the top as the loveliest patio in Québec City.\n1 2 3 4 5 La Cour arrière du Festibière behind the Café du Monde.\nLapin Sauté 5 of the best patios in the region The patios of Québec City’s bars and restaurants are some of the best places to revel in summer. Here are five where you can build unforgettable memories. By Allison Van Rassel, food journalist 117 What to do? — Summer Vertically You don’t need to be a great athlete to enjoy canyoning, which is rappelling down and along a waterfall. Give it a try at Jean-Larose Falls at Mont-Sainte-Anne. And Vallée Bras-du-Nord features four canyoning sites (from intermediate to expert). Will you risk getting wet? Descending At Parc de la Chute-Montmorency, jump into the void and go whizzing over the falls on a zipline, solo or with a friend. Or try the via ferrata. Either one is a guaranteed adrenaline rush! At Canyon Sainte-Anne, you can cross one of three suspension bridges over the deep canyon, then hop on the Air Canyon or try one of the via ferrata adventures. Breathtaking!\nFloating The Québec City area is full of waterways. Let the current carry you downriver through the stunning scenery.\nTry paddle boarding (SUP), kayaking or canoeing on a lake or valley river, or on the majestic St. Lawrence River.\n1 2 3 T he area sports lots of rivers and outfitters where you can fish for brook trout and many other species, particularly in the Portneuf and the Jacques‑Cartier areas. See you on the green Located just minutes from Québec City are several 9- and 18-hole golf and links courses (Club de golf Royal Charbourg, Golf de la Faune, Le Grand Portneuf, Le Grand Vallon). They’re the perfect way to make sure your swing doesn’t suffer during your stay! Parc national de la Jacques-Cartier 3 ways to get some fresh air in summer Photos: Jeff Frenette Photography (left), Francis Fontaine (right) DID YOU KNOW THAT THERE ARE WHALE WATCHING EXCURSIONS DEPARTING FROM QUÉBEC CITY?\n118 Load up on vitamins Why not get away for the day and discover the local fruit by berry picking?\nStarting in mid-June, you can pick your own strawberries in many places in the region. Île d’Orléans grows the most well-known berries in the province! In July, it’s raspberry season. It’s a short one, so take advantage of it while the berries are ripe and tasty. Pure delight!\nAnd finally, in August, the blueberries are ready. This iconic Québec fruit is a favourite of young and old. Enjoy them as you pick, but don’t eat them all! 119 What to do? — Summer PORT OF DEPARTURE OR FINAL DESTINATION Canada & New England Cruises May to October quebec-cite.com/cruises Mathieu Dupuis A colour explosion! Fall is undoubtedly the best time for nature lovers to visit Québec City. In late September and early October, the leaves burst into colour! The brilliant shades of orange, yellow and red create a spectacular show you’ll long remember.\nfall Photo: Steve Deschênes Parc national de la Jacques-Cartier Rouge et Or Football August to November Laval University’s football team enjoys the support of an incredibly loyal fan base. Local supporters head to the PEPS university stadium parking lot at dawn on game days for an old-fashioned tailgate party. It's more than a football match—it’s an experience!\nQuébec City Pride Festival Early September This social and artistic festival of LGBTQ+ pride offers a colourful program of music and drag queen shows, street performers and more! Envol et Macadam Early September This festival shakes up the city, showcasing the best alternative music from here and elsewhere!\nFestival Celtique de Québec Mid-September Celebrate the culture of the Celtic peoples and learn about Québecers’ FALL: A GREAT TIME TO BE OUTDOORS Celtic heritage! This festival is jam-packed with unique and amazing activities. Québec City Film Festival Mid-September Created in 2011, the Québec City Film Festival (QCFF) offers its city and visitors a major cinematographic event. The event take place at an exceptional site where a multitude of high-quality works evolve, with a special place reserved for Québec artists and artisans.\nGrand Prix Cycliste de Québec Mid-September The world’s greatest cycling teams come to Québec City to take part in this prestigious event registered on the UCI WorldTour calendar. A must for sports fans! Saint-Roch XP Mid-September This event celebrates music, food, art and comedy, at the heart of the trendy, urban Saint-Roch neighbourhood. Rue Saint-Joseph is closed to traffic and turned into a pedestrian mall for the event. Great Colour Adventure Mid-September to mid-October Tons of activities are organized for all to enjoy as Mont-Sainte-Anne dons her colourful autumn attire. Come to the mountain, enjoy the fall colours and join in the fun!\nLes Remparts de Québec Mid-September to March The Remparts are among the candidates for the Québec Major Junior Hockey League’s top honours. Get ready for action and a fun atmosphere on game nights at the Videotron Centre!\nMégaRelais Madame Labriski Mid-September On a journey of 287 kilometres and 122 A PLAYGROUND THAT’S FULL OF LIFE • 10,000 marine animals • Encounters with seals • Animal keeper for a day package • Discovery package • Water games • Hebertism circuit for the kids 1 866 659-5264 sepaq.com/aquarium Québec en toutes lettres Mid-October This 11-day literary festival brings together over 140 authors from Québec and beyond, to promote literature through round tables, literary performances, meetings, film screenings and more.\nHalloween October Get into the Halloween spirit with the many activities organized around the city. See the frightening decorations haunting the Plains of Abraham and city hall, take a themed guided tour, go pumpkin picking or join a neighborhood party! Québec table gourmande Mid-November During this food-centric event, enjoy great meals at reduced prices, as some 30 restaurants offer a fixed-price menu starting at $25.\n24 hours, the riders cross several municipalities of the MRC of La Côte-de-Beaupré, nine munici-palities of the MRC of La Jacques-Cartier and walk several kilometres in the MRC Portneuf. They also discover several Québec City boroughs.\nSSQ Québec City Marathon Early October Close to 10,000 marathon runners from around the world take part in this challenge that criss-crosses the city’s neighbourhoods. 123 What to do? — Fall 3 must-do activities this fall Be dazzled by nature’s fall transformation! Where to go leaf peeping 1 At Parc national de la Jacques-Cartier, there is over 100 kilometres (62 miles) of hiking trails so you can explore the park’s most spectacular vistas. 2 At Mont-Sainte-Anne, the yearly Great Colour Adventure appeals to families and sports enthusiasts.\n3 At Sentier des Caps de Charlevoix, you’ll find a range of colourful forest trails with stunning views of the St. Lawrence River.\n4 Vallée Bras-du-Nord offers hiking, canoeing, kayaking, mountain biking and canyoning—all in a breathtaking setting.\nPlains of Abraham, Parc des Champs-de-Bataille Wonder at the multi-coloured landscapes As the days grow shorter, the woods gradually take on their fall colouring, culminating in an explosion of red, orange and yellow. In mid-autumn, Mother Nature generally gifts us a period of warm, sunny weather, commonly referred to as Indian Summer. It’s the perfect time for outdoor activities.\n1 Photos: Francis Fontaine (left), Julie Audet (right) 124 Observe wildlife Wildlife is rather active in the fall, so get ready to enjoy some thrilling encounters.\nIn Forêt Montmorency, join a moose observation safari to search for the largest member of the deer family.\nAt the Cap Tourmente National Wildlife Area, witness the pageantry of the greater snow goose migration.\nNorth of Saint-Charles Lake, listen closely to the hundreds of bird species that have been reported in the Marais du Nord marshes.\nEnjoy invigorating walks in the woods Fall hikes offer cooler temperatures, few mosquitos and the unmistakeable scent of earth and leaves. They’re one of the best ways to savour autumn’s spectacular colours. Lace up your hiking boots and explore. The Bras-du-Nord trail is an 8-kilometre (5-mile) hike on fairly flat terrain leading to the majestic Delaney Falls. It’s perfect for kids. The Pionniers trail on Mont-Sainte-Anne unfurls for 6 kilometres (3.7 miles) in the heart of the forest. Do the whole loop or take the gondola up. The 11-kilometre (7-mile) Les Loups trail offers breathtaking views of the stunning Jacques-Cartier Valley. Shuttle service is available to trailheads. Head out to Mount Wright and admire 300-year-old trees while hiking 5 km to reach the summit. The Mestachibo trail is a steep path of 12.5 kilometres (7.8 miles) that links Saint-Ferréol-les-Neiges to the Jean-Larose Waterfalls along the Sainte-Anne River. It is suitable for advanced hikers. And plan for transportation back. In the city, hike Parc linéraire de la Rivière-Saint-Charles, a 32-kilometre (20-mile) walking trail that goes from the Old Port to the source of the river. 2 3 What to do? — Fall GO HUNTING At Réserve faunique de Portneuf, you can hunt small game, deer, moose and even bear (permit dependent). Pourvoirie du Lac-Moreau offers high-end moose hunting experiences. 125 THE LARGEST University Teaching and Research FOREST IN THE WORLD!\nCROSS-COUNTRY SKIING • SKI-SHOEING SNOWSHOEING • FISHING • HIKING FAUNA AND FLORA OBSERVATION FORÊT MONTMORENCY Outdoors • Cafeteria Sustainable tourism Accommodation foretmontmorency.ca 418-656-2034 Go apple picking Picking apples is a fall tradition for Québecers. And it offers a great backdrop for family photos! On Île d’Orléans, wander down the quaint roads to pick apples and even squash and pumpkins. The farms also offer homemade foods made with fresh-picked fruits and vegetables from their orchards and gardens.\nHarvest grapes The region’s wineries, like the Mitan and Isle de Bacchus vineyards, offer you the opportunity to take part in the grape harvest and to learn more about vine and vino. Cheers!\nGorgeous orchards It’s not only the forests that turn brilliant green, orange, yellow and red. Colour also takes over the fields and orchards.\n126 winter The winter wonderland capital The impressive snow-covered landscapes, nearby nature and tons of fun activities offered in the city will warm your heart! In winter, there’s magic gently falling from the sky, turning this French-speaking city into something out of a fairy tale. Québec is a city of the true north and offers ideal conditions for an array of winter activities. In fact, you’ll have trouble choosing from among the many ways to enjoy the 300 centimetres (118 inches) of snow that fall on our region every year!\nPhotos: Jeff Frenette Photography (apples), Francis Gagnon (couple) Christmas markets Go to... Bask in the European atmosphere created by the wooden booths at the German Christmas Market (read more on page 130) Discover the over 1,000 gourmet Québecois foods at the Christmas market at the Grand Marché de Québec Relive the traditions of the early 20th century at the Marché du Noël d’Antan de Cap-Santé Themed tours Go to... Learn about historical facts and legends with Promenades Fantômes Find out about the Christmas traditions of yesteryear, accompanied by a historical figure from New France, with Cicérone Tours Holiday shows and Christmas services Go to... Hear the best holiday songs performed by international artists Take time to reflect in our heritage churches 5 festively decorated spots 1 Rue du Petit-Champlain and Place-Royale, with... their old-world charm and intimate ambiance, are purely romantic!\n2 Place D’Youville, with... its skating rink surrounded by illuminated buildings, is right out of a fairy tale. It’s perfect for family outings. 3 Fairmont Le Château Frontenac with… its long corridor of decorated trees and a truly regal atmosphere. 4 Grande Allée and the Parliament Building with… their myriad lights. Brilliant! 5 Avenue Cartier with... its oversized streetlights, decorated with artwork from Québec painters, casting a soft light on the snow. A cultural treat!\nCheck out our suggestions for an enchanting winter stay in Québec City! Place D’Youville For a magical holiday season 128 Family festivities Here are five original ideas for sharing the magic of Christmas with your kids!\nMeet Santa Santa has magical powers that allow him to be at various places in the city. Keep an eye out! In the picturesque Petit-Champlain neighbourhood, from late November to late December In local shopping centres (and you can do your shopping at the same time!) At the Vallée Secrète nature park in Portneuf, where you can visit a gnome forest Bask in the glow at Festilumières at the Aquarium du Québec Your little ones won’t believe their eyes in this outdoor circuit featuring illuminated animals. There are also bouncy castles for maximum fun! Participate in the Parade des jouets In early November, join the Christmas parade, with floats, marching bands, entertainers, mascots and even letter carriers to take the kids’ letters to Santa! Awaken your inner artist at our museums Immerse yourself in the world of classic storytelling at the costume workshop of the Musée de la civilization, or head to the Musée national des beaux-arts du Québec to create a family artwork and explore the Family Gallery.\nWalk through Old Québec Immerse yourself in a real-life Christmas village, where the snow, lights and European architecture create a perfect backdrop for beautiful family photos. 1 2 3 4 5 What to do? — Winter One more idea Unique in Québec City, the tobbogan slide on Dufferin terrace has existed since 1884! With four on the slide, fly down at 70 km/h with an amazing view on the river. DON’T FORGET YOUR HATS, SCARVES AND MITTENS! Did you know...\nthe holiday celebrations last several days in Québec City?\nPhotos: Yves Tessier (left), Stéphane Audet Photo (right) 129 From the end of November to the end of December, its more than 90 charming wooden booths add to the area’s irresistible charm. You’ll find artisanal products from Québec, gingerbread from Nuremberg and traditional Christstollen cake. It’s also a perfect opportunity to enjoy German specialities like bratwurst sausage and pretzels!\nEven the Grinch’s heart would melt before the beauty of the twinkling lights and the warm comfort of hot chocolate or mulled wine. The WinterBar is the perfect spot to share a drink in a European ambiance and get in the holiday spirit.\nThe kids can enjoy tons of free activities: crafts, board games, music workshops and holiday stories in the chalet, outdoor games at Kinderplatz Ravensburger and puppet shows at Theaterplatz. Good times guaranteed!\nWinter charm, Old-World style Québec City’s German Christmas Market is a unique opportunity to experience the atmosphere of a European Christmas market, right in the heart of Old Québec, in front of City Hall. The music program is also rich and varied, including musicians from Germany, an opening night performance by the choir of the Orchestre symphonique de Québec, free indoor and outdoor shows, Christmas choirs and more. You won’t want to miss a thing! Québec City’s German Christmas Market 130 Poutine Week Early February More than 60 restaurants take part in this week dedicated to poutine, the typical Québecois dish. They all hope to claim the title of best poutine in Québec City! Québec Winter Carnival February Come meet Bonhomme Carnaval at the world’s largest winter carnival! The Ice Palace, snow sculptures, ice canoe races and night parades await you, in a fun and family-friendly atmosphere.\nMois Multi February Mois Multi is a contemporary art festival with an electrifying program showcasing new forms of multidisciplinary art, like performance and electronic art. WINTER AT ITS BEST 4 reasons to celebrate New Year’s on the Grande Allée Looking for an original location to ring in the New Year? You don’t have to go far. There’s tons going on outside, right on one of Québec City’s main streets! Count down to midnight and then enjoy the spectacular fireworks Dance to a DJ and traditional music Party with friends on the heated patios of the Grande Allée Celebrate as a family on the Ferris wheel with a view of Old Québec Québec International Pee-Wee Hockey Tournament February Every year since 1960, hockey fever has gripped the city as thousands of players aged 11 and 12 come from 15 different countries to represent their teams with pride.\nPentathlon des neiges February The Pentathlon des neiges is a multi-sport challenge involving cycling, running, skiing, skating and snowshoeing. Held on the Plains of Abraham, it’s the only event of its kind in the world and is open to everyone.\nGrand Défi des Traverseux (Ice Canoe Race) March Finalists in the Québec ice canoe race circuit take to the icy St. Lawrence River for this championship event. Depending on their category, teams must cross the river up to 10 times!\nWhat to do? — Winter Photos: Francis Gagnon (couple), Emmanuel Coveney (choir) 131 Hit the slopes The area around Québec City is bursting with breathtaking scenery and exceptional snow conditions. Four ski resorts offer different experiences for every level skier. All the resorts make snow, rent equipment and have respected ski schools, food services and ski boutiques. And then there’s the legendary après ski!\nDid you know... it snows an average of 300 cm in Québec City every winter? ATTENTION SKI BUFFS!\nSki Québec Charlevoix not only offers discounts and specials for lodging, it also sells a multi-resort ticket. Snow enthusiasts can decide each morning whether they want to ski at Mont-Sainte-Anne, Stoneham or Le Massif. This ticket is only available at select hotels in Québec City and Charlevoix. skiquebeccharlevoix.com Mont-Sainte-Anne 132 Day and night skiing at Mont-Sainte-Anne Just 30 minutes from Québec City, Mont-Sainte-Anne offers exquisite snow conditions across 71 trails, 2 snow parks, 1 boardercross area and 3 cross-country ski trails, on 3 sides of the mountain. With 19 lighted trails, Mont-Sainte-Anne provides the most elevation for night skiing in the country. Take in the fantastic view of the St. Lawrence River from the gondola.\nGet up there at the Massif de Charlevoix One hour’s drive from Québec City, the Massif boasts the highest vertical drop east of the Canadian Rockies. With is vast skiable terrain and breathtaking runs that plunge right toward the river, the mountain is also known for its abundant precipitation, piling up to nearly 600 cm (20 feet) of snow on average every year. Proficient skiers will adore the nearly 100 acres of off-piste skiing. A winning trio at Stoneham Mountain Resort Just 20 minutes from the city, Stoneham proposes 43 trails, an Olympic half-pipe, 3 snow parks and 19 lighted trails, on 3 mountain slopes. This first-rate ski resort offers a selection of trails from easy to double diamond. The vibe at the Quatre-Foyers bar and the delicious meals at the Feu-Follet restaurant create the perfect power duo of great skiing and great food. Family skiing at Le Relais Le Relais is an outstanding winter family destination near Québec City. Just 15 minutes from downtown, you’ll find 33 trails, 28 of which are lit at night, plus 3 snow parks and 2 bunny hills. The pros will love the glade runs! All aboard the shuttle!\nClimb aboard the SKI BUS shuttle that takes you from Québec City to the Mont-Sainte-Anne and Le Massif de Charlevoix ski areas. The bus picks you up at your downtown hotel and drops you off at the foot of the slopes. What an easy way to make the most of your winter vacation!\nMassif de Charlevoix Photos: Francis Gagnon (sunset, couple), Le Massif de Charlevoix What to do? — Winter Mont-Sainte-Anne 133 Northern adventures Want to enjoy winter to the max while staying in shape and having fun? When it comes to winter sports, Québec City has it all!\nCross-country skiing Get ready for the cross-country skiing adventure of a lifetime thanks to our outstanding snow cover. Head for Mont-Sainte-Anne, Canada’s biggest cross-country ski area, or Parc national de la Jacques-Cartier, with its off-trail Nordic ski area, or Forêt Montmorency, which opens in late October, or Camp Mercier.\nSnowshoeing Most cross-country skiing spots offer trails groomed for snowshoeing. If that sounds appealing, try the 40 kilometres of trails at Vallée Bras-du-Nord, or the Sentiers des Caps de Charlevoix and its breathtaking views of the St. Lawrence River.\nFatbiking The only qualification needed for riding these beefy-tired bikes is knowing how to ride a bike! Several centres and national parks offer trails, but Empire 47 at Lac-Delage is truly the best of the best, with about 40 kilometres (25 miles) of groomed trails.\nSnowmobiling Snow abounds from Portneuf to Saint-Tite-des-Caps, by way of Québec City, La Jacques-Cartier and Île d'Orléans. With the Charlevoix and Saguenay– Lac-Saint-Jean regions included, the entire area offers more than 5,000 kilometres of fantastic snowmobile trails through unforgettable scenery. Canyoning Try ice canyoning on the frozen 41-metre-high (135-foot) Jean-Larose Falls for a guaranteed adrenaline rush! Ice canoeing Now for something really different! Take an introduction to ice canoeing in downtown Québec City with Ice Canoeing Experience. Learn the rowing and “scootering” techniques on the St. Lawrence River, amid a magnificent frozen waterscape. 134 The go-to destination Village Vacances Valcartier is the largest winter playground in North America, with more than 35 runs for thrill-seekers of all ages. Slide on individual inner tubes, experience the Tornado or race down the slopes on a snow raft! There are also special play areas for the little ones! Dizzying descent The Toboggan Slide Au 1884 has been around for over a hundred years and is located next to the Fairmont Le Château Frontenac, on the Dufferin Terrace. Hang on to your hat as you zoom down one of the three icy runs at speeds of up to 70 kilometres per hour (44 miles per hour)! After all that excitement, a hot chocolate is a welcome treat!\nGo play outside with the family Bundle up the kids because it’s time to go slide and play outside!\nTHE TOP CHOICE OF… Annie Lafrance, journalist, sports enthusiast and mom of two “For a day of 100% fun, I recommend snow tubing at Les Glissades Tewkesbury. It’s a gorgeous setting, a friendly venue and really fun. Tip: bring your lunch and ski goggles.” Lace up your skates For a unique family outing, head to the Méga Parc amusement centre at the Galeries de la Capitale mall. Ice skate on the 750-metre (half-mile) track—the longest indoor skating path in North America—that loops around the park’s fun rides. It’s the perfect spot to introduce the little ones to skating, as learning supports are available for rental.\nWoof! If you’re looking for an adventure that’s really out of the ordinary, go dogsledding! This activity is offered at Portneuf (Au Chalet en Bois Rond), in the Jacques-Cartier Valley (Aventure Inukshuk and Aventures Nord-Bec Stoneham), at Mont-Sainte-Anne (Les Secrets Nordiques) and at Lac-Beauport (Pourvoirie du Lac-Beauport). Experience the excitement of tearing up the trails on a sled pulled by impressive huskies.\nVillage Vacances Valcartier Aventure Inukshuk Photos: Steve Deschênes (couple snowshoeing). Francis Gagnon (tubes), France Bernier (dogs) Hotel PUR, Quebec, a Tribute Portfolio Hotel Experience authentiCITY in the heart of Quebec Warming stations are set up by the City of Québec for visitors to warm up during their winter explorations. Each station offers a range of activities for passers-by. Keep your eyes open; there might be one near you! Places to gather and warm up Why not take time to relax by the fire… outside? 136 Québec City is the true north! No matter the weather, there’s always something fun to do outdoors. Ice skating Skate on a refrigerated rink at Place D’Youville, in the magical setting of Old Québec, right in the heart of the action. Or, try the loop rink on the Plains of Abraham: it’s a fantastic place for winter fun in a beautiful snowy landscape. Cross-country skiing Whether you prefer classic cross-country or skate skiing, there are groomed trails throughout the city, including at the Plains of Abraham, Domaine Maizerets and the Base de plein air de Sainte-Foy. For a family outing, the best spots are Parc linéaire de la Rivière-Saint-Charles and the Centre de plein air de Beauport.\nFishing Fish are still active in winter. Seasoned or novice fishers will be thrilled to catch trout, perch or walleye—mosquito free and in a gorgeous snowy setting! Pourvoirie du Lac-Beauport is an especially good spot.\nSnowshoeing Grab your snowshoes and head to the Plains of Abraham, Domaine Maizerets or the Base de plein air de Sainte-Foy for a full immersion in nature, right in the city!\nONE OF A KIND!\nThe ice hotel is built with 500 tons of ice and 40,000 tons of snow. The Hôtel de Glace is the only one of its kind in North America. Enjoy an unguided or guided tour of the Great Hall, the ice bar and the 40 thematic rooms and suites. And yes, people really do sleep there! This Valcartier desti nation will knock your socks off!\nPhotos: Pikur (left), Stéphane Audet Photo (rink), Hôtel de Glace Winter in the city Bar at the Hôtel de Glace Place D’Youville skating rink ONE DESTINATION FOUR SEASONS ONE DESTINATION FOUR SEASONS spring The sweet delights of spring As the snow melts and temperatures rise above the freezing point, Québecers trade their winter garb for less bulky clothing. Spring is also sugaring-off season, which means it’s time to head to the cabane à sucre (sugar shack) for lively French-Canadian folk music and hearty dishes that locals top with maple syrup. There’s sweet excitement in the air!\nPhoto: Francis Gagnon La Petite Cabane à sucre de Québec FIS Cross-Country World Cup Late March See the world’s top cross-country skiers compete in the finals of the FIS Cross-Country World Cup on the Plains of Abraham.\nImagiNation Writers’ Festival Early April Meet celebrated authors from Québec, Canada and beyond, during this English-language literary festival at the Morrin Centre.\nSalon international du livre de Québec Mid-April Over 60,000 visitors come to meet local and international writers at this book fair, an immensely popular literary event! Québec City Magic Festival Early May Be astonished, amazed and mystified by over 40 professional artists and 70 free events held in the streets of the Petit-Champlain area.\nTHE SPRING BUZZ Photos: Francis Gagnon (skiers sitting), André-Olivier Lyra (Alex Harvey) Québec Exquis May For this gourmet festival, more than 20 restaurants pair up with local food producers to create special menus showcasing local fare, offered at special prices. Carrefour international de théâtre de Québec Late May to early June A major event in the theatre world, the Carrefour presents award-winning productions from around the world, complemented by strong Québecois and Canadian components. The highly popular and free outdoor walk-through experience, Où tu vas quand tu dors en marchant? is a highlight of the season. St. Patrick’s Day Late March Québec City goes green for a weekend in celebration of its Irish heritage. Enjoy the St. Patrick’s Day parade, Festibière beer festival and festivities on the Grande Allée.\nAlex Harvey 140 1 800 665-6527 sepaq.com/pq/jac the uLtimate outdoor experience in the heart of a majestic vaLLey only 30 minutes from Québec city Canoeing | Kayaking | Hiking | Discovery activities Snowshoeing | Backcountry skiing | Cabins | Yurts Photo : Paul Dussault, Sépaq 4 activities for milder weather Ah, spring, when the days get longer and the sunshine grows warmer! It’s the perfect time for a spring getaway! Get inspired by these four outing ideas. Return of the greater snow geese In April and May, observe the greater snow geese coming back through the area on their yearly migration! And while you’re at the Cap Tourmente National Wildlife Area, why not take time to wander the 20 kilometres (12.5 miles) of hiking trails to enjoy the spring sunshine?\nGarden visits As nature awakens, head to one of the city’s many urban parks and gardens (list on page 44) to admire the early flowers. Parc du Bois-de-Coulonge and the gardens of Villa Bagatelle are especially enchanting in spring.\nRelaxing, Nordic style Hang out in a hammock, eat in your robe and relax in a hot tub outdoors, at one of the Nordic spas in or around the city. See our spa guide on page 48 to plan your day of repose. Spring skiing Hit the slopes under the warm spring sun at these four ski resorts: Mont-Sainte-Anne, Le Massif de Charlevoix, Stoneham Mountain Resort and Le Relais Centre. And don’t forget that cross-country skiing is also an option! 3 1 2 4 Photo: André-Olivier Lyra (farm) Parc du Bois-de-Coulonge 142 DISCOVER DISCOVER BOTH SHORES Visiting a sugar shack is one of the best ways to celebrate the beginning of spring. Gathering in large rustic log cabins, families sit down at long tables to enjoy a hearty maple-soaked meal of traditional Québecois cuisine. There The sweetest tradition is often a small stage where local musicians play Québec folk songs, rigodons and square dancing music. Outside, enjoy maple taffy on snow. Maple syrup is boiled, then poured in lines on clean snow. As it cools, ice pop sticks are used to roll the maple taffy into a chewy lollipop.\nAfter your maple extravaganza, you may want to walk off your meal while enjoying the first warmer days.\nChute- Montmorency Old Quebec Isle-aux-Coudres Île d’Orléans TRAINDECHARLEVOIX.COM 1 844 737-3282 All aboard Charlevoix Hotel & Spa Le Germain Charlevoix Baie-Saint-Paul Train Station Musée maritime de Charlevoix St-Joseph-de-la-Rive Hôtel-Pavillons-Spa Petit Manoir du Casino La Malbaie a u n i q u e e x p e r i e n c e Partnerships Château Frontenac Chute- Montmorency Old Quebec Isle-aux-Coudres Île d’Orléans TRAINDECHARLEVOIX.COM 1 844 737-3282 All aboard Charlevoix Hotel & Spa Le Germain Charlevoix Baie-Saint-Paul Train Station Musée maritime de Charlevoix St-Joseph-de-la-Rive Hôtel-Pavillons-Spa Petit Manoir du Casino La Malbaie a u n i q u e e x p e r i e n c e Partnerships Château Frontenac Québec City’s neighbourhoods—nestled closely together, safe and welcoming— have been shaped by the people who live there. Stores, cuisine, leisure, celebration: they each have their unique flavour. For the whole picture, check them all out!\nThe boroughs 148 148 Old Québec 150 Photo: André-Olivier Lyra First and foremost a UNESCO World Heritage site, fortified Old Québec offers a postcard-perfect setting in which to amble, whose beauty is only rivalled by the hospitality of its residents and shopkeepers.\nLeave the car at home: Québec City is made to be walked. \u001b\u001a\u0019\u0018\u0017\u0016\u0015\u0017\u001c\u0018\u0014\u0013\u0017\u0018 \u0018\u0014 \u0013\u0014\u0012 \u0018\u0017 \u001a\u0014 \u0016\u001e\u0011\u0015 \u0010\u001a\u0018\u001b\u0017 \u0018\u0014\u0013\u0014\u0012\u0018\u0017 \u001a \u0014\u0016 \u0011\u0015\u0010 \u001a\u0018\u001b\u0017 \u000f\u000e\u001c\u001a\u0013\u0017\u0016\r\u0017 \u000e\u001c\u001a\u0013\u0017\u0016\r\u0017 \u000e\u001c\u0018\r\u0017 \b\u000e\u001c\u0018\r\u0017 \u0018\r\u0017\u001c\u0007\u0017\u001c\u001b\u001a\u001c\u0015\u0018\u0006\u0014\u0005\u0011\u0018\u0006\r\u0004\u0017 \u0003\u0006\r\u001b\u0017\u0013\u001a\u0018\u0007\u001c\u0010\u0017\u0016\u0018\u0014\u0011\u0003\u0006\r\u0018\u001a \u001a MUSÉE DU FORT QUAI SAINT-ANDRÉ DALHOUSIE CHAMPLAIN BOULEVARD CHAMPLAIN MARCHÉ SAINT-JEAN SAINT-JEAN BOULEVARD CHAREST EST AVENUE HONORÉ-MERCIER AUTOR OU TE D UFFE RIN-MO NT MORENCY CÔTE D’ ABRAHAM RUE DE LA COURONNE RUE DORCHESTER BOULEVARD CHAREST OUEST BOULEVARD CHAREST OUEST MARIE-DE-L’INCARNATION SAINT-JEAN BOULEVARD RENÉ-LEVESQUE EST CHEMIN SAINTE-FOY GRANDE ALLÉE OUEST GRANDE ALLÉE EST \u0003\u0006\r\u001b \u0002\u0017\u001a\u0016\u0011\u001b\u0017\u001f\u001a\u0004\u0017 \u001a \u0006\u0018\u0006 \u0017\u001c\u001b\u001a\r\u0018\u0017\u0016\u001e\u0014\u0017\u0016\u0016\u0017 BO UL.
