Lépine Esc. de la Chapelle Esc. du Faubourg Esc. Lavigueur Côte Badelard Ascenseur Esc. Colbert Esc. Victoria Esc. des Franciscains Cap Diamants Esc. Casse-cou Esc. passage du Roi Place Royale Place de l’Assemblée-Nationale Palais Montcalm Parc de l’Esplanade Maison de la littérature Redoute Dauphine Parc du Cavalier-du-Moulin Résidence du gouverneur général Pôle culturel du Monastère des Ursulines Terminal de croisières 1 Château Frontenac 2 Musée du Fort 3 La Basilique-cathédrale Notre-Dame de Québec 4 Cathédrale Holy Trinity 5 Hôtel de ville 6 Morrin Centre 7 Lieu historique national des Fortifications-de-Québec 3 2 1 8 Séminaire de Québec 9 Terrasse Pierre-Dugua-De Mons 10 Terrasse Dufferin 11 Place D’Youville 12 Citadelle de Québec 5 4 6 7 9 8 10 11 12 151 The boroughs — Old Québec In summer, when Rue Saint-Jean is closed to traffic, pedestrians stroll, sip cold drinks on friendly terraces and admire the talents of street artists. In winter, the scene is straight out of a fairy tale, with gently falling snow and the cute wooden booths of the German Christmas market. Rue Saint-Louis, with its great restaurants and art galleries, is fun to explore at any time of year. Follow the street to its end, in front of the Samuel de Champlain monument and Dufferin Terrace, to the foot of the Château Frontenac for an absolutely breathtaking view of the river.\nBeyond the hustle and bustle, it’s the quiet side streets that bare Old Québec’s soul, with surprises around every corner: the Victorian-style Morrin Centre library, the Maison de la littérature library, along with jazz bars and little coffee shops patronized by artists and students. To get a unique view of the city, take a walk on the ramparts and around the Citadelle of Québec. You’ll be able to admire the military history of a city protected by 4.6 kilometres of fortifications. And be sure to get a picture of yourself with a real cannon! The historical neighbourhood After passing through the massive Porte Saint-Jean, you’ll be standing in the heart of Old Québec at its most lively and festive! Restaurants offering fine cuisine or more reasonable fare segue to shops, pubs and a variety of cultural and historical sites.\nSeveral busses stop at the Place D’Youville terminal (namely RTC Métrobus routes 800, 801 and 807), and there are always taxis waiting. In the heart of the downtown area, the historic district is only a 10- to 15-minute walk from most of the central neighbourhoods (Saint-Jean-Baptiste, Montcalm, Saint-Roch).\nHOW TO GET THERE 152 The boroughs — Old Québec Restaurants You’re spoiled for choice in Old Québec! The French influence is strong at Saint-Amour (one of the best restaurants in the city), L’Entrecôte Saint-Jean (winning steak and fries), Chez Jules (friendly bistro), Au petit coin breton and Casse-crêpe Breton (battle of the crêpes!), D’Orsay (European pub), Petit Château (crêpes, raclette and fondue), Café Buade (opened in 1919), Continental (with cooking at your table), Tournebroche (rotisserie chicken) and Frères de la Côte (all-out European). Italy is also on the map, at Ristorante Il Teatro (at Le Capitole theatre and hotel), Portofino (Italian bistro in an ancestral home), Conti Caffe (always popular), Parmesan (traditional fare and music) and Bello (with its incredible terrace).\nQuebecois and Indigenous cuisines are proudly represented at La Bûche (urban sugar shack), Aux Anciens Canadiens (typical dishes), Sagamité (traditional Indigenous cuisine), Maison Livernois (gourmet pub and distillery), Chez Boulay (boreal bistro) and its sibling, Comptoir Boréal, and, in gourmet renditions, 1608 – Bar à vin et fromages, Le SAM and Champlain at the Fairmont Le Château Frontenac. Casual diners will love the burgers at Trois Garçons, the pizza at Sapristi, the updated club sandwiches at Beclub, the fish and seafood at Brasserie Les Mordus and the Bistro le 1640, with its terrace facing the Château Frontenac. Then there’s Restaurant Wong, the king of the city’s Chinese restaurants. For quick eats, choose from Baguette & Chocolat (sandwiches and crêpes), Peppe (sandwiches and juice bar), Chic Shack (burgers and locally flavoured poutines), La Galette Libanaise Old Québec's local faves Literary life Take yourself over to the impressive Victorian library at the Morrin Centre, a literary and historic centre for English culture, and the neighbouring Maison de la littérature, before grabbing up the latest book titles at Libraire Pantoute and comics at Librairie Première Issue.\nComptoir Boréal Photos: Leonid Andronov (canon), André-Olivier Lyra (Comptoir Boréal) 153 (Mediterranean freshness) or Au 1884 (near the toboggan slide). For sweet treats, head to Nym Gelato, Sainte-Crème or Cows (ice cream from Prince Edward Island), to Chocolato and Chocolats Favoris for cones dipped in choco late or to Christophe Morel for some real chocolate fun.\nCoffee shops, pubs and bars If there’s one famous coffee shop in the neighbourhood, it’s Chez Temporel. But you can also wake up with a cuppa and a quick bite from La Maison Smith, Local or Café-boulangerie Paillard. So fast and easy! The nights never end at the area’s Irish pubs, like Saint-Patrick, Saint-Alexandre or Chez Murphy’s. If you like folk singers, Les Yeux Bleus is the bar for you. Jazz and gin lovers will get their fill at Bar Sainte-Angèle.\nStores Rue Saint-Jean inside the walls is a favourite shopping area. Get your wool sweaters at Ça va de soi; knitwear at Jourdain; tree ornaments at Boutique de Noël de Québec; hats at Bibi & Cie, Chapelier; designer labels at Maison Darlington; original decorative items at Ziba beautés d’Orient; Indigenous handicrafts at Boutique Stadaconé, Atelier-Boutique Atikuss or Boutique Le Sachem; handbags at Bagages de France; sweets at Kandju Bonbons, Bec et Bonbon or Confiserie C’est si bon; rustic-chic items at 3 Poules or ARTEQ; ambiance-shapers at Bougies de Charroux; colourful Culturally curious On opposite sides of Place D’Youville but acting in harmony, Palais Montcalm, Le Diamant and Le Capitole each offer original programming you’ll want to experience. Don't miss...\nT he passageway in Rue du T résor where local artists deck the walls in their art, to the great pleasure of passers-by.\nPub Saint-Patrick Le Diamant and Le Capitole 154 The boroughs — Old Québec Mikael Garneau, Chic Shack Restaurant 1 Pierre-Dugua-De Mons Terrace “To wind down after work, for a picnic in summer or fall and to enjoy its amazing view of the St. Lawrence River and Old Québec.” 2 Café La Maison Smith “It’s close to Chic Shack, so it’s easy to grab a coffee and croissants, but also it has a delicious date bar where I can fuel up before a long day at the restaurant!” 3 Quincaillerie du Vieux-Québec “This hardware store is completely unique! I usually hate hardware stores, but this one has a very particular personality and great customer service.” accessories at Ketto; adorable kids’ clothing at Souris Mini; Quebecois creations at Caméléon; super-smelling concoctions at Lush; gifts for the whole family at La petite maison bleue par Hatley, Sentiers or Magasin Général; high-end gifts at Artisans Canada, Most Bijoux or Bijouterie Zimmermann; treats for the feet at Moi mes souliers or Fillion Chaussures; fashion jewellery at Origines; Medieval objects at 3 Tours; paintings at Galerie Le chien d’or; Inuit artwork at Galerie Brousseau; outdoor clothing at Chlorophylle; fine oils at OLIV Québec; tea and herbal tea at David’s Tea; freshly popped popcorn at Mary’s Popcorn Shop; local foodstuffs at Couillard, Fils et cie… The list goes on! And the ultimate store for the best in fashion and home goods: La Maison Simons, since 1840!\nBagages de France Bibi & Cie, Chapelier T O P P I C K S Photos: André-Olivier Lyra (Saint-Patrick, Bibi & Cie, Chapelier, Bagages de France), Marie-Hélène Raymond (Le Diamant and Le Capitole) 155 Petit-Champlain Photo: Valérie Busque 156 Petit-Champlain The boroughs — Petit-Champlain Petit-Champlain’s picturesque pedestrian streets and the magnificent Place-Royale—where Samuel de Champlain established the first permanent French settlement in North America—will take you back more than 400 years, to Québec City’s founding.\nYou are not dreaming; it almost feels like we're in Old Europe. 1 Funiculaire du Vieux-Québec 2 Fresque du Petit-Champlain 3 Traverse Québec-Lévis 4 Église Notre-Dame-des-Victoires 5 Fresque des Québécois 6 Théâtre Petit Champlain \u001b\u001a\u0019\u0018\u0017\u0016\u0015\u0017\u001c\u0018\u0014\u0013\u0017\u0018 MUSÉE DU FORT QUAI SAINT-ANDRÉ DALHOUSIE CHAMPLAIN D CHAMPLAIN MARCHÉ SAINT-JEAN SAINT-JEAN ARD CHAREST EST AVENUE HONORÉ-MERCIER AUTOR OU TE D UFFE RIN-MO NT MORENCY CÔTE D’ ABRAHAM RUE DE LA COURONNE RUE DORCHESTER \u0003\u0006\r\u001b \u0002\u0017\u001a\u0016\u0011\u001b\u0017\u001f\u001a\u0004\u0017 BO UL. Cul-de-Sac Promenade des Gouverneurs Musée Royal 22e régiment Parc de l’Amérique-Française Jardin Jeanne-d’Arc Parc de la Francophonie Manège militaire Voltigeurs de Québec Place George-V Champs-de-Bataille Esc. des Glacis Esc. Lépine Esc. de la Chapelle Esc. du Faubourg Esc. Lavigueur Côte Badelard Ascenseur Esc. Casse-cou Esc. passage du Roi Place Royale Place de l’Assemblée-Nationale Palais Montcalm Parc de l’Esplanade Maison de la littérature Redoute Dauphine Musée des plaines d’Abraham Colline parlementaire & Plaines d’Abraham Hôtel du Parlement 3 2 1 4 5 6 157 Two- and three-storey stone houses later covered with plaster, raised common walls acting as firebreaks, charming dormer windows, gable roofs and wide chimneys. Be sure to take a moment to admire Notre-Dame-des-Victoires, one of the oldest churches in North America. One of the oldest and most charming commercial streets in North America, Petit-Champlain is lined with boutiques offering locally made handicrafts, art galleries, gourmet food stores and a variety of restaurants. Look up to see the decorative umbrellas hung overhead and the ladders on the roofs of some homes. In winter, Petit-Champlain gets into the Christmas spirit with tasteful lights and decorations. This neighbourhood is the perfect place for a leisurely evening stroll in a setting straight out of a fairy tale.\nThe European neighbourhood Lovers of architecture will adore this area, where the French influence abounds. The Old World’s influence is crystal clear in this neighbourhood! Casse-Cou Steps This neighbourhood was made to be explored on foot. Take the RTC 1 or 11 bus routes. The first passes through the Saint-Sauveur and Saint-Roch neighbourhoods, while the second passes through Upper Town (inside the fortifications). It’s also where the Québec City–Lévis ferry arrives and departs from. Another possibility is taking the Casse-Cou (or Breakneck) Steps from Old Québec or the funicular, for a unique experience! HOW TO GET THERE 158 Restaurants To really feel the magic of Petit-Champlain, there’s nothing like sitting on the Lapin Sauté’s terrace or within the old walls of Bistrot le Pape Georges. We also like the contemporary character of Bistro Sous le Fort and Q-de-Sac Resto-Pub. For a quick meal in the heart of the action, there’s Le Casse-Cou and the indispensable Cochon Dingue at any time of day. Families will get their fill at Sapristi Bistro-Bar or Spag & Tini. How about cocktails at Pub L’Oncle Antoine? Grilled fare at Côtes à Côtes? Or a full-on gastronomic experience at La Tanière3? What do you feel like tonight?\nStores Rue du Petit-Champlain, like the neighbouring streets, is a paradise of lovely boutiques! For fashion, check out Oclan, Atelier La Pomme, Zazou, Atelier Huguette Fecteau, Bilodeau Canada, Blanc Mouton, Boutique Red Canoe, Charlevoix Pure Laine and Le Capitaine D’a Bord. You’ll find beautiful accessories and shoes at Ibiza, Lowell, Amimoc (with their renowned moccasins) and Chaussures Fluchos. Get your skin care products at L’Occitane en Provence or Shamâne Cosmétiques, and treat the kids at les ptits mosüs. If you love jewellery and handicrafts, you’ve got to see Boutik Art Denis Nicolas, Boutique Artnika, Jules Perrier Joaillier, L’Oiseau du Paradis, La Dentellière, Boutique Le Sachem and Forgeron d’Or.\nAnd stock up on fun gifts and original souvenirs at Brin de Folie, Pot en Ciel, T-Dingue, Tabagie Casse-Cou, Temps Libre, Collections Feejos, Boutique Expression and La Malle aux souvenirs. There’s a good chance you’ll run out of room for it all in your luggage!\nPetit-Champlain's local faves Auditory pleasure The intimate, welcoming Théâtre Petit Champlain showcases singer/songwriters, especially in French. Before the show, hang out in nearby Parc Félix-Leclerc on beautiful summer evenings. Lapin Sauté DID YOU SEE THE NEIGHBOURHOOD’S TWO FRESCOES? SIMPLY STUNNING!\nThe boroughs — Petit-Champlain Photos: Steven Kriemadis (left), Sam St Onge (Lapin Sauté), Alexandre Strohl/Théâtre Petit Champlain (guitars) 159 Food shops This neighbourhood really has a sweet tooth, with La Petite Cabane à sucre de Québec, Madame Gigi Confiserie, La Fudgerie and the latest, La Nougaterie Québec. So much choice! But don’t forget to taste some local products at Cidrerie et Vergers Pedneault. Kick back with some crêpes and waffles at Crêperie et Cie 5.0 or a good coffee at the La Maison Smith, right in the middle of Place Royale. Art galleries Like an extension of the Old Port, the streets around Place Royale are also filled with art at Galerie d’art Royale, Beauchamp, Douce Passion, Espace Galerie B8, Galerie Québec Art, Boutique des métiers d’art du Québec and Galerie d’art Chez l’Artiste, in Petit-Champlain. Marie-Soleil Le Houillier, marketing advisor at Restos Plaisirs 1 Petit Champlain Theatre “I love this intimate theatre; it’s definitely my favourite in Québec City. You almost get the impression that the artist on stage is playing only for you!” 2 Lapin Sauté “I fell in love with this little restaurant that looks like an old Canadian house. It’s my favourite place to taste sophisticated and comforting dishes paired with Québec spirits.” 3 Mary’s Popcorn Shop “This popcorn shop is like Ali Baba’s cave for those who, like me, crave this delicacy! Must try: the sweet and savoury Québec style mix.” La Maison Smith Photos: Jeff Frenette Photography (La Maison Smith), Emmanuel Coveney (wharf) T O P P I C K S 160 Old Port A great place to wander and explore, Québec City’s Old Port offers stunning views and a wonderfully casual atmosphere. Filled with art galleries and antique shops, it is a treasure trove of great finds. \u001b\u001a\u0019\u0018\u0017\u0016\u0015\u0017\u001c\u0018\u0014\u0013\u0017\u0018 \u0017 \u000e\u001c\u0018\r\u0017 \u0003\u0006\r\u001b\u0017\u0013\u001a\u0018\u0007\u001c\u0010\u0017\u0016\u0018\u0014\u0011\u0003\u0006\r\u0018\u001a \u001a MUSÉE DU FORT QUAI SAINT-ANDRÉ DALHOUSIE CHAMPLAIN MARCHÉ SAINT-JEAN TE D UFFE RIN-MO NT MORENCY \u0003\u0006\r\u001b \u0002\u0017\u001a\u0016\u0011\u001b\u0017\u001f\u001a\u0004\u0017 BO UL. JEAN-LESAGE SAINT-PAUL FABRIQUE C Ô T E D E L A \u001a \u0006\u0018\u0006 \u0017\u001c\u0007\r\u0001\u0001\u0017\u0018\u0014\u0016\u0011\u0006\u0016\u001e\u0006\u0018\u0017\u0016\u0015 pitole Édifice Price Monastère des Augustines Jardin des gouverneurs Musée de l'Amérique francophone Esc. Frontenac Esc. Q.-du Roi Esc. Cul-de-Sac Esc. Casse-cou Esc. passage du Roi Place Royale 1 Marina du Port de Québec 2 Bassin Louise 3 Rue Saint-Paul 4 Espace 400e 5 Musée naval de Québec 6 Musée de la civilisation 7 Gare du Palais 8 Silos à grain Bungee 4 2 9 Place de la FAO 10 Place des canotiers 5 6 7 Did you know... Québec City is a famous international cruise port? 1 8 9 10 3 162 The Old Port’s charming setting is dotted with stately historic homes, and its magnificent Gare du Palais, built in 1915, was named a Heritage Railway Station. In the midst of the old financial district, the sculpture-fountain at Place de la FAO stands proudly, attracting passers-by, while Place des Canotiers is the perfect spot for watching huge international cruise ships come in. Québec City is now the leading destination for cruises on the St. Lawrence as well as a major port of call. If you wander down a side street, like Saint-Paul, Sault-au-Matelot or Sous-le-Cap, you’ll find a cozy bar, an unexpected terrace or a nationally known restaurant. It’s the perfect spot to take a break before heading off for the Musée de la civilisation, the Naval Museum of Québec, the Îlot des Palais or Louise Bassin, a marina popular with pleasure boaters from all over.\nAs soon as night falls, the Bunge grain silos form the backdrop for the stunning Aurora Borealis show, an impressive projection that paints the massive silos with dancing northern lights.\nThe quiet neighbourhood Quiet, but never boring! The Old Port is the perfect place to leisurely browse antique shops, visit art galleries and enjoy a drink on a terrace. It invites you to discover the city at your own pace, without constantly checking your watch. The boroughs — Old Port RTC bus line 1 departing from the Avenue Belvédère / Boulevard René-Lévesque Ouest area, and the RTC bus line 11 departing from Sainte-Foy both head to the ferry terminal and pass through the Old Port. When the weather is nice, you can take the 800 Métrobus, get off at the Gare du Palais and walk.\nPhoto: Stéphane Audet Photo HOW TO GET THERE 163 Restaurants The Old Port boasts some of the finest dining in the capital: boreal restaurant Légende, owned by the La Tanière group; Laurie Raphaël, run by the distinguished Vézina family; Café du Monde, with its terrace overlooking the river; L’Échaudé, with its impeccable French cuisine; Charbon Steakhouse, sure to satisfy meat-lovers; Chez Rioux et Pettigrew and its local cuisine; Louise Taverne & Bar à Vin with its fantastic wine list; and Chez Muffy, in the beautifully cozy Auberge Saint-Antoine.\nLet’s not forget the tried-and-trues: the festive atmosphere at the Claddagh Irish pub; an espresso and a pastry, or at Packwood for a quick meal. Miss your animal companion? The surprising Café félin Ma langue aux chats cat café is there to comfort you, with plenty of cats to “pet onsite.” the variety at Mille et une pizzas; the delicious burgers at Chez Victor; the gluten-free bistro menu at Veravin; Vietnamese and Thai dishes at Restaurant Asia; fine Italian favourites at Matto; and the comforting Québec-inspired menu at the popular Le Buffet de l’Antiquaire.\nCoffee shops When you’re ready for a break from browsing antique shops, stop in at Cafés du Soleil for Old Port's local faves Train station or castle?\nBuilt in 1915 in the same style as the Château Frontenac, the Gare du Palais gives visitors arriving by train a glimpse of the romanticism and picturesque architecture that awaits them in the city. In 1992, it was named a Heritage Train Station for its architectural, historical and environ-mental value.\nGare du Palais Photos: Guy Lessard (Gare du Palais), Sam St Onge (Terrace), Stéphane Bourgeois (Lili & Olivia) Café du Monde 164 The boroughs — Old Port Antique shops and art galleries The Old Port abounds with art galleries: Galerie AMF, Galerie Perreault, Galerie d’art Urbania, Galerie d’art Alain Lacaze, Galerie d’art internationale, Lacerte art contemporain and Galerie Roberge.\nIf you prefer antiques, you’ll want to visit G & M Bourget Antiquaires, Antiquités Bolduc, Rendez-vous du collectionneur, Héritage Antiquité and La Nouvelle-France Antiquités.\nStores To bring home one-of-a-kind souvenirs, head to the adorable concept store Lili & Olivia, or if you like furs, Fourrures du Vieux-Port.\nIn the summer months, head to Cour arrière du Festibière, behind the cruise terminal, to enjoy great beer, pub fare and games, all while cooling your feet in the mini-pool!\nVanessa Bell, Communications and marketing coordinator, Le Buffet de l’Antiquaire 1 Musée de la civilisation “It’s felt like home since I was a child. With its plays, conferences, exhibitions and special events, the museum always offers stimulating and smart activities. You learn something and enjoy it at the same time!” 2 The river “It would be ridiculous to be in the Old Port but not make the effort to go down to the river. You can take the wooden paths alongside the marinas or the bike path that winds between the cruise ship terminal and the river, where the winter ice is so majestic. And the colours when the sun rises and sets are breathtaking!” 3 Auberge Saint-Antoine “Whether I’m treating myself to a night in a hotel in my own city or going to the restaurant or the coffee shop, there’s always a good reason to keep coming back to the inn, where I feel like I’m on vacation!” T O P P I C K S Lili & Olivia 165 Saint-Jean-Baptiste 166 Saint-Jean-Baptiste The boroughs — Saint-Jean-Baptiste With its steeply sloping streets, colourful houses all squeezed together and its fun-loving, welcoming nature, Saint-Jean-Baptiste is a lively neighbourhood that likes to celebrate!\nPhoto: André-Olivier Lyra \u0018\u0014 \u0013\u0014\u0012 \u0018\u0017 \u001a\u0014 \u0016\u001e\u0011\u0015 \u0010\u001a\u0018\u001b\u0017 \u0018\u0014\u0013\u0014\u0012\u0018\u0017 \u001a \u0014\u0016 \u0011\u0015\u0010 \u001a\u0018\u001b\u0017 \u000f\u000e\u001c\u001a\u0013\u0017\u0016\r\u0017 \u000e\u001c\u001a\u0013\u0017\u0016\r\u0017 \b\u000e\u001c\u0018\r\u0017 \u0018\r\u0017\u001c\u0007\u0017\u001c\u001b\u001a\u001c\u0015\u0018\u0006\u0014\u0005\u0011\u0018\u0006\r\u0004\u0017 SAINT-JEAN BOULEVARD CHAREST EST AVENUE HONORÉ-MERCIER AUTOR OU TE D U CÔTE D’ ABRAHAM RUE DE LA COURONNE RUE DORCHESTER BOULEVARD CHAREST OUEST BOULEVARD CHAREST OUEST MARIE-DE-L’INCARNATION SAINT-JEAN BOULEVARD RENÉ-LEVESQUE EST CHEMIN SAINTE-FOY GRANDE ALLÉE OUEST GRANDE ALLÉE EST \u001a \u0006\u0018\u0006 \u0017\u001c\u001b\u001a\r\u0018\u0017\u0016\u001e\u0014\u0017\u0016\u0016\u0017 SAINT-JOSEPH EST SAINT-JOSEPH EST SAINT-JOSEPH O.\nSAINT-VALLIER OUEST SAINT-VALLIER OUEST BOULEVARD WILFRID-HAMEL AVENUE CARTIER SAINT-VALLIER EST BOULEVARD RENÉ-LEVESQUE EST Le Diamant Fontaine de Tourny Le Capitol Tour Martello 2 Parc de l’Amérique-Française Jardin Jeanne-d’Arc Parc de la Francophonie Manège militaire Voltigeurs de Québec Place George-V Esc. des Glacis Esc. Lépine Esc. de la Chapelle Esc. du Faubourg Esc. Lavigueur Côte Badelard Ascenseur Esc. Colbert Esc. Victoria Esc. des Franciscains Place de l’Assemblée-Nationale Musée des plaines d’Abraham Colline parlementaire & Plaines d’Abraham Hôtel du Parlement It's worth wandering out of the gates of the walled city!\n1 Cimetière St. Matthew 2 Centre des congrès de Québec 3 Hôtel du Parlement de Québec 4 Observatoire de la Capitale 4 1 5 Grand Théâtre de Québec 6 Église Saint-Jean-Baptiste 7 Tour Martello 4 5 2 3 6 7 167 Foodies will love the little neighbourhood bistros offering mouth-watering local cuisine, as well as all the different food shops: gourmet bakeries, grocery stores, chocolate shops, health-food counters, coffee shops, a gelato shop and on and on! The gastronomic adventure continues in the bars and pubs, where locally crafted beer flows late into the night. Whether you enjoy live music, jam sessions or poetry readings, the neighbourhood’s many venues offer a variety of events to liven up your evening.\nAlong Rue Saint-Jean, get recommendations from passionate record store owners and find literary gems in the used bookstores.\nThe bohemian neighbourhood Nestled between Old Québec and Montcalm, Saint-Jean-Baptiste is bisected by Saint-Jean, the main commercial street. This barely one-kilometre stretch contains something to please everyone, and more! Lastly, the experience wouldn’t be complete without a little shopping in the neighbourhood’s boutiques, where you will find beautiful, one-of-a-kind clothing and home décor items. Locally designed fashions and handmade articles are especially popular. Now you know why the residents look so stylish! 168 Restaurants The Saint-Jean-Baptiste neighbourhood is just a tiny part of downtown, but there’s a concentration of excellent restaurants there: seafood’s the star at Albacore; Swiss comfort at La Grolla; fun sharing plates at La Buvette Scott and its little “brother” Sardines; the new Bleu Marine bistro; crêpes bretonnes at Billig; the must-visit Le Hobbit; the surprising Sinsemilla focused on hemp; the Japanese Tokyo; the laidback vibe at Bonnet d’Âne; the burgers at Chez Victor; and the food-nightlife combo at elli. In a rush? Grab a quick but delicious bite at Snack-Bar Saint-Jean, Comptoir. Vegetarian options are available at Gourmandises Louca, Pow Ramen, Attaboy, pizzéria de quartier or Bols et Poké.\nSaint-Jean-Baptiste's local faves The Saint-Jean-Baptiste neighbourhood is easily accessible on foot from Old Québec. Simply leave the fortifications via the Saint-Jean gate and cross Avenue Honoré-Mercier. You can also take the 800, 801 or 807 bus lines, which stop at Honoré-Mercier/ D’Aiguillon and the Place D’Youville bus station.\nWALKS ON RUE SAINT-JEAN ARE A MUST! La Buvette Scott Sardines The boroughs — Saint-Jean-Baptiste Photos: Stéphane Audet Photo (left), Maxime Maltais (La Buvette Scott, Sardines) HOW TO GET THERE 169 Sylvie Cliche and Josiane Turmel-Poulin, owners of the Jupon Pressé boutique 1 La Buvette Scott “Wow! The food is delicious and the menu changes often, so every time we go, we taste something different. The owners are really welcoming and create a lot of ambiance in their place. You couldn’t ask for better!” 2 Nina pizza napolitaine “This is a new addition to the neighbourhood, but we’ve loved their other location for a long time! The décor is superb, the pizza delectable, and it’s fun to eat there as a family or to order out. Besides that, it’s run by a team of women entrepreneurs, and we love to support them! You’ve got to try it!” 3 Boutique Schü’z “You’ll feel like a kid in a candy store here. Everything is carefully selected. It’s hard to resist with such a great choice! The owner is super friendly and extremely on point with advice!” T O P P I C K S Coffee shops, pubs and bars Start the day off at Cantook Micro Torréfacteur or La Brûlerie Saint-Jean, and wrap it up at Sacrilège (a veritable institution), Pub Nelligan’s, Drague (LGBTQ+), La Ninkasi or Projet. Don't be surprised to see locals enjoying a cup of coffee or a gelato in the Saint Matthew cemetery. T his Protestant cemetery, going back to 1772, was desacralized and has become a green oasis in the neighbourhood. T he church was also transformed... into a lovely library!\nSacrilège Photos: Catherine Bergeron-Duchesne (Sacrilège), André-Olivier Lyra (Tutto Gelato) 170 gg.ca/Citadelle Residence of the Governor General Visit • Explore 1, côte de la Citadelle, Québec 1-866-936-4422 The boroughs — Saint-Jean-Baptiste ice cream shop. Pick up some gourmet health food at Crac, aliments sains and European staples at J.A. Moisan and Épicerie européenne. To explore beers from the province, stop by La duchesse D’Aiguillon or Axe du Malt.\nFood shops Craving gluten? Stop by the Paingrüel and Epi’Fanny bakeries. Satisfy your sweet tooth with nostalgia at Pâtisserie Simon, get your chocolate fix at Érico and cold treats at the Tutto Gelato Italian Stores For clothing, head to Jupon Pressé, Roba & Casso, Exil, Séraphin, Schü’z, L’un et l’autre and Point d’exclamation. For music, there’s CD Mélomane and Musique Chez Sonny. And for great used-book finds, go to Laforce, Nelligan and Comptoir du Livre.\nJupon Pressé Tutto Gelato 171 Montcalm 172 The boroughs — Montcalm Photo: André-Olivier Lyra A perfect balance between bourgeois and bohemian, the Montcalm neighbourhood attracts foodies, sports enthusiasts and art and culture lovers to its beautiful historical setting. Head to Upper Town to enjoy a feast for the eyes, ears and palate! \u0018\u0014\u0013\u0014\u0012\u0018\u0017 \u001a \u0014\u0016 \u0011\u0015\u0010 \u001a\u0018\u001b\u0017 BOULEVARD CHAREST EST RU RUE DORCHESTER BOULEVARD CHAREST OUEST BOULEVARD CHAREST OUEST MARIE-DE-L’INCARNATION SAINT-JEAN BOULEVARD RENÉ-LEVESQUE EST CHEMIN SAINTE-FOY GRANDE ALLÉE OUEST GRANDE ALLÉE EST \u001a \u0006\u0018\u0006 \u0017\u001c\u001b\u001a\r\u0018\u0017\u0016\u001e\u0014\u0017\u0016\u0016\u0017 SAINT-JOSEPH EST SAINT-JOSEPH O.\nSAINT-VALLIER OUEST SAINT-VALLIER OUEST BOULEVARD WILFRID-HAMEL AVENUE CARTIER SAINT-VALLIER EST Tour Martello 2 Parc de l’Amérique-Française Jardin Jeanne-d’Arc Parc de Franco Manège militaire Voltigeurs de Québ Place George-Parc des Champs-de-Bataille Esc. du Faubourg Esc. Lavigueur Côte Badelard Esc. Colbert Esc. Victoria Esc. des Franciscains Festive Grande Allée, classy Avenue Cartier; this is Montcalm! 1 Avenue Cartier 2 Grande Allée 3 Musée national des beaux-arts du Québec (MNBAQ) 4 Plaines d’Abraham 5 Cinéma Cartier 2 1 4 5 3 173 HOW TO GET THERE The Montcalm neighbourhood is only a 10-minute walk from Old Québec via Boulevard René-Levesque. You can also get there on the RTC Métrobus lines 800, 801 or 807 (get off at the Cartier stop) and via bus route 11 from the ferry terminal.\nThe arts neighbourhood Bookstores, a theatre, a museum, a movie theatre and art galleries: Montcalm has everything culture fans could want.\nIn July, the neighbourhood grooves to the beat of the Festival d’été de Québec. For 11 days, the city turns into an open-air concert hall, featuring both renowned inter-national and homegrown artists. When you add its theatres, art galleries and bookstores (offering both new and used books), it’s clear why Montcalm is known as the arts district! Art even graces giant lampshades that top the street lights along Avenue Cartier!\nIf you enjoy exploring museums, you’ll love the Musée national des beaux-arts du Québec (MNBAQ), which offers a wide scope of Québec art through its extensive collection of more than 38,000 works.\nPeople don’t just come for the culture; the food justifies a visit too! Choose among friendly neighbourhood bistros, affordable chain restaurants, pubs, bars and coffee shops. Avenue Cartier also has a number of lovely little boutiques, great for a little browsing or window shopping. In the summer, its urban patios are very popular, and for good reason! Grande-Allée, the main street leading directly to the foot of the Château Frontenac, offers many dining and entertainment options year-round.\nMNBAQ Photos: Idra Labrie (MNBAQ), André-Olivier Lyra (Cochon Dingue), Marie-Hélène Raymond (Avenue Cartier) 174 Restaurants There are many styles of cuisine to choose from in Montcalm! Take your taste buds around the world, to Corsica at Petits Creux Corsica Origina; to the Mediterranean at Café Sirocco, Graffiti and Bistro La Goulette; to Greece at Mezzé taverna grecque; to Ireland at Pub Galway, to Italy at La Scala and Milano Pizza; to India at Cari Doré; to Belgium at Frites Alors!; to France at Moulins La Fayette and Cochon Dingue; to Japan at Metropolitain Eddie Sushi Bar; and to a few different places at Blaxton. Or get a taste of Québec with locally inspired cuisine at Bistro B or Quarante 7.\nIf you’re looking for something light, tuck into raw fish at Sushi Taxi, buy delicious, inspired creations at the Sushi à la maison counter to eat at home or get tasty vegetarian bowls at Freshii. Enjoy!\nCoffee and food shops To put a little pep in your step at any time of day, head to Café Krieghoff (a veritable institution) or Café Castelo. And for tea, there’s Sebz. If you’re craving something sweet, feast your eyes on the selection at the Gaël Montcalm's local faves Vidricaire bakery.\nNeed a hit of sugar? Step into Confiseries Pinoche, Glacier Aberdeen or Chocolats Favoris. Start your day on the right foot with a warm bagel fresh out of the oven from Bügel or assorted pastries from Les Cousins.\nIn the heart of Montcalm, Cinéma Cartier offers the best of popular and art-house movies. Cochon Dingue Avenue Cartier 175 The boroughs — Montcalm Discover the birthplace of Desjardins Group! Located in the heart of Old Lévis, this historic Victorian landmark is open year-round. Free guided tours and activities.\nMaison Alphonse-Desjardins A jewel of our heritage Foodies will adore exploring Halles Cartier, with its 30-something food shops and restaurants, including the Morena Italian grocery store and catering service.\nStores Montcalm residents love fashion and it shows at MUSE par Christian Chenail, Autrefois Saïgon, Moi Lingerie, Urbain Prêt-à-Porter, Les Nanas Chic, Murmure, Boutique IVY, Chaussures Leclerc, Paris Cartier, Le coureur nordique and more. Plenty of choice! Happy shopping! You can also browse books at the Librairie du Quartier bookstore; check out the magazine selection and stock up on office supplies at Un Coin du Monde; and find the perfect home décor at Zone Maison or Au Loft par Style Libre.\nZone Maison Morena Photos: Valérie Busque (Morena), André-Olivier Lyra (Zone Maison) 176 Observatoire de la Capitale Marie-Guyart Building 1037, rue De La Chevrotière – 31st floor, Québec A unique discovery experience Free FOR KIDS UNDER 6 THE HIGHEST AND BEST VIEW OF QUÉBEC!\nPRESENTED BY observatoire-capitale.com Marie-Hélène Raymond, digital strategy coordinator, MNBAQ 1 The Plains of Abraham “A truly beautiful place to stroll around or practice a sport, both in winter and summer!” 2 La Librairie du Quartier “A lovely bookstore where we get good tips and can discuss with other readers.” 3 The Musée national des beaux-arts du Québec “For its huge collection of Quebecois art, its impressive bookstore-boutique and its international exhibits.” T O P P I C K S 177 The boroughs — Montcalm Saint-Roch 178 Saint-Roch Photo: André-Olivier Lyra Like Griffintown in Montréal and Williamsburg in Brooklyn, the trendy Saint-Roch neighbourhood has gone from working-class to where-it’s-at in a few short years. Good food, culture, technology, fashion, nightlife: discover the best of what Québec City’s Lower Town has to offer. \u0018\u0014 \u0013\u0014\u0012 \u0018\u0017 \u001a\u0014 \u0016\u001e\u0011\u0015 \u0010\u001a\u0018\u001b\u0017 \u0018\u0014\u0013\u0014\u0012\u0018\u0017 \u001a \u0014\u0016 \u0011\u0015\u0010 \u001a\u0018\u001b\u0017 Rue Soumande \u000f\u000e\u001c\u001a\u0013\u0017\u0016\r\u0017 \u000e\u001c\u001a\u0013\u0017\u0016\r\u0017 \u000e\u001c\u001a\u0013\u0017\u0016\r\u0017 \u000e\u001c\u0018\r\u0017 \b\u000e\u001c\u0018\r\u0017 \u0018\r\u0017\u001c\u0007\u0017\u001c\u001b\u001a\u001c\u0015\u0018\u0006\u0014\u0005\u0011\u0018\u0006\r\u0004\u0017 SAINT JEAN SAINT-JEAN BOULEVARD CHAREST EST AVENUE HON AUTOR OU TE D UFFE RIN-MO NT MORENCY CÔTE D’ ABRAHAM RUE DE LA COURONNE RUE DORCHESTER BOULEVARD CHAREST OUEST BOULEVARD CHAREST OUEST MARIE-DE-L’INCARNATION SAINT-JEAN CHEMIN SAINTE-FOY \u0003\u0006\r\u001b \u0002\u0017\u001a\u0016\u0011\u001b\u0017\u001f\u001a\u0004\u0017 \u001a \u0006\u0018\u0006 \u0017\u001c\u001b\u001a\r\u0018\u0017\u0016\u001e\u0014\u0017\u0016\u0016\u0017 BO UL. JEAN-LESAGE SAINT-PAUL SAINT-JOSEPH EST SAINT-JOSEPH EST SAINT-JOSEPH O.\nSAINT-VALLIER OUEST SAINT-VALLIER OUEST BOULEVARD WILFRID-HAMEL BOULEVARD WILFRID-HAMEL AVENUE EUGÈNE-LAMONTAGNE C Ô T SAINT-VALLIER EST Le Diamant Le Capitole Monastère des Augustines Esc. des Glacis Esc. Lépine Esc. de la Chapelle Esc. du Faubourg Esc. Lavigueur Côte Badelard Ascenseur Esc. Colbert Esc. Victoria Esc. des Franciscains Redoute Dauphine Saint-Roch, the neighbourhood that practically never sleeps! 1 Église Saint-Roch 2 Jardin Jean-Paul-L’Allier 3 Îlot des Palais 4 Rue Saint-Joseph 5 Stade Canac (baseball) 2 1 5 3 4 179 The boroughs — Saint-Roch Québec City’s busiest commercial street until the 1960s, Saint-Joseph East is now taking back its title as The Main with the arrival of creative young entrepreneurs. The area is especially notorious with food lovers. Fine dining establishments rub shoulders with small neighbourhood restaurants with more modest fare, while the ever more popular microbreweries offer a thirst-quenching selection of original beers. And don’t forget to visit one of the gourmet food stores for local products to enjoy at home!\nIn summer, young web programmers and video game designers join students having lunch in Jardin Jean-Paul-L’Allier, an oasis of greenery in the midst of the city.\nOn your way, stop to gaze up at the imposing Saint-Roch church, built in the early 20th century.\nThere’s entertainment to be found year-round, day or night, thanks to Saint-Roch’s many bars and pubs, where you can extend the evening after a great concert or play. An inviting lighted path will guide your way on Rue Saint-Joseph. Brilliant! The trendy district If you want to browse trendy stores, eat at the new restaurant everyone is talking about, grab a drink in a pub or attend the hottest shows, Saint-Roch is the place to be!\nSaint-Roch is an easy five-minute walk from Old Québec or the Saint-Jean-Baptiste neighbourhood. You’ll need to climb a few stairs, but it’s good cardio! The Faubourg elevator at the corner of Sainte-Claire and Saint-Réal also connects the Upper and Lower towns; practical if you’re pushing a stroller or have a bike with you. The 800 and 801 RTC Métrobous routes serve this neighbourhood regularly, with stops at De Sainte-Hélène and Université du Québec. You can also get there London-style on a double-decker bus, whose tourist route includes a stop midway down Rue Saint-Joseph.\nPhotos: Stéphane Audet Photo (left), Sam St Onge (jjaques) HOW TO GET THERE 180 Restaurants Prepare your stomachs, because you’ll eat well— in all meanings of the word—in this neighbour-hood! Start at sweet bistros like L’Affaire est Ketchup (chic comfort food), Le Clocher Penché (must try), La Gueule de Bois (market cuisine), Les Sales Gosses (European influences), Table (twist on Quebecois cuisine) and jjacques (seafood and cocktails). Or tour the world, with stops in Italy at the highly reputed Battuto, fan-favourite Nina pizza napolitaine and the modern Italian-style Birra & Basta pub; in Japan, with the noodles at Tora-Ya Ramen, the small, sharable dishes at Torii Izakaya and the Honō Izakaya pub with cocktails; in Mexico at the Tequila Lounge, with its impressive choice of tequilas; and in Southeast Asia with Bati Bassac and its lunchroom next door. And it continues with the buvette (wine bar) trend, where good food meets a good wine list at Laurentien Buvette Gourmande and Buvette Le Voisin, and with the friendly yet unclassifiable La Cuisine (comfort food and DJ) and Deux22 (food/bar/boutique).\nFor pubs, the neigh-bourhood has nothing to envy the others, with the likes of London Jack (typical English style), le Pub du Parvis (for sports matches), Le Phœnix du Parvis (food/beer/ shows) and Shaker (cuisine and mixology).\nFind a quick, affordable bite at Bols et Poké (Hawaiian influence), Ogari-San Sushi (Japanese bowls), Poutineville (the name says it all), Casse-Croûte Chez Gaston (authentic), Phil Smoked Meat (also pretty clear) and Stat Café (a coffee/ sandwich shop).\nCoffee shops, bars and microbreweries There’s a great choice for liquids: find your hops at La Barberie, La Korrigane Saint-Roch's local faves Saint-Roch has art in its blood. You'll find music at L'Impérial Bell, L' ANTI bar et spectacles, District Saint-Joseph, and Le Knock-Out music store; fabulous writing in the stacks at Librairie Pantoute and Bouquinerie Rock' n Livre; plays at La Bordée and contemporary art at Méduse.\njjacques 181 The boroughs — Saint-Roch André-Olivier Lyra, photographer and neighbourhood resident 1 Deux22 “I go there for the cool stuff they have in the boutique and for the delicious tacos and tequila.” 2 Hono Izakaya “The menu at this Japanese wine bar is always sumptuous, the cocktails are original and refined, and the team is easy to get along with.” 3 jjacques “It is a hidden bar that’s really worth a visit for its awesome ambiance, wild cocktails and inspirational menu.” and Noctem Artisans Brasseurs; your tea at Camellia Sinensis and Café Pékoe; and your caffeine at Le Nektar caféologue, Café Saint-Henri microtorréfacteur, Brûlerie Saint-Roch and Maelstrøm Saint-Roch. Wrap the night up at MacFly Bar Arcade, LvlOp or Salons d’Edgar.\nFood shops The best spots for a great gourmet selection are Accro cuisine et dépendances or La Place. For a bakery, go with La Boîte à pain or Croquembouche. Got a hankering for cheese? Hit Fromagerie des Grondines et ses amis. Meat? Boucherie Eumatimi. European sausages and hot dogs? William J. Walter Saucissier. And last but definitely not least, chocolate? Champagne Chocolatier!\nStores Saint-Roch’s stores are just as eclectic as the rest of the neighbourhood. You’ll find toys for little kids at Benjo and for big kids at La Planque jeux vidéo and Fanamanga; high fashion at Cœur de Loup and Baizenville; edgy looks at X20 and EXO; seconhand clothes at Friperie Babelou; skin care at Bloomi; fun and weird stuff at L’Inventaire and Rétro Bordello; fresh new décor items at Brico Déco and somewhat less new at Déjà Vu.\nChampagne Chocolatier Photos: André-Olivier Lyra (Champagne Chocolatier), Stéphane Audet Photo (right) T O P P I C K S 182 Limoilou Limoilou is streets lined with majestic trees, narrow alleyways with playing children and old brick buildings with charming spiral staircases. Welcoming, original (it has a circus school!) and very popular with young families and entrepreneurs, this up-and-coming neighbourhood even has its own hashtag: #Limoilove!\n\u0018\u0014 \u0013\u0014\u0012 \u0018\u0017 \u001a\u0014 \u0016\u001e\u0011\u0015 \u0010\u001a\u0018\u001b\u0017 Rue Soumande \u000f\u000e\u001c\u001a\u0013\u0017\u0016\r\u0017 \u000e\u001c\u001a\u0013\u0017\u0016\r\u0017 \u000e\u001c\u001a\u0013\u0017\u0016\r\u0017 \u000e\u001c\u0018\r\u0017 \b\u000e\u001c\u0018\r\u0017 \u0018\r\u0017\u001c\u0007\u0017\u001c\u001b\u001a\u001c\u0015\u0018\u0006\u0014\u0005\u0011\u0018\u0006\r\u0004\u0017 \u0003\u0006\r\u001b\u0017\u0013\u001a\u0018\u0007\u001c\u0010\u0017\u0016\u0018\u0014\u0011\u0003\u0006\r\u0018\u001a \u001a \u0003\u0006\r\u001b \u0002\u0017\u001a\u0016\u0011\u001b\u0017\u001f\u001a\u0004\u0017 \u001a \u0006\u0018\u0006 \u0017\u001c\u001b\u001a\r\u0018\u0017\u0016\u001e\u0014\u0017\u0016\u0016\u0017 BOULEVARD WILFRID-HAMEL AVENUE EUGÈNE-LAMONTAGNE \u001a \u0006\u0018\u0006 \u0017\u001c\u0007\r\u0001\u0001\u0017\u0018\u0014\u0016\u0011\u0006\u0016\u001e\u0006\u0018\u0017\u0016\u0015 A not-so-touristy neighbourhood that certainly deserves a visit!\n1 3e Avenue 2 Grand Marché de Québec 3 Centre Vidéotron 4 École de cirque 5 Parc linéaire de la Rivière-Saint-Charles 6 Parc Cartier-Brébeuf 7 Centre de foires 2 1 3 4 5 6 7 184 Limoilou’s food scene is hopping, with many young chefs opening restaurants on 3e Avenue. On warm summer evenings, the patios fill up quickly. Patrons happily greet their neighbours in a joyful hubbub typical of the close-knit community. The pubs and microbreweries also offer a great way to stretch a pleasant evening late into the night.\nOn lazy summer Sundays, stop by Marché public de Limoilou for something tasty to take to Parc Cartier-Brébeuf, where Jacques Cartier wintered more than 450 years ago. Or take advantage of the Parc linéaire de la Rivière-Saint-Charles for some family exercise.\nThe family neighbour-hood Limoilou’s streets and perpen-dicular avenues form a grid, like in New York City. When you “go up” 3e Avenue, between 2e Rue and 16e Rue, you come across a cluster of quaint little shops just begging you to go in: gourmet grocers, bakeries, coffee shops, artisan boutiques, art galleries, a tea room, an ice cream parlour and more!\nIN JUNE, SEARCH FOR UNIQUE FINDS AT THE GRAND BAZAR DU VIEUX-LIMOILOU STREET SALE. IT’S QUITE THE EXPERIENCE! When the weather is nice, you can easily get to Limoilou on foot from the Saint-Roch neighbourhood by crossing the Dorchester Bridge over the Saint-Charles River. You can also take the bike path to this bike-friendly neighbourhood! Otherwise, the RTC Métrobus routes 801, 3 and 28 offer regular service in the Limoilou area, departing from Upper Town or Lower Town.\nPhoto: Stéphane Audet Photo Wall art by MC Grou HOW TO GET THERE 185 The boroughs — Limoilou Restaurants On 3e Avenue, you’ll find all the essentials: inventive local cuisine at La Planque; dishes to share in good company at Cendrillon; fresh Japanese flavours at Hosaka-Ya; the comforting warmth of Soupe & Cie; sumptuous breakfasts at La Signature; a retro look and scones at Lièvre et la tortue; caffeinated concoctions at Nektar Caféologue, La Maison Smith and Café Sobab; dipped sweet treats at La Diperie; ice cream cones at Maître Glacier; Neapolitan pizzas at Pizzéria No 900; sun-inspired dishes at La Salsa; a gustatory trip east at Miyagi and Nicky Sushi; the feel of a friendly neighbourhood bistro at Colocs, Ket’Chose and Fistons; and finally, mouth-watering calzones and pastries at La boîte à pain/Café Napoli.\nAround 3e Avenue, Limoilou’s streets hold many other great spots: tasty Mediterranean dishes at La Kebaberie Izgara, poutines and the Limoilou's local faves classics at Casse-Croûte Pierrot, and cheesy creations at La Mama Grilled Cheese.\nBars and microbreweries For a nice cold one, head to the La Souche, Le Trèfle or Brasserie Générale 18e Rue microbreweries: there’s no shortage of choices! For a local hangout, think Bal du Lézard. If you like gin, you’ll want to visit Distillerie Stadaconé. Finally, see if you can find Nénuphar, an authentic speakeasy tucked away somewhere around 4e Rue.\nAbsolute musts For gourmet items, be sure to visit the Grand Marché (see page 68) and drop by SNO Microbrasserie Nordik while you’re there.\nLa Boîte à pain Hosaka-Ya Photos: André-Olivier Lyra (Hosaka-Ya, La Boîte à pain), Catherine Côté (Soupe & Cie), William Desrochers (Cendrillon), Jacques Gaines (École de cirque) Soupe & Cie 186 Stores Limoulou residents love good food. They stock up on cheese at Yannick Fromagerie; on fresh bread at Chez Lefebvre or Borderon et Fils; on all sorts of delicious spreads at Conserverie du Quartier or La Réserve, épicerie fine; and on fine deli meats cured onsite at Boucherie Le Croc Mignon!\nReaders will love browsing the Librairie Morency bookstore, and those who enjoy a good find will delight in the handmade and vintage items at Article 721.\nMC Grou, resident and street artist 1 La Taqueria “We go there often, for real Mexican tacos.” 2 Article 721 “We visit this awesome shop because it’s beautiful and smells great.” 3 Le Cendrillon “For the spritz and oysters, it’s the perfect place for a happy hour with some friends.” Don’t forget to look up at the bright yellow tower. That’s L’école de cirque (the circus school)! Le Cendrillon T O P P I C K S Article 721 187 The boroughs — Limoilou Saint-Sauveur Le Parc Durocher 188 Photo: André-Olivier Lyra Saint-Sauveur, affectionately called “Saint-So” by its residents, is a working-class neighbourhood that has, over the last few years, welcomed a growing number of families and professionals seeking the feel of a close-knit community. Welcome to Lower Town!\nGet off the beaten track in Saint-Sauveur! 1 Parc Victoria 2 Parc Durocher 3 Rue Saint-Vallier 4 Rue Marie-de-l’Incarnation 5 Rivière Saint-Charles \u0018\u0014\u0013\u0014\u0012\u0018\u0017 \u001a \u0014\u0016 \u0011\u0015\u0010 \u001a\u0018\u001b\u0017 Rue Soumande BOULEVARD CHAREST EST RUE DORCHESTER BOULEVARD CHAREST OUEST BOULEVARD CHAREST OUEST MARIE-DE-L’INCARNATION SAINT-JEAN CHEMIN SAINTE FOY \u001a \u0006\u0018\u0006 \u0017\u001c\u001b\u001a\r\u0018\u0017\u0016\u001e\u0014\u0017\u0016\u0016\u0017 SAINT-JOSEPH EST SAINT-JOSEPH O.\nSAINT-VALLIER OUEST SAINT-VALLIER OUEST BOULEVARD WILFRID-HAMEL BOULEVARD WILFRID-HAMEL AVENUE EUGÈNE-LAMONTAGN SAINT-VALLIER EST Esc. du Faubourg Esc. Lavigueur Côte Badelard Esc. Colbert Esc. Victoria Esc. des Franciscains 1 2 4 5 3 189 The boroughs — Saint-Sauveur Tucked into the side streets, you’ll find Asian grocery stores, artisan boutiques, fashion stores and institutions that have withstood the test of time and changing trends! The strong community spirit that unites Saint-Sauveur residents can be felt during neighbourhood events, which bring together locals and visitors in a festive atmosphere. Another neighbourhood favourite is the public market held in Parc Durocher on Saturdays in summer, great for stocking up on fresh produce!\nBecause Saint-So residents enjoy getting together, several public places have been set up as meeting spots in a one-of-a-kind urban setting: a refreshing oasis in the concrete jungle, raised garden beds, inviting seating areas and streetside terraces. There’s also Parc Victoria, where you can stretch your legs and relax with family and friends. Saint-Sauveur is a great place to meet new people!\nThe authentic neighbourhood It’s easy to visit the Saint-Sauveur and Saint-Roch neighbourhoods on the same trip. Take a pleasant walk on Rue Saint-Joseph Est, which leads directly to Saint-Sauveur. RTC bus route 1 serves Petit-Champlain and Saint-Roch, travelling along Rue Saint-Vallier Ouest and Rue Marie-de-l’Incarnation Street, while route 18 connects Saint-Sauveur and Saint-Roch (departing from Gare du Palais). Finally, bus route 19 connects the Limoilou and Montcalm neighbourhoods via Saint-Sauveur.\nPhotos: André-Olivier Lyra (Ma Station Café, Griendel) To discover all Saint-Sauveur’s best-kept secrets, take Rue Saint-Vallier Ouest, the main commercial thoroughfare. Here you’ll find friendly neighbourhood restaurants offering unbeatable quality for great prices, a coffee shop, a bakery, pubs, bars, quaint independent shops and more. HOW TO GET THERE 190 Restaurants A trip to Saint-Sauveur is a must for foodies! You’ll find the Le Fin Gourmet bistro with its wonderful comfort foods; legendary Lyon-inspired Pied Bleu and its wine-bar cousin, Le Renard et la Chouette; beloved cocktails at Chez Tao !; the classic Fabrique du Smoked Meat; creative concoctions at Patente et Machin; gourmet fast food at Diner Saint-Sauveur and its nearby Supérette (mini-market); fresh oysters and seafood at Kraken Cru; a flavour trip to the Andes at El Ceviche; unbeatable pizza with homemade dough at Pizzazorg; tasty sandwiches at Franky-Johnny and hearty Hellenic eats at Déli-Grec.\nCoffee shops, microbreweries and food shops Griendel’s reputation for cold beers and a warm atmosphere has spread beyond the neighbourhood, as has news of the giant waffles at Ma Station Café and the mushrooms of Chapeau les bois. The best breads and pastries can be found at Pains sur la planche, a cooperative bakery, or at Saint-Suave Librairie-Café (a play on the neighbourhood’s name if you didn’t catch it). Cravings for sweets are satisfied by the delectable selection at Au royaume de la tarte, and it’s always pleasant to wander the aisles at the Lao Indochine and La Montagne Dorée grocery stores.\nSaint-Sauveur's local faves Thania Goyette and Louis Bouchard Trudeau, owners of Pied Bleu and Le Renard et la Chouette and residents of the Saint-Sauveur neighbourhood 1 Saint Charles River “It’s one of the most beautiful spots in Québec City! We leave the house and take a walk along the riverbanks. The kids’ school is right nearby too. It’s really amazing!” 2 Borderon et Fils “Mostly for François cheery welcome, but also for the generous loaves of bread, the variety and the great-quality flours.” 3 La Fabrique du Smoked Meat “Louis takes the kids there every Saturday. It’s a family ritual!” Griendel T O P P I C K S 191 The boroughs — Saint-Sauveur Your unforgettable experiences quebecoriginal.com Stores Its high-quality leather hats, moccasins and accessories make Cuir Indiana a must-visit. Prefer hunting and fishing? You’ll find everything you need at the huge Latulippe store. For unique finds of all kinds, stop at Cœur de Mailles for wool and knitwear; Zozo et Arty for carefully curated toys and accessories; Les Ateliers de la Pente Douce for quality antiques and Lucia F. for second-hand fashions that will make you look like a million bucks.\nStraddling the Saint-Roch and Saint-Sauveur neighbourhoods, Parc V ictoria offers playgrounds, soccer fields, tennis courts, a skate park, a bike path and walking paths. Something for everyone! Les Ateliers de la Pente Douce Photos: André-Olivier Lyra 192 Sillery Overlooking the river from the cliff-top, this neighbourhood offers magnificent views in any direction you look, whether toward the horizon or the dynamic, picturesque Avenue Maguire. You’ll be intrigued by the history of Sillery!\nT he ultimate in class! \u001b\u001d\u001a\u0019\u0018\u0017\u0016\u0015\u0014\u001e\u0018\u001c\u0013\u001d\u0016\u0015 AVENUE MAGUIRE CHEMIN SAINT-LOUIS BOULEVARD LAURIER GRANDE ALLÉE OUEST BOUL. RENÉ-LEVESQUE OUEST CHEM IN S AIN T-L OUIS BOULEVARD CHAMPLAIN 3 4 2 1 1 Domaine Cataraqui 2 Avenue Maguire 3 Villa Bagatelle 4 Parc du Bois-de-Coulonge 194 Packed together within a few hundred metres are attractive boutiques featuring the latest in fashion and excellent restaurants and coffee shops where you’ll want to linger. In summer, you’ll want to sit on a patio, enjoying a glass of wine or a latte while watching life buzz on the street. Get your fill of sweet and savoury surprises at the area’s bakeries, pastry shops, tea salons, ice cream shops and chocolate makers. There’s plenty to bring home, or to sneak before you get there!\nTo help digest it all, take a walk in the area with forays onto the side streets to admire pastel-coloured 200-year-old homes that look like doll houses or the Maison des Jésuites de Sillery, a historic site of the first Jesuit mission in 1637. A little farther northwest lies the Parc du Bois-de-Coulonge, a true representative of Sillery’s grace and refinement, with its footpaths, arboretum, flower gardens and amazing view of the river. The refined neighbourhood While somewhat removed from downtown, Sillery is definitely worth a detour, especially to visit the lively main commercial artery, Avenue Maguire. Public transit serves Avenue Maguire via RTC bus lines 11 (ends at the ferry terminal) and 16 (a loop that includes Sainte-Foy and Université Laval). There’s also the Métrobus lines 800 and 801, which stop at Gérard-Morisset, on Boulevard René-Lévesque Ouest, just a 10-minute walk from Maguire. Photo: André-Olivier Lyra Parc du Bois-de-Coulonge HOW TO GET THERE Restaurants How can a single street have so many great places to eat? Avenue Maguire and the surrounding streets offer a ton of delicious options: the best bagels at Bagel Maguire Café; Lebanese take-out at Galette Libanaise; pizza at No 900 and Pizza Mag; classic eats at the legendary Pat Rétro diner, followed by dessert at its Crime Puff dairy bar; bistro fare at Chaz, L’Épicurien and Montego; Portuguese chicken at Lio Fiesta; Asian cuisine at Champa Maguire, Saveurs de l’Inde and Erawan Lao; great wine and bites at Tapas & Liège; huge sandwiches at the famous Brynd Smoked Meat; fresh seafood at La Gaspésienne 51 and Middle Eastern flavours at Rameau d’Olivier.\nCoffee shops Locals take their coffee break at Café Castelo, which roasts its own beans, while tea lovers gather at La Maison des 100 thés.\nSillery's local faves Pat Rétro Marie-Hélène Vaugeois, bookseller at Librairie Vaugeois 1 Faks “Eating at Faks is like being at a restaurant but feeling at home. We love it just as much for the food as for the extremely friendly service.” 2 Mount Hermon Cemetery “We like to walk there for its peacefulness and beauty, and all the different headstones, the amazing view of the river and especially the sign of rules from 1851.” 3 Villa Bagatelle “This heritage home presents inspiring exhibitions. Whenever we go there, we also spend time admiring its wonderful garden.” Photos: André-Olivier Lyra (Pat Rétro, Domaine Cataraqui), Vanessa Bell (Marie-Hélène Vaugeois) T O P P I C K S 196 Stores A trip to Avenue Maguire would not be complete without tasting chocolates at Eddy Laurent and foie gras at Canard Goulu!\nFashionistas love Boutik Suisse, Boutique Anne Laurence, Livia Amie Lolë (for yoga enthusiasts!), Musselli et Fils’ fantastic jewelry and the intoxicating scents at Parfumerie Paris.\nFinally, readers and culture lovers will appreciate the vast selection at Librairie Vaugeois, the eclectic collection at the L’ancre des mots used bookstore and the handmade cards and other unique items at Papetruc & Paillasson.\nDomaine Cataraqui Sillery has a strong New England feel. V isit V illa Bagatelle or Domaine Cataraqui's incredible vegetable garden to soak up this neighbourhood's overflowing charm.\nA hotel gem in its historic case, right in the heart of Old Québec!\n★★★★ OLD QUÉBEC 44, Côte du Palais, Old Québec (Québec) G1R 4H8 197 The boroughs — Sillery Sainte-Foy Area 198 The boroughs — Sainte-Foy Area Sainte-Foy, located at the base of the two bridges that cross the river toward Québec City, welcomes you with open arms. As the settling point for new arrivals, transient students and the area’s workers, Sainte-Foy is buzzing with activities. Photo: Stéphan Poulin - Ivanhoé Cambridge inc.\nSainte-Foy, the doorway to the city!\nBOULEVAR D R E N É -L E VESQUE O AUTOROUTE HENRI IV CHEMIN SAINT-LOUIS AUTOROUTE ROBERT-BOURASSA CHEMIN DES QUATRE-BOURGEOIS BOULEVARD LAURIER CHEMIN SAINTE-FOY CHEMIN SAINT-LOUIS 1 Complexe Jules-Dallaire 2 Laurier Québec 3 Place de la Cité 4 Place Ste-Foy 5 Université Laval 6 Pyramide 7 Aquarium du Québec 8 Base de plein air de Sainte-Foy 9 Parc de la Plage-Jacques-Cartier 10 Parc Roland-Beaudin 5 3 4 2 6 1 8 7 9 10 0.7 KM 3 KM 5 KM 199 A 360˚ Immersion into Quebec City’s History 1191, rue St-Jean, Québec 418 204-2592 immersionquebec.com Available languages: Sainte-Foy’s liveliness isn’t anything new. This former municipality experienced a demographic boom at the end of the last century, when its large swaths of agricultural land were converted to a combined residential, commercial and institutional neighbourhood. Families will love meeting the 10,000 species of aquatic animals at the Aquarium du Québec. You can paddle, swim and practice a host of outdoor activities at the Base de plein air Sainte-Foy. Or visit the Jardin universitaire Roger-Van den Hende or the supremely peaceful Parc de la Plage-Jacques-Cartier, one of the only natural wooded areas open to the public that is located right on the St. Lawrence. The “Knowledge hub” neighbourhood Going from parliament hill westward, Métrobus lines 800 and 801 take René-Lévesque and Laurier boulevards, while line 807 takes Chemin Sainte-Foy. Coming from south of the river? Cross the Québec Bridge or Pierre-Laporte Bridge and you’re there!\nPhotos: Stéphane Groleau (Bibliothèque Monique-Corriveau), Jeff Frenette Photography (Aquarium) Sainte-Foy is home to schools, colleges and the oldest French university in North America: Université Laval. In addition to the enormous and highly popular shopping centres, the neighbourhood also has a wide selection of coffee shops, businesses and stores on its boulevards and main thoroughfares. HOW TO GET THERE Bibliothèque Monique-Corriveau 200 A 360˚ Immersion into Quebec City’s History 1191, rue St-Jean, Québec 418 204-2592 immersionquebec.com Available languages: The boroughs — Sainte-Foy Area Restaurants Need a quick lunch? Head to La Pyramide where all the student favourites are located under one roof. If you’re going out, grab a creative cocktail at Shaker, the most popular pub in the quad. The more nostalgic stick with Le Pub Universitaire and get coffee at Au Temps Perdu. The urban vibe continues on Boulevard Laurier, where trendy bistros like Cosmos, La Belle et la Bœuf, La Voie Maltée, Paris Grill, JAJA Sainte-Foy, Chez Victor, the Houston, the Bistango, the Ginger and Ô 6e Sens attract crowds. If you have a sweet tooth, you won’t be able to keep your hands off the decadent doughnuts at Beiko.\nStores Popular for its shopping malls and flagship stores Sainte-Foy's local faves Aquarium du Québec 201 *Can not be combined with any other promotion. Applicable only on bookings at the regular rate. Not applicable for groups.\nBOOK DIRECTLY 1-888-223-9918 Get American breakfast for free!* 330, rue de la Couronne, Québec, Canada G1K 6E6 418-649-1919 | 1-888-223-9918 | www.hotelquebec.com IN THE HEART OF QUEBEC CITY hotelquebec.com OTQ-Pub-EN-2019_2.pdf 1 19-11-13 15:53 Photo: Emmanuel Coveney (Bonne Entente) Bonne Entente like La Maison Simons, Boulevard Laurier also boasts some extraordinary boutiques. You can spend hours dreaming up new home designs at MUST or Un fauteuil pour deux, with its distinctive furniture. Fill your food bags at Halles Sainte-Foy and the Sainte-Foy public market, with its vegetable growers, florists and local farmers. Going out See a movie at Le Clap, one of the most high-tech theatres in the country, now located in Place Ste-Foy. Need some downtime? Contemplate the city from the 17th floor at SkySpa, or mix relaxation, an overnight stay and good food at Amerispa in the Bonne Entente hotel.\nT he Bibliothèque Monique-Corriveau is a cultural gem. T his church-turned-library is named after a beloved Quebecois children's author. 202 *Can not be combined with any other promotion. Applicable only on bookings at the regular rate. Not applicable for groups.\nBOOK DIRECTLY 1-888-223-9918 Get American breakfast for free!* 330, rue de la Couronne, Québec, Canada G1K 6E6 418-649-1919 | 1-888-223-9918 | www.hotelquebec.com IN THE HEART OF QUEBEC CITY hotelquebec.com OTQ-Pub-EN-2019_2.pdf 1 19-11-13 15:53 Paul Samson, student and neighbourhood resident 1 Cochon Dingue Place Ste-Foy “With its ambiance of a French bistro, it’s perfect for weekday breakfast or weekend brunch.” 2 Parc Roland-Beaudin “From late June to the end of August, the park’s stage hosts musical shows and film screenings, so you can make the most of summer evenings without getting caught in the crowds.” 3 Mayflower café “The favourite of avid coffee lovers. Definitely one of the best coffee shops in the neighbourhood!” The boroughs — Sainte-Foy Area T O P P I C K S 203 Whether you are the type to go on a whim or planning your getaway for months, we have what you need: tailor-made itineraries to fully experience all the magic of Québec City.\nPlan your stay 204 Planifier THE PERFECT GETAWAY Québec City—with its historic downtown, cultural events and nearby natural areas— has exactly what it takes to please people who love culture, history, food and wide, open spaces. Whether you’re planning a short excursion or a longer stay, make it even more magical by tailoring it to your interests. Follow the guide!\nPhotos: Emmanuel Coveney (left), Jeff Frenette Photography (right) 206 Dufferin Terrace and Château Frontenac A ride in the funicular provides an amazing view of the Petit-Champlain neighbourhood, the Place Royale and the St. Lawrence River. At the top are the Dufferin Terrace and the Château Frontenac. Admire another beautiful view from the Terrasse and observe the Château. Then, stop by one of the many restaurants to try some local food!\nPlace Royale and Petit-Champlain Stroll the cobblestone streets in this historic district, the cradle of French-speaking America, and visit the Notre-Dame-des-Victoires church. At the end of Rue Sous-le-Fort, you’ll find the Casse-Cou (Breakneck) steps and, to the left, Rue du Petit-Champlain with its cool boutiques, restaurants and artisan shops. After exploring all the nooks and crannies in Petit-Champlain, take the funicular next to the Breakneck steps to get to the summit of Cap Diamant. 24 hours in Québec City How can you get the most from a one-day visit to Québec City? By taking in the main historic attractions on foot and devouring a delicious poutine, that’s how!\nMorning Place Royale 207 Plan your stay Experience .\nCome for the view.\nStay for the hiltonquebec.com Grand reopening: January 2021 Discover the heart of Québec City from a new perspective.\nAnd it all starts with Hilton Québec.\nAfternoon Choose a theme for your afternoon based on your interests!\nFor history buffs Upper Town is brimming with intriguing sites like the Citadelle of Québec, the Musée du Fort and the Fortifications of Québec National Historic Site.\nFor happy shoppers Québec City has countless boutiques, and depending on your time (and budget), you could stay in Old Québec or explore the neighbouring districts. For antiques and art, head toward the Old Port. For fashion, you’re in the right place! Old Québec, Petit-Champlain and Saint-Roch are your best bets. For food finds, you’ll want to go to the other side of the Saint-Jean gate to the Saint-Jean-Baptiste district, where you’ll find a host of delights to bring home with you! Petit-Champlain Evening Indulge! After spending your day walking to explore the city’s best attractions, you might want to have a bite at one of Old Québec’s traditionally Québecois or gourmet restaurants. Do you flip for microbrews or unique bistro food? You’ll find just what you need at Griendel in Saint-Sauveur as well as in the Saint-Roch district, where you can quench your thirst at Korrigane, Noctem Artisans Brasseurs or La Barberie. We hope you enjoyed your day!\nTHE TOP CHOICES OF… Sonia Plourde, fashion designer and resident of Old Québec “During summertime, the Pierre-Dugua-De Mons Terrace is our favourite picnic. The view is just breathtaking! You can see the Château, the Terasse Dufferin, the majestic river and the bridge to the island. It’s peaceful. And when the lights come on at the end of the day, it’s pure magic.” Pierre-Dugua-De Mons Terrace La Barberie AFTER A BIG DAY, NOTHING IS MORE WELCOME THAN A DELICIOUS BEER FROM ONE OF OUR MICROBREWERIES! Photos: Emmanuel Coveney (Petit-Champlain), Valérie Busque (La Barberie), Robert Greffard (winter) 209 Plan your stay Québec CITY & REGION SIGHTSEEING EXCURSIONS FROM $3750 +TX For our packages or tour schedules, visit: oldquebectours.com INFORMATION / RESERVATION 418 664-0460 • 1 800 267-8687 Québec CITY & REGION SIGHTSEEING EXCURSIONS FROM $3750 +TX For our packages or tour schedules, visit: oldquebectours.com INFORMATION / RESERVATION 418 664-0460 • 1 800 267-8687 Hôtel Clarendon Content Partnership Hôtel Clarendon: 150 years of history Did you know that Hôtel Clarendon is the oldest hotel in Canada? Celebrating 150 years of hotel heritage this year, the Old Québec's building has recently undergone renovations. Completely restored, the new transitional style preserves the Clarendon’s refined and unique character while adding a hint of modernity. It’s a true gem to discover!\nCharacter FAMILY BUSINESS A unique badge of honour, the Clarendon has always been owned by a succession of Québécois families.\nLocated in the heart of Old Québec, just steps from tourist attractions, activities and major events, the Hôtel Clarendon offers a distinctive experience through four-star service. Its 144 fully renovated rooms combine comfort and heritage, and still reflect the personality of the historical hotel, with sophisticated noble materials and furnishings. After a stroll in Old Québec, enjoy a delightful break at Les Mordus restaurant to taste its fish and seafood dishes. The ambiance of this new local pub is lively and welcoming. Drop by any time from morning to night to hang out, delight in flavourful cooking and chat with glass in hand. 57, rue Sainte-Anne 418-692-2480 hotelclarendon.com 213 Content Partnership The best of Québec City in 72 hours Day 1 Explore Old Québec Get your bearings by touring Old Québec on the famous red double-decker bus. Get on and off at will while enjoying the exceptional view from the top deck. As you ride along the historic streets of Old Québec, snap some pictures of the city’s iconic features: Château Frontenac and the Dufferin Terrace, the fortifications and the gates, the Citadelle, Parliament and the Tourny fountain, and of course, the Plains of Abraham.\nIn summer, you’ll feast your eyes on street performers! In the evenings, sit out on a terrace, then after your meal, stroll along the streets oozing with European charm. In winter, enjoy the magical feeling of the decorated and cheerily lit streets. Hungry? Sample some traditional Québecois cuisine in one of the area’s specialized restaurants (see page 73). During the day, get a 360-degree view of Québec City from the Observatoire de la Capitale, the highest viewpoint in the city. FOR CULTURE BUFFS Make sure you plan to visit the Musée national des beaux-arts du Québec and the Musée de la civilisation. In summer, walk through Lower Town to see Passages insolites, a temporary installation of outstanding public art. What should you do if you have three days to spend in Québec City and don’t want to miss anything? Here’s our suggestion for an itinerary that will make the most of your stay, regardless of the season.\nDufferin Terrace Photo: Jeff Frenette Photography 214 QuebecForAll.com Tourist and cultural information for travelers with disabilities Day 2 Walk along the oldest streets in Canada Walk along Rue du Petit-Champlain, one of the most beautiful streets in Canada, to shop and discover our talented local craftspeople. In the same neighbourhood, fall under the spell of the legendary Place Royale, the founding place of Québec City. Keep your eye out for the many architectural details evoking our French and British origins. From April to October, take a cruise aboard AML Cruises’ MV Louis Jolliet. The panoramic view of Québec City from the St. Lawrence is breathtaking! Next, see what’s happening in town on the event calendar quebec-cite.com/ en/what-to-do/events. You just might catch one of the city’s many festivals, concerts or summer activities. In winter, take a thrilling ride down the Dufferin Terrace toboggan slide. When you’re ready to relax, try a beer at one of the many microbreweries (see page 84) or wrap up the evening in one of the popular bars featuring folk singers. 215 Plan your stay Day 3 Discover the best of the surrounding area Wonder at the immense waterfalls of the Parc de la Chute-Montmorency, lodged between the river and cliffs. If summer is in full swing, hop over to the other side of the bridge to take in the view of Québec City from the tip of Île d’Orléans and sample some regional specialty foods (see page 66-67). Head back to the city for dinner (if you still have room!) and get acquainted with cuisine inspired by the boreal forest or Indigenous traditions (see page 73).\nWhen it’s cold out, take a drive to see the one-of-a-kind Hôtel de Glace, and spend the afternoon at the Village Vacances Valcartier, North America’s biggest winter playground featuring 35 tubing slides and illuminated skateway. In the evening, throw on your bathing suit to enjoy the magical and relaxing atmosphere at one of the region’s many Nordic spas. FOR NATURE LOVERS If it’s snowing, head to Mont-Sainte-Anne for all kinds of activities, including downhill skiing, snowboarding, cross-country skiing, snowshoeing, backcountry skiing, dog sledding and even ice canyoning at the base of the mountain! Visiting in summer? The Parc national de la Jacques-Cartier is a must for an unforgettable hike. Feel like you haven't quite discovered all the secrets of Québec City? How about staying a little longer to really get to the heart of all the region has to offer? Follow the guide!\nHôtel de Glace Mont-Sainte-Anne Photos: Renaud Philippe (Hôtel de Glace), Francis Gagnon (snowboard), Emmanuel Coveney (right) 216 2 more days Planning on spending five days in Québec City? Add one of these seasonal itineraries onto the three-day suggestions and you’ll have the dream plan for discovering the national capital!\nSummer Day 4 Live like the locals Visit one of the area’s major attractions: the Aquarium du Québec. Meet more than 10,000 marine specimens and take a fascinating stroll through the Grand Océan tunnel.\nAt Village Vacances Valcartier, have fun at the outdoor water park on its 35 water slides and river rides. Unless you’d rather do the indoor water park, with its hints of Polynesia!\nIn Wendake, discover the ancestral Huron-Wendat culture through Indigenous dance, stories and legends. Visit a traditional long house and taste Indigenous foods (see page 32-33). For a unique evening, get off the beaten track with a visit to one of the neighbourhoods that locals love too, like Saint-Roch, Saint-Jean-Baptiste or Limoilou.\nDay 5 Replenish with open spaces or culture Nature lovers, take a beautiful hike at the Parc national de la Jacques-Cartier. And why not kayak or canoe down the river? Or visit the Vallée Bras-du-Nord adventure resort and explore nearly 100 kilometres of mountain biking trails.\nArts and culture connoisseurs, visit the Musée national des beaux-arts du Québec, home to the largest collection of Québecois art. Then hop over to the Musée de la civilisation where its innovative, interactive exhibits will completely absorb you. Still have some time? Double your enjoyment with a trip to the Musée de l’Amérique francophone and the Musée du Fort, which are right next to each other.\nFor your last evening, take a second look at the street or neighbourhood that most struck your fancy so you can really etch the ambiance in your memory. And don’t forget to try a poutine before you leave!\nPowwow, Wendake Petit-Champlain Photos: Les Festifs (Wendake), Jeff Frenette Photography (Petit-Champlain), Catherine Côté (German Christmas Market) 218 Winter Day 5 Fall in love with winter Did you know that some of the residents at the Aquarium du Québec adore winter? Step in to admire the seals, bears, walruses and more than 10,000 marine specimens. If you’re interested in Indigenous culture, you won’t want to miss Wendake and the Onhoüa Chetek8e Huron Traditional Site, to see the most authentic replica of an Indigenous village in the province. Love skiing? Spend at least half a day at one of the four downhill ski resorts in the area (see pages 132 to 133).\nMost importantly, don’t leave until you’ve tasted the famous poutine, a classic Québecois dish whose reputation has crossed the border. ABSOLUTE MUSTS Set time aside in your schedule to experience some of the major winter events, like the German Christmas Market (late November through December), the Holidays and New Year’s Eve celebrations on Grande Allée and the Québec Winter Carnaval (February). Day 4 Replenish with open spaces or culture Strap on snowshoes to discover the Parc national de la Jacques-Cartier, one of the most beautiful glacial valleys in Québec. If nature isn’t your cup of tea, plan a visit to the Musée national des beaux-arts du Québec or the Musée de la civilisation for some cultural immersion. Settle in with a good book in the stunning surroundings of the Maison de la littérature, then marvel at the neighbouring Morrin Centre’s Victorian library. Wrap up your day with hot and cold baths at one of the region’s Nordic spas (see page 48). It’s a good idea to relax on vacation too!\nGerman Christmas Market 219 Plan your stay A UNIQUE SHOPPING EXPERIENCE !\nMany Quebec products 45 BOUTIQUES RESTAURANTS & ENTERTAINMENTS quartierpetitchamplain.com For couples Share a romantic interlude at one of the city’s excellent cocktail bars, like Artefact at the Auberge Saint-Antoine or Le SAM at the Château Frontenac. Ice skate hand in hand at the Place D’Youville, or snuggle up on a horse-drawn carriage ride.\nFor families The Méga Parc amusement park at the Galeries de la Capitale mall offers 18 rides the whole family will love, and the Benjo toy store will thrill children and adults alike. For history buffs Travel back into the city’s military history with a visit to the Citadelle of Québec. Watch the changing of the guard and tour its exceptional museum. Then wander all over the extraordinary Plains of Abraham!\nFor foodies Stop by the Grand Marché de Québec public market to load up on local products and meet the passionate artisans who make them. And don’t miss a visit to the Saint-Roch neighbourhood, known for its adoration of food!\nMore ideas! Méga Parc des Galeries de la Capitale Photos: Jeff Frenette Photography (Méga Parc), Francis Fontaine (bus) Go on tour!\nDowntown is the doorway to everything you could desire. There’s a whole range of guided tours and visits available, whether you want a classic tour, a specific theme or something completely custom.\nBy theme Learn the history of the city straight from the mouths of its New France residents with a tour by the King’s daughters (À la Rencontre des Filles du Roy). Or get a fun thrill with a ghost walk (Les Promenades Fantômes or Les visites fantômes de Québec). You could always opt for a private guided tour by historian David Mendel, trusted city guide of dignitaries!\nThrough the stomach Find out all there is to know about the food scene with a food tour. It’s a delicious way to experience authentic culture in the city’s neighbourhoods or on Île d’Orléans, with Cicérone Tours, HQ Tourism Services, Local Québec City Food Tours, Québec Bus Tour (Île d’Orléans) or Broue-Tours (microbreweries).\nFrom a different angle Imagine exploring the city from above, in a helicopter with GoHelico or a plane with Québec Hydravion. Or take two wheels with Cyclo Services or Location Echo Sports. Unless you’d rather go for a romantic carriage ride with Calèches du Vieux-Québec, or take the picturesque train to the Charlevoix region. See the surrounding areas in a completely different way!\nOn a regal bus Get on and off at any of the 14 stops around the city as you ride on the red double-decker bus (Bus Tours of Old Québec). You can explore whatever piques your curiosity, then climb back aboard to move on!\n1 2 3 4 221 221 Plan your stay SAINT-FRANÇOIS SAINTE-FAMILLE SAINT-JEAN SAINT-PIERRE SAINT-LAURENT SAINTE-PÉTRONILLE SAGUENAY BAIE-SAINT-PAUL SAINTE-ANNE-DE-BEAUPRÉ QUÉBEC RIVIÈRE-DU-LOUP 368 40 138 Île d’Orléans Bridge St. La wr e n c e R iver 1 Parc maritime de Saint-Laurent 2 Manoir Mauvide-Genest 3 Confiturerie Tigidou 4 Maison Drouin 5 La Boulange 6 Espace Félix-Leclerc 7 Cassis Monna & Filles 8 Vignoble de l’Isle de Bacchus Île d’Orléans Île d’Orléans, just 15 minutes from downtown, right across from the Montmorency Falls, is well worth a visit! After crossing the island’s only bridge, you’ll immediately fall for this small island, where agriculture, culture and local cuisine will delight you year-round. Fall in love with this unique island, the cradle of french civilization in North America 1 5 4 3 2 8 7 6 222 THE TOP CHOICE OF Dominique Laflamme, Director of operations of a non-profit organization and resident of Saint-Laurent-de-l’Île-d’Orléans “I swear by the strawberries and raspberries from OhBio! at Jean-Pierre Plante’s farm in Saint-Laurent. They are grown with love by the fourth generation of the Plante family on that land, young farmers who innovate and try new things. Their products are sold at the stand in summer.” The tip of the island Called this by local islanders, the village of Sainte-Pétronille lays out an unobstructed view of the Montmorency Falls, the Baie de Beauport, Cap Diamant and the Château Frontenac. It’s one of the most popular viewpoints in the area!\nTreat yourself by stopping at the town’s vineyard, cider house and chocolate shop. While nibbling some divine chocolate, stretch your legs with a walk along along Rue Horatio-Walker to take in the river, or walk on the tidal flats!\nWelcome to treasure island!\nDrive around the entire island, taking time to admire the views. Head off counter-clockwise to discover its many treasures.\nFOOD LOVERS Some call the island the food basket of Québec City. Check out our gourmet suggestions on page 65 to 74 to chart an itinerary to tantalize your taste buds! 1 Photo: Benoit Camirand Chocolaterie de l’île d’Orléans 223 Plan your stay TOURIST INFORMATION Stop by the visitor information centre upon arriving on the island to get a guide or a multi-use map or to learn more about the island’s tourism routes and activities. tourisme.iledorleans.com How about them apples?
