The oldest parish of the island, Sainte-Famille provides an amazing view of the Côte-de-Beaupré and Mont-Sainte-Anne. Awaken your senses at its orchards, most of which allow you to pick your own. Continuing on, you’ll spot the largest grouping of stone houses from the French Regime, including the Maison Drouin, one of the first dwellings from the 17th century.\nWelcome to the garden You’ll notice that Saint-Laurent-de-l’Île-d’Orléans is home to countless vegetable farms and strawberry fields, for which the island is known.\nAround 1830, the parish had about 20 boat-builders and a few shipyards. You can learn about their history with a visit to the local Parc Maritime. Standing on the pier, with your eyes on the river and the cliffs of Lévis, take a deep breath of sea air. On your way to the next town, keep your eye out for the flour mill, built in 1715.\n4 2 3 Vignoble Ste-Pétronille A wealth of history At one time, most of the residents of Saint-Jean-de-l’Île-d’Orléans were ship pilots and sailors. You’ll see some of their houses, dating between 1825 and 1860, in the town centre. Take a guided tour of the Manoir Mauvide-Genest, a gem of the New France period, built in 1734. Saint-Jean boasts the largest strawberry farms in Canada, growing berries from spring to fall. It’s a beautiful place to taste the red fruit, whether fresh or processed, like at the Tigidou jam factory.\nAnother essential stop is at the riverside La Boulange for its delectable European-style pizza.\nPhotos: Benoit Camirand (boat), Francis Fontaine (vineyards, observation tour) Parc maritime, Saint-Laurent-de-l’Île-d’Orléans 224 1 833-663-9555 • hampton.com Close to the Old town Hot and cold luxury breakfast Meeting rooms Heated inside pool Free Wi-Fi Free outside parking Charging point for electric car Urban centre Since it’s close to the bridge, Saint-Pierre-de-l’Île-d’Orléans is the most accessible and populated area of the island. The Espace Félix-Leclerc is located here as a place to honour and present the music of this well-known Québecois singer and poet. Before leaving, pop into the artisans’ gift stores to taste cider, terrines and confits, apple butter and other local products, including the renowned blackcurrant liqueur made by island liqueur-maker Cassis Monna et Filles and the delicious products of the vine from the Île de Bacchus winery.\nA moment of observation Saint-François-de-l’Île-d’Orléans, at the island’s eastern end, attracts many tourists to its municipal rest area and observation tower, where you can spy the islands of the archipelago and the St. Lawrence estuary.\n5 6 225 Plan your stay PORTNEUF CAP-SANTÉ DONNACONA NEUVILLE DESCHAMBAULT-GRONDINES SAGUENAY BAIE-SAINT-PAUL RIVIÈRE-DU-LOUP 1 km = 0.6 mi 0 4 8 16 km MONTRÉAL USA SAINT-AUGUSTIN-DE-DESMAURES 138 175 Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré St. La w r enc e River Chemin du Roy The Chemin du Roy (King’s Road) is a marked road starting in Old Québec that showcases the rich heritage and history of Québec City. Built in 1731, the Chemin du Roy, which connects Québec City and Montréal, is the oldest drivable route in Canada. Discover Québec's wealth and heritage through different must-see attractions! 1 5 9 3 4 2 8 7 6 10 1 Parc de la Plage-Jacques-Cartier 2 Église de Sainte-Famille 3 Vieux Presbytère 4 Église Saint-Joseph 5 Moulin de La Chevrotière 6 Moulin à vent de Grondines 7 Marché public de Deschambault 8 Magasin général Paré 9 Julie Vachon Chocolats et crémerie artisanale 10 Aliksir huiles essentielles 226 You’ll find uniquely beautiful homes and gardens, and tasty places to stop along the way. In the Québec City region, the Chemin du Roy is mainly associated with the Portneuf area, where you’ll come across some of the most beautiful towns in Québec, like Neuville, Cap-Santé and Deschambault-Grondines. Why not break for a snack at the Parc de la Plage-Jacques-Cartier?\nFirst is Neuville, notable for its century homes and the patios facing the river. In summer, as you drive along Route 138 from Neuville (and the best corn on the cob!) to Donnacona, produce stands beckon you to stop and fill up on fresh local fruits and vegetables. Then you come to Cap-Santé, the proud home of one of the rare churches from the French Regime. In fact, the Sainte-Famille church, built between 1754 and 1767, is the largest church to have been constructed in a rural area. And the view of the river is stunning!\nLast stop, Deschambault-Grondines with its old Presbytère, built in 1815, and the splendid Saint-Joseph church. And don’t miss the nearby mills, Moulin de La Chevrotière and Moulin à vent de Grondines, which are registered historic monuments. The Deschambault public market is well worth a stop for local products. You’ll also want to see the Magasin Général Paré Tracing our history Travelling over this land-based twin of the St. Lawrence will take you through Québec’s history, with glimpses of gorgeous landscapes and fascinating history!\nPhoto: Yves Tessier, Les Productions Tessima 227 227 Plan your stay - SINCE 1737 -Running along the majestic St. Lawrence River and crossing three tourist regions of Quebec, the Chemin du Roy invites you to discover a rich historic route, with many must-see stops along the way! LECHEMINDUROY.COM TWO-WHEELED TOURING Another great way to explore the area’s picturesque towns is by bike. You can ride on the Chemin du Roy, which is also a bike route called the “Route verte,” in complete safety.\nPhoto: Jeff Frenette Photography (general store), Julie Vachon Chocolats et crémerie artisanale (chocolate and ice cream) and Aliksir huiles essentielles (essential oils). Since the area is known for its cheeses, definitely visit the Fromagerie des Grondines!\nlecheminduroy.com 228 BEAUPRÉ SAINTE-ANNE-DE-BEAUPRÉ CHÂTEAU-RICHER SAINT-JOACHIM L’ANGE-GARDIEN BOISCHATEL SAGUENAY BAIE-SAINT-PAUL RIVIÈRE-DU-LOUP St. La wr e n c e R iver 40 360 Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré Route de la Nouvelle-France This tourist route between Old Québec and Côte-de-Beaupré was mapped out by Monseigneur de Laval, then used by residents of Côte-de-Beaupré to transport food goods to the city.\nGet a real immersion in Québec's history by visiting one of the oldest arteries in North America! 5 3 4 2 8 7 6 1 1 Sanctuaire Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré 2 Aux Trois Couvents 3 Parc de la Chute-Montmorency 4 Atelier Paré 5 Ferme ancestrale du Comte de Roussy 6 Vignoble Domaine L’Ange-Gardien 7 Chez Marie 8 Auberge Baker 229 229 Plan your stay Follow this route steeped in history and heritage, and meet local artisans and artists along the way.\nGo ahead – friendly encounters and mouthwatering discoveries await!\nExplore Route de la Nouvelle-France at cotedebeaupre.com From Old Québec to Cap Tourmente ...\nAnd a few scrumptious stops Take the road through L’Ange-Gardien to Château-Richer to taste the local treasures. Start at the Comte de Roussy homestead with its patio overlooking the river, then stop in at the Vignoble Domaine L’Ange-Gardien vineyard and winery. Surrender to the tantalizing aromas of bread baking in a 150-year-old oven at Chez Marie, then conclude your food tour at the renowned Auberge Baker restaurant for a gourmet meal in the house dating back to 1776. routedelanouvellefrance.com Atelier Paré This wood-sculpture “economuseum” takes an original approach to incarnating Québecers’ joie de vivre with works that represent well-known Québec legends. Take your time envisioning the stories about werewolves, will-o’-the-wisp ghosts, forest demons and other characters who have populated the collective imagination for generations. 4 5 Domaine l’Ange-Gardien vineyard 231 Plan your stay Parc de la Chute-Montmorency Just a few minutes outside of the city, Parc de la Chute-Montmorency offers several high points to catch the view, including the thrilling via ferrata on the cliff-side and its zipline. A unique and exciting outing!\nAux Trois Couvents Located on the historical and archeological site of the Château-Richer convents, this interpretation centre presents the regional history of one of the first French settlements in North America. You’ll wonder at the archeological remains of the first two convents (1694 and 1830) and the reconstructed classroom from 1912. Take a break at the cultural café before you go!\nShrine of Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré Just 20 minutes from Québec City, the Shrine of Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré, one of five national shrines in the province, welcomes nearly one million visitors every year, and has been visited by pilgrims for more than 350 years. Explore this important house of worship that honours Saint Anne, Jesus’s grandmother, through paintings, mosaics, stained glass, and stone and wood sculptures. 3 2 1 You can still see ancestral homes, a heritage site and several interesting attractions, like a vineyard, farms, art galleries and more. Discover four centuries of bustling life! 5 stops along the Route de la Nouvelle-France Photos: André-Olivier Lyra (left), vignoble Domaine l’Ange-Gardien (right) Canyon Sainte-Anne 230 12, rue Donnacona, Québec (Québec) PoleCulturelDesUrsulines.ca Te expo where you play!\nWe grow up playing by Starting November 22th 2019 C M J CM MJ CJ CMJ N AVERAGE TEMPERATURES IN DEGREES CELSIUS High Low January -8 -18 February -6 -16 March 0 -9 April 8 -1 May 17 5 June 22 11 July 25 14 August 24 13 September 18 8 October 11 2 November 3 -4 December -4 -13 New Year’s Day January 1 Good Friday March or April Easter Monday March or April Victoria Day second to last Monday in May Québec’s National Day / Saint-Jean-Baptiste Day June 24 Canada Day July 1 Labour Day first Monday in September Thanksgiving second Monday in October Christmas December 25 STATUTORY HOLIDAYS Useful infos Québec City is located in the same time zone as New York City. Customers are expected to leave a 15% tip on the total, before taxes, in restaurants, bars and taxis.\nFrench is the official language of the province of Québec, but English is widely spoken in tourist areas.\nIn case of emergency, dial 911 (police, ambulance, fire) or *4141 for the public safety service.\nCanada uses the metric system. T emperatures are given in degrees Celsius, and speed limits are posted in kilometres per hour. Québec City has several free internet access points through ZAP Québec.\nThe legal currency is Canadian dollars.\nThere are two types of sales tax in Québec: the 5% federal goods and services tax (GST/ TPS) and the 9.975% provincial sales tax (PST/TVQ). Most prices are displayed without the taxes. Cannabis is legal in the province of Québec for consumers aged 21 or over. It is prohibited to use cannabis in the streets or in public areas. The Tobacco Control Act prohibits smoking (tobacco, cannabis and electronic cigarettes) in all public establishments including bars, restaurants and terraces.\nThe legal age to drink alcohol is 18 years old. Alcohol is sold at SAQ stores, convenience stores and grocery stores. In Québec, gasoline is sold by the litre (1 gallon = 3.8 litres). Winter tires are required between December 1 and March 15. However, if your vehicle is not registered in Québec, winter tires are not mandatory. It is recommended to have an international driver's licence if your valid licence is not in English or French. 232 Permanent Tourist Information Centres Open year-round Centre Infotouriste® de Québec 12 rue Sainte-Anne, Québec 1-877-BONJOUR (266-5687) quebecoriginal.com/en-ca Maison régionale du tourisme, Tourisme Chaudière-Appalaches Tourist Welcome Bureau 800 autoroute Jean-Lesage, Lévis 418-831-4411 | 1-888-831-4411 chaudiereappalaches.com/en Île d’Orléans Tourist Welcome Bureau 490 côte du Pont Saint-Pierre-de-l’Île-d’Orléans 1-866-941-9411 tourisme.iledorleans.com Saint-Raymond Tourist Welcome Bureau 160-2 place de l’Église, Saint-Raymond 1-855-337-6667 saintraymond.ca Tourisme Wendake Tourist Welcome Bureau 10 Place de la Rencontre, Wendake 1-888-WENDAKE (936-3253) tourismewendake.ca E v e r y t h i n g y o u n e e d t o p l a n y o u r s t a y !\nNetwork of tourist information centres Personalized service by qualified staff Official tourist guides to Quebec regions Suggested itineraries Full details on attractions, events and accommodation Did you know... tourist information agents ride electric bikes or walk around the Old Québec area to give information? Laurier, entrance 4B Québec City quebec-cite.com Contact Québec City Tourism 1-877-783-1608 | 418-641-6290 quebec-cite.com info@quebec-cite.com Walking Old Québec has two sections: Upper Town and Lower Town. From Rue du Petit-Champlain, a ride in the funicular is the perfect way to easily get from one section to the other. The free Faubourg elevator connects the Saint-Roch neighbourhood in Lower Town to the Saint-Jean-Baptiste district in Upper Town, but you can also take one of the many stairways to get from one place to another. Car rental If you’d like to rent a car, there are several companies to choose from in the Québec City area, including Avis, Budget, Discount, Enterprise and Hertz.\nPublic transit Québec City’s public busses (run by the Réseau de transport de la Capitale, or RTC) serve most tourist attractions. There are several types of tickets to choose from that allow unlimited trips (one day, unlimited weekend or five consecutive days). Children 12 and under travelling with a paying parent always have free access.\nTaxis You’ll recognize taxis in the Québec City area by the small dome on the car roof. You can call a taxi company or ask an employee at your hotel’s reception desk, a restaurant or an attraction to call for you. To reach Taxi Coop Québec, dial 418-525-5191.\nBike The Québec City region offers nearly 400 kilometres of bike paths and routes. Bike rental: Cyclo Services, Demers bicyclettes, Location Echo Sports, Vélos Roy-O, Québec Aventure Tours (Île d’Orléans), Éco Plein Air (Saint-Raymond).\nWHERE TO BUY RTC TICKETS You can pay your way on the bus with exact change. Tickets are available at most convenience stores and drug stores: look for the sign in the window. Getting around Photos: Jeff Frenette Photography 236 By foot, bike or bus, Québec City is incredible to discover, no matter how you do it! Ferry The ferry between Québec City and Lévis runs daily, all year long. It departs every 30 minutes during the day and every hour at night. Airport The Québec City Jean Lesage International Airport is about 15 kilometres from downtown Québec City. It’s easy to get to and from using public transit (bus lines 76 and 80) or by taxi. Train All trains serve both stations in Québec City: the Gare du Palais in Lower Town (RTC bus lines 800 and 1) and the Gare de Sainte-Foy (RTC line 76).\nBus terminals (intercity transport) All long-distance busses serve both bus terminals in Québec City: the Gare du Palais terminal (same location as the train station, RTC lines 800 and 1) and the Sainte-Foy bus station (RTC Métrobus 800 and 801).\nParking Most downtown and nearby streets have parking meters or allow parking for a specific period of time. The free Copilote application can be used to pay the meters remotely (iOS and Android).\nThere are also several paid parking lots. Parking during snow clearing operations: From November 1 to April 15, parking is prohibited on certain streets during snow clearing operations. Drivers are notified of parking restrictions by the blinking orange lights installed throughout the city.\n237 Plan your stay Old Québec/ Inside the walls ACCOMMODATIONS Bed and Breakfast Le Coureur     {
      "url": "https://www.itsfivehere.com/where-eat-drink-in-quebec-city",
      "title": "Where to Eat and Drink in Québec City Right Now - It's Five O'Clock Here",
      "content": "For those who don’t like foie gras (we also realize that the food itself is controversial), try their Release the Kraken cocktail (CAD $16). Featuring Kraken spiced rum, Bacardi Oakheart, lime and mace syrup, lime leaf bitters, ginger beer, and honey-flavored whipped cream, it’ll transport you to a tropical island in the springtime. (Note that their menu changes seasonally; check Bistro Le Sam’s website in advance.)\n\nBistro Le Sam produces excellent cocktails. It's one of the best places to drink in Quebec City.\n\nWhipped cream in a cocktail does a body good.\n\n## Bistro L’Atelier\n\nAddress: 624 Grande Allée E, Québec, QC G1R 2K5, Canada [...] Take Le Fruit Défendu (CAD $28), for instance. While the cocktail itself (Christian Drouin’s Gin de Calvados, Amaro Montenegro, Tonka-flavored apple butter, lemon juice, maple syrup, fig & cinnamon bitters, and speculoos) is well balanced, the kicker here is that the cocktail is actually served with a side of foie gras. If that doesn’t scream decadence, I don’t know what does! [...] Address: 1200 Rue Saint-Jean, Québec, QC G1R 1S8, Canada\n\n My friend Lauren of Twirl the Globe had seen a “massive red cocktail” on Instagram, and after scrolling through a dozen photos of the larger-than-life drink, we knew we had to go. (It also helped that it was Cinco de Mayo that day — just one more reason to celebrate.)\n\nIt may have taken a small army to vanquish the “Grosse Rita” cocktail — all right, three people in total — but it was completely worth it. Add to that some mouthwatering food (read: a decadent burger with pork cheek bacon, sautéed mushrooms, and melted brie cheese) and the fact that we ate dinner while sitting on a chair swing, and you have a recipe for a real winner here.\n\n## Bistro Le Sam\n\nAddress:1 Rue des Carrières, Québec, QC G1R 4P5, Canada",
      "score": 0.35019964,
      "raw_content": "[![It's Five O'Clock Here](https://www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2016/01/websitelogo_65perred.png)](https://www.itsfivehere.com/ \"It's Five O'Clock Here\")\n\n# Where to Eat and Drink in Québec City Right Now\n\n![](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010006-e1536037938923.jpg?fit=750%2C563&ssl=1)\n\n*Some posts on this site contain affiliate links, meaning if you book or buy something through one of these links, I may earn a small commission (at no extra cost to you!). Opinions are always my own and I’ll never promote something I don’t use or believe in.*\n\n---\n\nA long time ago in my college days of yore, a friend returned from winter break with tales of a strange land up north. “They speak French, but it’s not in Europe,” he waved his hands wildly. “They have this one hotel that looks like a huge castle, and there’s fries with these cheese things and gravy. And get this — they have another hotel made completely of ice!” Suffice it to say that I’ve been dreaming about Québec City ever since.\n\nFlash forward fifteen years later: when I learned that the annual [Women in Travel Summit](https://www.sheswanderful.com/witsummit/) was taking place in Québec City in 2018, I knew it was the perfect time to go. Québec City — and its hip food and drink scene — surpassed my expectations, and I can’t wait to share all these spots with you. **So without further ado, here’s your ultimate guide to where to eat and drink in Québec City.**\n\nTo get started, here’s a nifty map of where you’ll be eating and drinking in Québec City.\n\n↓ Hint: *Click on the square with an arrow to see the key and layers.*\n\n# Where to Drink in Québec City: Craft Beer Edition\n\n## Noctem Artisans Brasseurs\n\n*Address:* 438 Rue du Parvis, Québec, QC G1K 6H8, Canada\n\n[![Noctem Artisans Brasseurs has great craft beer, and it's one of the best places to drink in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010084.jpg?resize=650%2C488&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010084.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nBelzébuth, our favorite beer at Noctem, is a stout featuring flavors of grapefruit and red pepper.\n\n**It was love at first “cat sighting” for us at Noctem Artisans Brasseurs, a microbrewery you’ve got to hit in Québec City.** Beyond the fact that their beer is on point — standouts here included Belzébuth (6% ABV) and Herbosophie (6% ABV; a Saison with sage, rosemary, and thyme) — the brewery has a hilarious, yet endearing, cat theme. You’ll find subtle decor of their four-legged friends all over (great for us, as owners of two cats), but their excellent craft beer will have you lingering for hours.\n\n[![Check out the cat decor at Noctem Artisans Brasseurs, one of the best places to drink in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010085.jpg?resize=650%2C488&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010085.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nMeow! Cats can be found in the most unexpected places at Noctem. (Don’t worry, there aren’t actual cats on the premises, though we wouldn’t object.)\n\n[![](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010086-2.jpg?resize=500%2C667&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010086-2.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nThe sign on the women’s restroom at Noctem takes Cat Lady to a whole new level.\n\n## La Barberie\n\n*Address:* 310 Rue Saint-Roch, Québec, QC G1K 6S2, Canada\n\n[![La Barberie is one of the best places to drink beer in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010087.jpg?resize=650%2C488&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010087.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nLa Barberie, here we come!\n\n**Founded in 1995, La Barberie is a brewery in the Saint-Roch district of Québec City.** Interestingly, there’s no boss here: La Barberie is a cooperative enterprise, with co-op “workers” running the joint.\n\nI really enjoyed samples of their Rousse Anglaise (English Red) and the Mathieu New England IPA, though their Pilsner Bohemienne and Stout Double Chocolat were also tasty. Bonus points for serving beer in this crazy contraption — it made my head spin!\n\n[![La Barberie has amazing craft beer, and is one of the best places to drink in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010094.jpg?resize=650%2C487&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010094.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nIt’s the Carousel of Progress — er, I mean beer!\n\n[![La Barberie serves excellent craft beer and is one of the best places to drink in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/La-Barberie_Quebec-City.jpg?resize=650%2C520&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/La-Barberie_Quebec-City.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nBeer, glorious beer.\n\n## La Korrigane\n\n*Address:* 380 Rue Dorchester, Québec, QC G1K 6A7, Canada\n\n[![Korrigane produces excellent craft beer in Quebec City. It's one of the best places to drink in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010102.jpg?resize=650%2C488&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010102.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nRed beer! (Actually, it’s their Saison Aux Framboises.)\n\n**Also located in Québec’s Saint-Roch neighborhood, La Korrigane derives its name from a sailboat which departed from France and journeyed across the Pacific Ocean from 1934 to 1936.** The brewery serves a long list of craft beer, from the Saison Aux Framboises (a tart, delicious Saison with raspberry flavors) to Cornik, an Oatmeal Stout Cream Ale.\n\nAs [WITS](https://www.sheswanderful.com/witsummit/) attendees, we were thrilled to discover that La Korrigane is owned by a woman and focuses on fostering positive development in Québec’s craft beer scene. A craft brewery that understands the need for sustainable development? *C’est incroyable!*\n\n***Pro Tip ⇒*** I highly recommend **taking a Québec City craft beer tour with [Broue Tours](https://www.broue-tours.ca/en/),** which visits the three craft breweries listed above. I received a complimentary tour as a WITS attendee, but all opinions are completely mine. Not even 12 beer samples can change that.\n\n#### Honorable Mention\n\nThese three craft breweries are just the tip of the iceberg when it comes to where to drink beer in Québec City. Other spots recommended to us by locals included **Brasserie L’Inox,** **L’Archibald,** and **Corsaire Microbrasserie,** but there’s plenty more to try!\n\n# Where to Drink in Québec City: Cocktails Edition\n\n## Batinse\n\n*Address:* 1200 Rue Saint-Jean, Québec, QC G1R 1S8, Canada\n\n[![](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-2657.jpg?resize=500%2C667&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-2657.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nI am in this shot solely to demonstrate the sheer size of this drink. Seriously. It took three people to finish this cocktail!\n\nI’ll be honest: we originally visited Batinse for the ‘gram. (I know, I know.) My friend Lauren of [Twirl the Globe](http://www.twirltheglobe.com/) had seen a “massive red cocktail” on Instagram, and after scrolling through a dozen photos of the larger-than-life drink, we knew we had to go. (It also helped that it was Cinco de Mayo that day — just one more reason to celebrate.)\n\n**It may have taken a small army to vanquish the “Grosse Rita” cocktail — all right, three people in total — but it was completely worth it.** Add to that some mouthwatering food (read: a decadent burger with pork cheek bacon, sautéed mushrooms, and melted brie cheese) and the fact that we ate dinner while sitting on a chair swing, and you have a recipe for a real winner here.\n\n## Bistro Le Sam\n\n*Address:*1 Rue des Carrières, Québec, QC G1R 4P5, Canada\n\n[![This cocktail is called Le Fruit Défendu, and you can find it at Bistro Le Sam in Quebec City. Bistro Le Sam is one of the best places to drink in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010006.jpg?resize=650%2C488&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010006.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nCocktail with a side of foie gras? Don’t mind if I do.\n\nBistro Le Sam, located in the [Fairmont Le Chteau Frontenac](https://www.booking.com/hotel/ca/fairmont-chateau-frontenac.en.html?aid=1234597&no_rooms=1&group_adults=1), is one of the best places to drink in Québec City. Not only are the views of the Saint Lawrence River stunning, but the cocktails are as creative as any we’ve seen in NYC.\n\nTake Le Fruit Défendu (CAD $28), for instance. While the cocktail itself (Christian Drouin’s Gin de Calvados, Amaro Montenegro, Tonka-flavored apple butter, lemon juice, maple syrup, fig & cinnamon bitters, and speculoos) is well balanced, **the kicker here is that the cocktail is actually served with a side of foie gras.** If that doesn’t scream decadence, I don’t know what does!\n\nFor those who don’t like foie gras (we also realize that the food itself is controversial), try their Release the Kraken cocktail (CAD $16). Featuring Kraken spiced rum, Bacardi Oakheart, lime and mace syrup, lime leaf bitters, ginger beer, and honey-flavored whipped cream, it’ll transport you to a tropical island in the springtime. (Note that their menu changes seasonally; check Bistro Le Sam’s website in advance.)\n\n[![Bistro Le Sam produces excellent cocktails. It's one of the best places to drink in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-2579-2.jpg?resize=500%2C667&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-2579-2.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nWhipped cream in a cocktail does a body good.\n\n## Bistro L’Atelier\n\n*Address:* 624 Grande Allée E, Québec, QC G1R 2K5, Canada\n\nThough I didn’t personally get the chance to stop by Bistro L’Atelier, I spoke to several bloggers who raved about the cocktails they drank here. Favorites included L’Alpha, a cocktail including Campari, Patron tequila, and raspberries; and the Bloody Mutant Ninja Turtle, which is kind of like a Bloody Mary with sake and a host of other surprise ingredients.\n\n#### Honorable Mention\n\nSome other cocktail bars you’ll want to check out in Québec City include **Bar Artefact**, which proudly disproves the notion that bars in hotel lobbies are terrible, and **Bar Sainte-Angèle**, which boasts live jazz on certain nights.\n\n# Where to Drink in Québec City: When You Want to Have a Fun Night Out Edition\n\n## Pub Saint-Alexandre\n\n*Address:* 1087 Rue Saint-Jean, Québec, QC G1R 1S3, Canada\n\n[![Pub Saint-Alexandre is one of the best spots to drink in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-2729.jpg?resize=650%2C488&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-2729.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nPromise us you’ll get the French onion soup.\n\n**Pub Saint-Alexandre is everything you want in a good night out:** live music, french onion soup, the company of good friends, and Québécois beer. (What? Toto, I’m not in my 20s any more. Going clubbing until 3 AM is not my definition of a good night out.)\n\nIn all seriousness, if I lived in Québec City, Pub Saint-Alexandre would be my go-to spot on weekends. Staff are friendly, the music gets people movin’, and the French onion soup really does warm your bones. ‘Nuff said.\n\n## Bar Le Sacrilège\n\n*Address:* 447 Rue Saint-Jean, Québec, QC G1R 1P5, Canada\n\nHmm, suspicious that I don’t have a picture of this place? That’s because we actually *did* have a late night out here (contrary to popular belief, thirtysomethings can pull one of these once in a blue moon). Secret intelligence suggests that I may have blurry cell phone photos of people having fun here, but the world may never know.\n\n**What I will say is that Bar Le Sacrilège is a popular spot for locals and tourists alike.** By night, you’ll find a laidback crowd dancing to ’90s tunes and drinking affordable beer. By day, you’ll notice that the seats are church pews and — gasp — green potted plants actually hang from the ceiling!\n\n## Taverne Grande Allée\n\n*Address:*585 Grande Allée E, Québec, QC G1R 2K4, Canada\n\nAnother excellent choice for a late night out in Québec City, the fun doesn’t stop at Taverne Grande Allée until 3 AM. With its exposed brick walls, cozy ambiance, and affordable fare (special appearances by poutine and bitterballen), it’s a place that welcomes both your nightlife-loving friend and your coworker who just wants to sip tea and talk.\n\n#### Honorable Mention\n\nIf nightclubs are what you’re after, then your best bets in Québec City include Le Dagobert and Maurice Nightclub.\n\n# Where to Eat in Québec City\n\nSince it’s never advisable to drink on an empty stomach, may we humbly present some pretty darn good restaurant options to choose from in Québec City.\n\n## Le Chic Shack\n\n*Address:* 15 Fort St, Québec City, QC G1R 3Z8, Canada\n\n[![La Braisee poutine at Le Chic Shack. Le Chic Shack is one of the best places to eat in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010010.jpg?resize=650%2C487&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010010.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nPoutine… mmmm.\n\nWe may have stopped here for dinner because it was close to the [Fairmont Le Chteau Frontenac](https://www.booking.com/hotel/ca/fairmont-chateau-frontenac.en.html?aid=1234597&no_rooms=1&group_adults=1), but Le Chic Shack is worth seeking out regardless of where you’re staying.\n\nOpen since 2012, Le Chic Shack is known for their poutine, burgers, shakes, and salads crafted from local ingredients. Top picks here include La Braisée — poutine with red ale-braised beef, Parmesan, cheese curds, pickled onions, horseradish aioli, and fresh herbs — and their boozy milkshakes. Maple salted caramel milkshake with maple whisky? *Oui, oui!*\n\n## Chez Ashton\n\n*Address:* Various locations; we visited the one at 54 Côte du Palais, Québec, QC G1R 4H8, Canada\n\n[![Chez Ashton has incredible poutine. Chez Ashton is one of the best spots to eat in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-2728.jpg?resize=650%2C488&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-2728.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nMmmm. More poutine, please!\n\nBecause we all know that poutine is one of the six major food groups, it should come as no surprise that we’ve got another poutine entry on this list.\n\nLike Taylor Swift songs, college football, and that [movie with Tommy Wiseau](https://amzn.to/2NicAey), poutine wasn’t something I originally liked until I had a pint of beer. Then the universe shifted, the stars aligned, and I *got it.*Cheese curds, gravy, and fries? *Mais bien sûr, mon chéri!* Makes perfect sense.\n\nWant to try poutine, but don’t have the time to wait? Chez Ashton is the perfect choice: they’re fast, and their cheese curds are made fresh daily. Plus, it’s open 24 hours, so you have no excuse *not* to go. As David Tennant would say in *Doctor Who*, “Allons-y!”\n\n## BeClub Bistro Bar\n\n*Address:* 17 Rue Saint-Stanislas, Québec, QC G1R 4G7, Canada\n\n[![BeClub Bistro is one of the best spots to eat in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-2584-2.jpg?resize=500%2C667&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-2584-2.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nPure coincidence that our wine totally matches our food.\n\nYou know you’re eating a memorable meal when the only sound you hear is yourself chewing. Every brain cell I had was laser-focused on this **insanely good smoked meat sandwich** with a side of poutine (topped with, you guessed it, smoked meat) that I had for lunch at BeClub Bistro. It was filling, but damn, it exploded with flavor. Bonus: they have gluten-free options available if needed!\n\n## L’Échaudé\n\n*Address:* 73 Rue du Sault-au-Matelot, Québec, QC G1K 3Y9, Canada\n\n[![L'Échaudé is one of the best places to eat in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010181.jpg?resize=650%2C488&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010181.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nBone Marrow at L’Échaudé\n\n**L’Échaudé is a classy, upscale French bistro in the Lower Town of Old Québec** that’s perfect for date night. From the salmon tartare and tender bone marrow to the scallops, homemade black pudding, and roasted duck breast, there’s something to please everyone’s palette.\n\n*Pro tip:* there’s no way you’ll be able to pick just one option from the dessert list, so save yourself the trouble and order the dessert tasting plate for two. Double your pleasure, double your fun!\n\n[![Incredible dessert platter at L'Échaudé, which is one of the best places to eat in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-2675-2.jpg?resize=500%2C667&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-2675-2.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nDesserts for two — or four?\n\n## Le Moine Échanson\n\n*Address:* 585 Rue Saint-Jean, Québec, QC G1R 1P7, Canada\n\n[![Le Moine Echanson is one of the best places to eat in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010264.jpg?resize=500%2C667&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/08/Quebec-1010264.jpg?ssl=1)\n\nSicilian lamb and anchovy meatballs at Le Moine Échanson\n\n**If you’re an oenophile, look no further than Le Moine Échanson in Québec City.** This bistro serves upscale French cuisine alongside a diverse array of natural wines. We particularly loved the 2016 Le Clos Des Grillons Primo Senso Rouge from Côtes du Rhône in France (wow, that’s a mouthful). Staff here have encyclopedic knowledge of wine and can help you select a wine that suits your tastes — and, thankfully, won’t judge you if you can’t pronounce it.\n\n#### Honorable Mention\n\nOf the restaurants I missed in Québec City, **Paillard** came up most frequently. Their breakfast, especially their croissants, earned glowing praises from fellow travel bloggers. **Chez Muffy** and its farm-to-fork cuisine comes highly recommended as well.\n\n***Essential Info:***  \n **Where should you stay in Québec City?** Check out all options **[here](https://www.booking.com/searchresults.en.html?city=-571851&aid=1234597&no_rooms=1&group_adults=1).**  \n I personally stayed at [Fairmont Le Chteau Frontenac](https://www.booking.com/hotel/ca/fairmont-chateau-frontenac.en.html?aid=1234597&no_rooms=1&group_adults=1)and the [Hilton Québec](https://www.booking.com/hotel/ca/hilton-quebec.en.html?aid=1234597&no_rooms=1&group_adults=1).  \n **Want a guidebook for Québec City?** Check out [Fodor’s](https://amzn.to/2MUxtfZ), [Moon](https://amzn.to/2LEb7KT), or [Lonely Planet](https://amzn.to/2wvHs10).  \n **What else have I written about Canada?** Find out [here](https://www.itsfivehere.com/category/destinations/north-america/canada/).  \n **Have questions for me?** Hit me up on [Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/itsfivehere/), [Twitter](https://twitter.com/itsfivehere), [Instagram](https://www.instagram.com/itsfivehere/), or [Pinterest](https://www.pinterest.com/itsfivehere/)!\n\n***Did you like this post? Pin it! ↓↓***\n\n[![From beer and cocktails to poutine and dessert, here is what you'll want to eat and drink in Quebec City.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/09/LEchaude_QuebecCity.png?resize=735%2C1102&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/09/LEchaude_QuebecCity.png?ssl=1)\n\n[![Sure, there's delicious beer and poutine, but what makes Québec City a foodie's paradise? Here's where you need to eat and drink in Québec City right now.](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/09/Spots-to-Eat-and-Drink-Quebec.png?resize=735%2C1102&ssl=1)](https://i0.wp.com/www.itsfivehere.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/09/Spots-to-Eat-and-Drink-Quebec.png?ssl=1)\n\n### *Related*\n\n### 14 thoughts on “Where to Eat and Drink in Québec City Right Now”\n\n1. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/98dda33d01d5d20cb1f74a37489fd7efe9675202d1fbbf2101403cd1a8f20045?s=74&d=mm&r=g)\n\n   ### [Justin](https://madhattersnyc.com)\n\n    \n\n   Great post, Julianne! I really only like to drink dark beer, ever since Prauge and pilsner. Then as I was salivating I saw the smoked meat sandwich, can you say HEAVEN!! I didn’t realize that Quebec was such a foodie town!\n\n   [Reply](#comment-1573)\n7. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/e117f3ed0573dcbd58c047d891de503423dedfb6692daa13c934cd5141ea5d0e?s=74&d=mm&r=g)\n\n   ### Cat\n\n    \n\n   Wow! You drank a lot in Quebec City! You have also found some “cats” there! I didn’t really drink much beer or cocktail, but I got my fair share of icewine and ice cider 😛 Next time, I will need to hit up some of these places!\n\n   [Reply](#comment-1574)\n8. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/d9768370e851cadbd0877b0337014ad1838e814269841edd88506c5525110974?s=74&d=mm&r=g)\n\n   ### [Kimberly Erin Davies](https://www.walkaboot.ca)\n\n    \n\n   I am not much of a drinker…but what I am is Canadian. I can seriously go for some of these poutine options, because I know how great poutine is. I mean, Quebec does it the best, looks delicious\n\n   [Reply](#comment-1575)\n9. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/b2eaac05dc05d783f92c99247d0623adbcd0faf511464bf1da0a6904c4396307?s=74&d=mm&r=g)\n\n   ### [Andi](https://misadventureswithandi.com)\n\n    \n\n   I can’t even. I don’t even know where to start. Montreal and Quebec have long been on my bucket list and I was devastated when I could not get to WITs this year, which of course was the perfect opportunity to eat, eat, eat and okay, maybe drink a little to. Being a huge francophile it is the most accessible North American option and I am dying to go there!\n\n   [Reply](#comment-1577)\n10. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/7f8919aec3b6633340cdecaa28c61e6980273365f2115b1ace9457fea05cb0fe?s=74&d=mm&r=g)\n\n    ### [Rebecca](https://www.rebeccaandtheworld.com)\n\n     \n\n    Well, it looks like all my eating is planned out for me when I finally get to Quebec!! I’ve also subscribed to your blog – love the name!\n\n    [Reply](#comment-1578)\n11. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/6838b8f08db1dea58d9a6e5d53bfa7eadbdefb4c6cafe0bc00cb55f87930837e?s=74&d=mm&r=g)\n\n    ### [Ann](https://anncavittfisher.com/)\n\n     \n\n    Well, your whole post is super-Instagrammable! But you’re right — that huge cocktail — the Grossa Rita. Well. You had several people with you — just have to do it :-). Worth the photograph. But I’d have to say, the Belzébuth stout would be the one I’d be going after. And then the bone marrow at L’Échaudé.\n\n    [Reply](#comment-1579)\n12. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/53073aa18d86baf29fa78e8777f6d13efae7c6bd2949c8cb147f185e69a0ed9f?s=74&d=mm&r=g)\n\n    ### [Elaine Masters](https://www.tripwellgal.com)\n\n     \n\n    Loved my experience of WITS and Quebec. Wish I’d gone bar-hopping more. Stellar spots. So funny about the cats! Great photos too. Hope you weren’t too cold on the trip. I was thankful for my clunky insulated coat. Must return and try more of the city’s drinking scene.\n\n    [Reply](#comment-1580)\n13. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/0a4caea97a662f89f72e751ff24439a6275de37beb387167b09f1ad82087bc09?s=74&d=mm&r=g)\n\n    ### [Ashley Hubbard](https://asoutherngypsy.com)\n\n     \n\n    Umm can you just drop me off at the cat themed one and pick me up at the end of the trip? kthanks. Also, I want one of those grosse ritas so maybe combine the two haha. Seriously, that drink is ridiculous. How did you feel afterwards?\n\n    [Reply](#comment-1581)\n14. ![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/d4e21e1bf77b4cd18d9a1facacf762ec5a908ea9727ce98e28d9f9f25a6fed4c?s=74&d=mm&r=g)\n\n    ### Naomi Brown\n\n     \n\n    I love the beautiful French dishes of Le Moine Échanson.       "url": "https://www.quebec-cite.com/en/restaurants-quebec-city/best-cafes",
      "title": "The Best Cafés in Québec City | Visit Québec City",
      "content": "Étienne Dionne\n\n  3\n\n  ## Cantook\n\n  Cantook is everyone’s favourite coffee shop in Saint-Jean-Baptiste. As the city’s first local, small-batch roaster, it’s also the third-wave capital of Québec City. Everything that’s sold in a bag and served over the counter is roasted on-site in this gorgeous neighbourhood café, named Best Coffee Shop in Québec City in 2020 by Th3rdwave. Now that’s some bang for your buck! [...] 10\n\n  ## Saint-Henri micro-torréfacteur\n\n  If you want to catch the vibe in one of Québec City’s hippest neighbourhoods, head to the end of Rue Saint-Joseph. This bright and airy café features huge windows great for reading and studying, which may be why it’s so popular with students. Or maybe it’s the amazing doughnuts made on site by Sumo Beignerie Artisanale. Yum! The coffee served here is expertly roasted at the company’s small-batch roastery in Montréal. Free Wi-Fi.\n + Philtre café\n\n    Philtre café\n\n  11\n\n  ## Philtre Café [...] Café Pekoe\n\n  9\n\n  ## Café Pékoe\n\n  Québec City’s most Italian coffee shop also specializes in bubble tea and serves up delicious banh mi, a type of Vietnamese sandwich. There are also cookies and other homemade treats to go along with your coffee, which is made with beans from Union, a Montréal roastery where Café Pékoe’s owner learned to sling single-shots with the best of them. Free Wi-Fi and the washrooms are wheelchair accessible.\n + Café Saint-Henri\n\n    Café Saint-Henri\n\n  10\n\n  ## Saint-Henri micro-torréfacteur",
      "score": 0.33895233,
      "raw_content": "0  Start your search\n\n# The Best Cafés in Québec City\n\n![Allison Van Rassel](/sites/otq/files/styles/author_preview/public/media/image/Allison_van_rassel_0.JPG?itok=2zTPf4Yn)\n\n Allison Van Rassel\n\nUpdated on **May 14, 2026**\n\n![Macaroons and coffee tasting on a terrace of La Maison Smith, in summer.](/sites/otq/files/styles/landscape_wide_desktop/public/blog/jefffrenettephotography_otqete_160819_0024.jpg?itok=Zr_G4akf)\n\nJeff Frenette Photography\n\nStart the day off right or take a moment later on to stop and smell the java in one of the best coffee shops in Québec City — specifically, in the heart of town. All are third-wave and use specialty beans, some of which are roasted on-site. If you’re wondering where to drink coffee in Québec City, read on.\n\n* + ![A couple drink a coffee next to Notre-Dame-des-Victoires Church, on the terrace of Maison Smith in Place Royale.](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 850 638'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n    Jeff Frenette Photography\n\n  1\n\n  ## La Maison Smith at Place Royale\n\n  Almost every neighbourhood in Québec City has a [Maison Smith](/en/businesses/la-maison-smith-place-royale). But the original Maison Smith at [Place Royale](/en/what-to-do-quebec-city/place-royale) is a particularly gorgeous spot to have a coffee in Old Québec. The historic square is home to Notre-Dame-des-Victoires, one of the oldest churches in North America. So pull up a seat on the patio or by the window and enjoy the beauty of your surroundings. The taste of coffee made with beans hand-roasted on site can only add to the experience! There are two other Maison Smith cafés in Old Québec, on Rue Saint-Jean and Rue des Jardins. Free Wi-Fi.\n* + ![Café Faux Mouvement](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 850 638'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n    Café Faux Mouvement\n\n  2\n\n  ## Faux Mouvement\n\n  [Faux Mouvement](https://fauxmouvement.cc/) is a running store that doubles as a third-wave coffee shop. Located in the [Petit-Champlain](/en/old-quebec-city/petit-champlain), in beautiful Old Québec, it’s become a gathering place for runners: a place where you can shop for running clothes and accessories, sip on a specialty coffee, and enjoy the clean lines and natural stone walls of this gorgeous, modern space. The coffee is made with beans from Pilot Coffee Roasters in Toronto, one of Canada’s third-wave pioneers.\n* + ![Cantook](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 850 638'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n    Étienne Dionne\n\n  3\n\n  ## Cantook\n\n  [Cantook](/en/businesses/cantook-micro-torrefaction) is everyone’s favourite coffee shop in [Saint-Jean-Baptiste](/en/neighbourhoods-quebec-city/saint-jean-baptiste). As the city’s first local, small-batch roaster, it’s also the third-wave capital of Québec City. Everything that’s sold in a bag and served over the counter is roasted on-site in this gorgeous neighbourhood café, named Best Coffee Shop in Québec City in 2020 by Th3rdwave. Now that’s some bang for your buck!\n\n  The beans and brews vary depending on what’s available, and the service is outstanding thanks to a roster of bright young coffee aficionados. There’s also a selection of fine coffee-brewing paraphernalia and local creations. If you’re down in Saint-Sauveur, Cantook recently opened a second location there, so now you have two chances to try some of the best coffee in Québec City.\n* + ![deTerroir café](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 850 638'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n    deTerroir\n\n  4\n\n  ## deTerroir Café\n\n  [deTerroir Café](https://deterroir.cafe/fr) is recognized as much for its high-quality locally roasted coffee beans, as for its outstanding customer service. Focused on environmental sustainability, all their coffee can be traced back to its point of origin.\n* 5\n\n  ## Café Olive\n\n  Try a [coffee shop](https://www.instagram.com/cafeolivequebec/) that’s surfing the third wave in Québec City’s arts district. This cute café on Avenue Cartier in [Montcalm](/en/neighbourhoods-quebec-city/montcalm) is inspired by the surfer lifestyle, with a cozy, minimalist interior and simple furnishings. There are a few places to sit and a tiny patio in the summer. The coffee is made with beans from La Cabra, a roastery in Aarhus, Danemark.\n* + ![musette](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 850 638'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n    musette\n\n  6\n\n  ## musette\n\n  [musette](https://musettecc.ca/) is the place to be for bike and coffee enthusiasts. It’s a boutique-workshop for high-end bicycles that also offers specialty coffees. Enjoy coffees roasted by Ontario’s Hatch Roaster as well as local craft beers and privately imported wines.\n* + ![Maelstrøm Saint-Roch - Un décor chaleureux et invitant](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 850 638'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n  7\n\n  ## Maelstrøm Saint-Roch\n\n  With a daily rotation of specialty coffees and a cocktail bar in the evening, [Maelstrøm](https://maelstrom.cafe/) is a [Saint-Roch](/en/neighbourhoods-quebec-city/saint-roch) hotspot, day and night. Come dinner time, their short-but-smart food menu has something for everyone, while the sophisticated cocktail menu makes judicious use of Maelstrøm’s signature cold brew. If you’re there for weekend brunch, try the homemade veggie paté and the scrambled tofu burrito with Tex-Mex spices. What a wakeup! The coffee is made with beans from Escape Coffee Roasters, with a house blend that’s perfect for cortados. Free Wi-Fi.\n* + ![Nektar torrefacteur](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 850 638'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n    Nektar\n\n  8\n\n  ## Nektar Torréfacteur\n\n  [Nektar](https://nektar.ca/) has long been considered one of the best coffee shops in Québec City. Its two locations, in Limoilou and Saint-Roch, are bright and welcoming and popular with students. The one in [Saint-Roch](/en/neighbourhoods-quebec-city/saint-roch) is the original Nektar — a precursor to third-wave specialty coffee shops in Québec City. All of the grains are roasted in-house, with a new brew featured daily. Both cafés have a small boutique area where you can pick up the latest coffee paraphernalia. Free Wi-Fi.\n* + ![Café Pekoe](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 850 638'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n    Café Pekoe\n\n  9\n\n  ## Café Pékoe\n\n  Québec City’s most Italian coffee shop also specializes in bubble tea and serves up delicious banh mi, a type of Vietnamese sandwich. There are also cookies and other homemade treats to go along with your coffee, which is made with beans from Union, a Montréal roastery where [Café Pékoe](/en/businesses/cafe-pekoe)’s owner learned to sling single-shots with the best of them. Free Wi-Fi and the washrooms are wheelchair accessible.\n* + ![Café Saint-Henri](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 850 638'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n    Café Saint-Henri\n\n  10\n\n  ## Saint-Henri micro-torréfacteur\n\n  If you want to catch the vibe in one of Québec City’s hippest neighbourhoods, head to the end of Rue Saint-Joseph. This bright and airy [café](/en/businesses/saint-henri-micro-torrefacteur) features huge windows great for reading and studying, which may be why it’s so popular with students. Or maybe it’s the amazing doughnuts made on site by Sumo Beignerie Artisanale. Yum! The coffee served here is expertly roasted at the company’s small-batch roastery in Montréal. Free Wi-Fi.\n* + ![Philtre café](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 850 638'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n    Philtre café\n\n  11\n\n  ## Philtre Café\n\n  Tucked away in [Saint-Sauveur](/en/neighbourhoods-quebec-city/saint-sauveur), [Philtre Café](https://www.facebook.com/lephiltrecafe/) is everything you want in a coffee shop. Winner of Th3rdwave’s award for Best New Coffee Shop in Québec City, it’s a cute, cozy café with genuinely friendly baristas. It’s also one of the best cafés for studying and offers a tasty selection of breakfast items. Not sure what to order? Try the quinoa breakfast cookie. It’s vegan, gluten-free, and baked with love. Philtre serves Zab coffee, which is roasted in Montréal. Free Wi-Fi.\n* + ![Ma Station Café](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 850 638'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n    Ma Station Café\n\n  12\n\n  ## Ma Station Café\n\n  [Ma Station Café](https://www.facebook.com/mastationcafe) is more than just a café, it’s a meeting place in the Saint-Sauveur neighbourhood. Whether you’re looking to relax, chat with friends or enjoy a quiet moment, you’ll find it all in one place. You’ll find coffees from Montréal’s roaster Barista, featuring dark Italian roasts.\n\n#### You'll Also Like\n\n* ![A woman walks through the Isle de Bacchus vineyard on Île d'Orléans.](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 370 370'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n  ##### Agritourism and Artisan Producers\n\n   [Read](/en/restaurants-quebec-city/agritourism)\n* ![Nordic cuisine at restaurant Chez Boulay bistro Boréal](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 370 370'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n  ##### Nordic Cuisine in Québec City\n\n   [Read](/en/restaurants-quebec-city/nordic-cuisine)\n* ![L'Orygine - Bar extérieur](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 370 370'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n  ##### Sustainable Restaurants in Québec City\n\n   [Read](/en/restaurants-quebec-city/sustainable-restaurants)\n* ![Le Monastère des Augustines - Libre-service au Vivoir](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 370 370'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n  ##### Vegetarian Restaurants in Québec City\n\n   [Read](/en/restaurants-quebec-city/vegetarian)\n* ![Plat végétarien du Restaurant Don Végane](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 370 370'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n  ##### Vegan Restaurants in Québec City\n\n   [Read](/en/restaurants-quebec-city/vegan-restaurants)\n* ![Femme qui monte l'escalier Casse-cou](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 370 370'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n  ##### 2 Days in Québec City: A Self-Guided Walking Tour\n\n   [Read](/en/what-to-do-quebec-city/2-days-itinerary)\n* ![Auberge Triangle d'été](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 370 370'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n  ##### 7 Ways to Embrace Self‑Care and Wellness Tourism in Québec City\n\n   [Read](/en/what-to-do-quebec-city/wellness-tourism)\n* ![Rue du Trésor - Plusieurs touristes Rue du Trésor](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 370 370'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n  ##### Our Top Picks for a Made-In-Québec Shopping Experience\n\n   [Read](/en/what-to-do-quebec-city/products-made-in-quebec)\n\n1. [Home](https://www.quebec-cite.com/en)\n2. [Restaurants](https://www.quebec-cite.com/fr/restaurants-quebec)\n3. [The Best Cafés in Québec City](https://www.quebec-cite.com/en/restaurants-quebec-city/best-cafes)",
